\displaystyle\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M)\leq\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M^{\prime})\leq\bm{S}_{i},\qquad\forall i\in[d],\ 0<M<M^{\prime}<\infty.

(4.3)

As described in Section
3.2
,
our proof of Theorem
3.2
hinges on a recursive application of the equality (
1.3
)
that decomposes
\bm{S}_{i}
into a nested collection of the pruned
\bm{S}_{j}^{\leqslant}(M)
’s.
Now, we describe this recursive procedure in full detail.
Consider a probability space supporting (for each
j\in[d]
)

\displaystyle\bm{B}^{(k,m,t,q)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}\bm{B}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j},\qquad\forall k,m,t,q\geq 1,

(4.4)

with

\bm{B}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}=(B_{1\leftarrow j},B_{2\leftarrow j},\ldots,B_{d\leftarrow j})^{\top}

being the offspring distribution
in (
1.1
).
Let

\displaystyle\bm{S}^{(k,m)}_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{t\geq 0}\bm{X}_{j}^{(k,m)}(t),\quad\text{where }{\bm{X}_{j}^{(k,m)}(t)}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{q=1}^{X_{j,i}^{(k,m)}(t-1)}{\bm{B}}^{(k,m,t,q)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i},\ \ \forall t\geq 1,

(4.5)

under initial value
\bm{X}_{j}^{(k,m)}(0)=\bm{e}_{j}
.
Besides, we adopt the notation in (
3.18
) and use
\bm{B}^{\leqslant,(k,m,t,q)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}(M)

to denote the truncated version of
\bm{B}^{(k,m,t,q)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}
under threshold
M
.
Let

\displaystyle{\bm{X}^{\leqslant,(k,m)}_{j}(t;M)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{q=1}^{X^{\leqslant,(k,m)}_{j,i}(t-1;M)}{\bm{B}}^{\leqslant,(k,m,t,q)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(M),\quad\forall t\geq 1,

(4.6)

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{j}^{\leqslant,(k,m)}(M)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{t\geq 0}\bm{X}_{j}^{\leqslant,(k,m)}(t;M),

with initial value
{\bm{X}^{\leqslant,(k,m)}_{j}(0;M)}=\bm{e}_{j}
.
Analogous to (
3.22
), (
3.24
),
we define

\displaystyle W^{>,(k,m)}_{j;i}(M)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{t\geq 1}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{q=1}^{X^{\leqslant,(k,m)}_{j,l}(t-1;M)}B^{(k,m,t,q)}_{i\leftarrow l}\mathbbm{I}\big\{B^{(k,m,t,q)}_{i\leftarrow l}>M\big\},

(4.7)

\displaystyle N^{>,(k,m)}_{j;i}(M)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{t\geq 1}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{q=1}^{X^{\leqslant,(k,m)}_{j,l}(t-1;M)}\mathbbm{I}\big\{B^{(k,m,t,q)}_{i\leftarrow l}>M\big\}.

Given
M>0
and
i\in[d]
,
note that
the collection of vectors

\displaystyle\Big(\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(k,m)}_{j}(M),W^{>,(k,m)}_{j;1}(M),\ldots,W^{>,(k,m)}_{j;d}(M),N^{>,(k,m)}_{j;1}(M),\ldots,N^{>,(k,m)}_{j;d}(M)\Big)_{k,m\geq 1}

(4.8)

are i.i.d. copies of

\big(\bm{S}_{j}^{\leqslant}(M),\ W^{>}_{j;1}(M),\ldots,W^{>}_{j;d}(M),\ N^{>}_{j;1}(M),\ldots,N^{>}_{j;d}(M)\big).

Now, given
j\in[d]
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\delta>0
, we consider the following procedure,
where
k
denotes the iteration in the recursive application of (
1.3
), and

M^{n|\delta}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}(k)

denotes the truncation threshold employed in the
k^{\text{th}}
iteration
for sub-trees induced by type-
j
nodes.

1.

Set

\displaystyle\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{j}(0)=\bm{e}_{j}.

(4.9)

In addition, set

\displaystyle M^{n|\delta}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(1)=n\delta,\qquad\forall i\in[d].

(4.10)

2.

Starting from
k\geq 1
, do the following inductively.
If there is some
i\in[d]
such that

\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k-1)>0,

let

\displaystyle\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;l}(k)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k-1)}W^{>,(k,m)}_{i;l}\Big(M^{n|\delta}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(k)\Big),\qquad\forall l\in[d],

(4.11)

\displaystyle\bm{S}^{n|\delta}_{j;\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(k)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{m=1}^{\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k-1)}\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(k,m)}_{i}\Big(M^{n|\delta}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(k)\Big),\qquad\forall i\in[d],

(4.12)

\displaystyle\bm{S}^{n|\delta}_{j}(k)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{i\in[d]}\bm{S}^{n|\delta}_{j;\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(k)=\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k-1)}\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(k,m)}_{i}\Big(M^{n|\delta}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(k)\Big),

(4.13)

and set

\displaystyle M_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}^{n|\delta}(k+1)=\delta\cdot\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k),\qquad\forall i\in[d].

(4.14)

Otherwise, move onto step (iii).

3.

Now,
let

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{n|\delta}_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\max\Big\{k\geq 0:\ \tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)>0\text{ for some }i\in[d]\Big\}.

(4.15)

By step (ii) and the definition of
\mathcal{K}^{n|\delta}_{j}
,
we have
\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)=0\ \forall i\in[d]
under
k=\mathcal{K}^{n|\delta}_{j}+1
.
For all
k>\mathcal{K}^{n|\delta}_{j}+1
, we also set

\displaystyle\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)=0,\qquad\forall i\in[d].

(4.16)

By (
1.3
),

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{n|\delta}_{j}+1}\bm{S}_{j}^{n|\delta}(k)=\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{n|\delta}_{j}+1}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k-1)}\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(k,m)}_{i}\Big(M^{n|\delta}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(k)\Big).

(4.17)

In particular,
step (ii) is a recursive application of (
1.3
).
For each
k\geq 1
,
we use
\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k-1)

to count the number of copies of
\bm{S}_{i}
that remain to be generated after the
(k-1)^{\text{th}}
iteration of step (ii).
At the
k^{\text{th}}
iteration,
the independent copies of
\bm{S}_{i}
are generated via (
1.3
)
under the truncation threshold

M^{n|\delta}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(k),

which is determined by the rule (
4.14
)
using the values of
\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k-1)
in the previous iteration.
We add a few remarks:

•

Under the sub-criticality condition in Assumption
1
,
step (ii) will almost surely terminate after finitely many steps
(meaning that
\mathcal{K}^{n|\delta}_{j}<\infty
almost surely).
This is because
\left\lVert\bm{S}_{j}\right\rVert<\infty
almost surely, and each copy
\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(k,m)}_{i}(M)
will add a least one node—the ancestor along the
i^{\text{th}}
dimension that induces this sub-tree.

•

To prove Theorem
3.2
,
it suffices to consider a finite-iteration version of step (ii).
Indeed, Lemma
4.8
confirms that, given
\gamma>0
,
it holds for all
K
large enough that
the probability of step (ii) running beyond
K
iterations is of order
\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})
.
Therefore,
by picking a constant
K
large enough to ensure an
\mathit{o}(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n))
bound for such pathological cases,
we can prove (
3.13
) (given
A
and
\bm{j}
) or (
3.14
)
by only
applying equality (
1.3
) for
K
times (instead of stopping randomly) in step (ii).
Switching to this alternative approach has no real consequences for our subsequent analysis, and we will not explore it in detail.

•

Lastly, by (
4.1
) and the choices of
M^{n|\delta}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(k)
above, we have

\displaystyle\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)>0\quad\iff\quad\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)>n\delta^{k},\qquad\forall k\geq 1,\ i\in[d].

(4.18)

To proceed with our proof of Theorem
3.2
,
let

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}n^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{n|\delta}_{j}+1}\bm{S}_{j}^{n|\delta}(k)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}n^{-1}\bm{S}_{j},

(4.19)

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{k\geq 1}\sum_{i\in[d]}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)\cdot\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}.

(4.20)

The last equality in (
4.19
) follows from
(
4.17
).
Next, recall the definition of the mapping
\Phi
in (
2.7
),
and define

\displaystyle(\bar{R}^{n}_{j},\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Phi(\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{j}),\qquad(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{j},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Phi(\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{j}),

(4.21)

which can be interpreted as the polar coordinates of
\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{j}
and
\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{j}
.
Note that
\bar{R}^{n}_{j}=\left\lVert\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{j}\right\rVert
and
\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{j}=\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{j}\right\rVert
.
Meanwhile,
based on the definitions of
\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)
in (
4.9
) and (
4.11
),
we define

\displaystyle I^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\mathbbm{I}\big\{\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)>0\big\},\qquad\forall i\in[d],\ k\geq 1.

(4.22)

By the definition of
\mathcal{K}^{n|\delta}_{j}
in (
4.15
), we have

•

I^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)=0\ \ \forall k\geq\mathcal{K}^{n|\delta}_{j}+1,\ i\in[d]
,

•

For any
k=1,\ldots,\mathcal{K}^{n|\delta}_{j}
, there exists some
i\in[d]
such that
I^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)=1
.

We say that

\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{j}=\big(I^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)\big)_{k\geq 1,\ i\in[d]}
is the
(n,\delta)
-type of

\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{j}
.
Note that
\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{j}
can take values outside of
\mathscr{I}
, the collection of all types in Definition
3.1
.
We thus consider the following generalization,
where
\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{j}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
a.s. due to
\mathcal{K}^{n|\delta}_{j}<\infty
a.s.

Definition 4.1

(Generalized Type)
.

\bm{I}=(I_{k,i})_{k\geq 1,\ j\in[d]}
is a
generalized type
if it satisfies the following conditions:

•

I_{k,j}\in\{0,1\}
for all
k\geq 1
and
j\in[d]
;

•

There exists
{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
\sum_{i\in[d]}I_{k,j}=0\ \forall k>\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}
and
\sum_{j\in[d]}I_{k,j}\geq 1\ \forall 1\leq k\leq\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}
.

We use
\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
to denote the set containing all generalized types.
For each
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
, we say that

\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.19441pt\raisebox{5.64096pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-3.19441pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\big\{j\in[d]:\ \sum_{k\geq 1}I_{k,j}=1\big\}

is the set of
active indices
of the generalized type
\bm{I}
,
and
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}
is the
depth
of
\bm{I}
.
For each
k\geq 1
, we say that

{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.19441pt\raisebox{5.64096pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-3.19441pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\big\{j\in[d]:\ I_{k,j}=1\big\}

is the set of
active indices at depth
k
in
\bm{I}
.

We prove Theorem
3.2

by establishing the asymptotic equivalence between

(\bar{R}^{n}_{j},\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{j})

and

(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{j},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{j})

in terms of
\mathbb{M}
-convergence (see Definition
2.1
),
and we view
{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{j}}=\big(I^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)\big)_{k\geq 1,\ i\in[d]}
as a mark of
(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{j},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{j})
that encapsulates the spatio-temporal information of the big jumps in the underlying branching process.
To this end, we prepare
Lemma
4.2
.
This result can be seen as a version of Lemma 2.4 in
[
78
]
tailored for the space of polar coordinates
\mathbb{S}=[0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}
under the metric
\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}
defined in (
2.6
),
and there are only two key differences.
First,
Condition (
4.23
) explicitly requires that, under polar transform, the pre-image of
\mathbb{C}
is a cone in
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
.
Second, we augment the approximations
Y^{\delta}_{n}
with random marks
V^{\delta}_{n}
; in this regard, Lemma 2.4 in
[
78
]
can be seen as a simplified version of our Lemma
4.2
featuring “dummy” marks (e.g.,
V^{\delta}_{n}\equiv 1
).
The proof is similar to that of Lemma 2.4 in
[
78
]
and is collected in Section
C
of the Appendix for the sake of completeness.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
(R_{n},\Theta_{n})
and
(\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})
be random elements taking values in
\mathbb{S}=[0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}
with metric
\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}
in (
2.6
).
Let
V^{\delta}_{n}
be random elements taking values in a countable set
\mathbb{V}
.
Let
\mathbb{C}\subseteq\mathbb{S}
be such that
(0,\bm{w})\in\mathbb{C}
for any
\bm{w}\in\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}
, and

\displaystyle(r,\bm{w})\in\mathbb{C},\ r>0,\ \bm{w}\in\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad(t,\bm{w})\in\mathbb{C}\ \ \forall t\geq 0.

(4.23)

Let

\mathcal{V}\subset\mathbb{V}
be a set containing only finitely many elements (i.e.,
|\mathcal{V}|<\infty
),
and let
\mu_{v}\in\mathbb{M}(\mathbb{S}\setminus\mathbb{C})
for each
v\in\mathcal{V}
.
Let
\epsilon_{n}
be a sequence of strictly positive real numbers with
\lim_{n\to\infty}\epsilon_{n}=0
.
Suppose that

1.

(
Asymptotic equivalence
)
Given
\Delta\in(0,1)
,
it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\big\{R_{n}\vee\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}>\Delta\big\}\cap\Big\{\frac{\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}}{R_{n}}\notin[1-\Delta,1+\Delta]\text{ or }\left\lVert\Theta_{n}-\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}\right\rVert>\Delta\Big\}\bigg)=0;

2.

(
Convergence given the marks
V^{\delta}_{n}
)
Let the Borel set
B\subseteq\mathbb{S}
be bounded away from
\mathbb{C}
under
\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}
,
and let

\Delta\in\big(0,\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}(B,\mathbb{C})\big)
;
under any
\delta>0
small enough,
the claim

\displaystyle\mu_{v}(B_{\Delta})-\Delta

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\big((\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in B,\ V^{\delta}_{n}=v\big)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\big((\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in B,\ V^{\delta}_{n}=v\big)\leq\mu_{v}(B^{\Delta})+\Delta

holds for each
v\in\mathcal{V}
, and we also have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\big((\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in B,\ V^{\delta}_{n}\notin\mathcal{V}\big)=0.

Then,

\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in\ \cdot\ \big)\to\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\mu_{v}(\cdot)

in
\mathbb{M}(\mathbb{S}\setminus\mathbb{C})
.

Recall the definitions of
\mathbb{R}^{d}(\bm{j})
and
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})=\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},0)
in
(
2.1
) and (
2.3
), respectively.
For any
\bm{j}\subseteq\{1,2,\ldots,d\}

that is non-empty, let

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}^{d}(\bm{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{(r,\bm{w})\in[0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}:\ r\bm{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}(\bm{j})\big\},

(4.24)

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{(r,\bm{w})\in[0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}:\ r\bm{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})\big\}.

Recall the definitions of

{(\bar{R}^{n}_{j},\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{j})}

and

{(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{j},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{j})}

in (
4.21
).
The next two key propositions allow us to apply Lemma
4.2
and establish Theorem
3.2
.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.
Given
i\in[d]
,
\gamma>0,\ \Delta\in(0,1)
, and
non-empty
\bm{j}\subseteq\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
,
it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\Big\{\bar{R}^{n}_{i}\vee\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}>\Delta\Big\}\cap\bigg\{\frac{\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}}{\bar{R}^{n}_{i}}\notin[1-\Delta,1+\Delta]\text{ or }\left\lVert\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{i}-\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert>\Delta\bigg\}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=0.

(4.25)

Proposition 4.4
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.
Let
i\in[d]
, and let
\bm{j}\subseteq\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
be non-empty.
Let

\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.19441pt\raisebox{5.64096pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-3.19441pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\big\{\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}:\ \bm{j}^{\bm{I}}=\bm{j}\big\},

where
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}
is the set of active indices of type
\bm{I}
, and
\mathscr{I}
is the collection of all types (see Definition
3.1
).
Given
\Delta>0
and a Borel set

B\subseteq[0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}
that is bounded away from

\mathbb{C}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})
under
\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}
,
the following claims hold.

1.

Under any
\delta>0
small enough, it holds for each
\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Big((\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i})\in B,\ \bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\circ\Phi^{-1}(B^{\Delta})+\Delta,

(4.26)

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Big((\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i})\in B,\ \bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\circ\Phi^{-1}(B_{\Delta})-\Delta,

where
the measure
\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\bm{I}}\circ\Phi^{-1}(\cdot)
is defined using
(
2.8
)
with the
\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}_{i}(\cdot)
’s in (
3.12
).

2.

Under any
\delta>0
small enough

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Big((\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i})\in B,\ \bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\notin\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})\Big)=0.

(4.27)

3.

\sum_{\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\bm{I}}\circ\Phi^{-1}(B)<\infty.

To conclude Section
4.1
,
we
provide the proof of Theorem
3.2
using Propositions
4.3
and
4.4
.
The remainder of Section
4
is devoted to establishing Propositions
4.3
and
4.4
.

Proof of Theorem
3.2
.

We first prove Claim (
3.13
).
Under the choice of

(R_{n},\Theta_{n})=(\bar{R}^{n}_{i},\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{i})
,

(\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})=(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i}),

\mathbb{S}=[0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+},

\mathbb{C}=\mathbb{C}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j}),

\epsilon_{n}=\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n),

V^{\delta}_{n}=\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i},

and

\mathcal{V}=\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})=\big\{\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}:\ \bm{j}^{\bm{I}}=\bm{j}\big\},

Propositions
4.3
and
4.4

verify the conditions in
Lemma
4.2
.
In particular,
part (iii) of Proposition
4.4

confirms that
\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\circ\Phi^{-1}\in\mathbb{M}\big(\mathbb{S}\setminus\mathbb{C}_{\leqslant}^{d}(\bm{j})\big)

for each
\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
.
Next, Proposition
4.3
verifies condition (i) of Lemma
4.2
,
and
parts (i) and (ii) of Proposition
4.4
verify condition (ii) of Lemma
4.2
.
This allows us to apply Lemma
4.2
and obtain

\displaystyle\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\Big((\bar{R}^{n}_{i},\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{i})\in\ \cdot\ \Big)\to\sum_{\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\bm{I}}\circ\Phi^{-1}(\cdot)\quad\text{ in }\mathbb{M}\big(\mathbb{S}\setminus\mathbb{C}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})\big).

Lastly, applying Lemma
2.4

under the choice of
X_{n}=\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}
,
(R_{n},\Theta_{n})=(\bar{R}^{n}_{i},\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{i})
,
and
\mu=\sum_{\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\bm{I}}
,
we conclude the proof of Claim (
3.13
).

The proof of Claim (
3.14
) is almost identical,
and
the plan is to apply Lemma
4.2
under the choices of

(R_{n},\Theta_{n})=(\bar{R}^{n}_{i},\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{i})
,

(\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})=(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i}),

\mathbb{S}=[0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+},

\mathbb{C}=\mathbb{C}^{d}([d]),

\epsilon_{n}=n^{-\gamma},

and with dummy marks (i.e.,
V^{\delta}_{n}\equiv 0
,
\mathcal{V}=\mathbb{V}=\{0\}
).
Again, Proposition
4.3
verifies condition (i) of Lemma
4.2
.
Meanwhile, note that

\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}([d])
and
(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i})\in\mathbb{C}^{d}([d])

by definitions in (
4.20
) and (
4.21
).
Then, for any
B\in\mathbb{S}
that is bounded away from
\mathbb{C}^{d}([d])
, it holds trivially that

\mathbf{P}\big((\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i})\in B\big)=0,

thus verifying condition (ii) of Lemma
4.2
with
\mu_{v}\equiv 0
.
By Lemma
4.2
,
we get

n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\big((\bar{R}^{n}_{i},\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{i})\in\ \cdot\ \big)\rightarrow 0

in

\mathbb{M}\big(\mathbb{S}\setminus\mathbb{C}^{d}([d])\big)

for any
\gamma>0
.
Applying Lemma
2.4
again,
we conclude the proof.
∎

4.2
Proof of Proposition
4.3

We first state two lemmas to characterize tail asymptotics and provide concentration inequalities for
\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M)
defined in (
1.3
).

Lemma 4.5
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.
Given any
\Delta,\ \gamma\in(0,\infty)
,
there exists
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\Delta,\gamma)>0
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Delta\Big)=0,\qquad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}),\ i\in[d].

(4.28)

Lemma 4.6
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.
Given
\epsilon,\ \gamma>0
, there exists
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\epsilon,\gamma)>0
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i}(n\delta)-\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}\right\rVert>\epsilon\Bigg)=0,\qquad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}),\ i\in[d],

where

\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}=\mathbf{E}\bm{S}_{i}
(see (
3.2
)),
and the

\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i}(M)
’s
are
independent copies of
\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M)
.

These results follow from concentration inequalities for truncated heavy-tailed random vectors.
Indeed, by definitions
in (
3.18
)–(
3.19
),

\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)
can be expressed as a randomly stopped sum of i.i.d. copies of heavy-tailed random vectors
\bm{B}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}
truncated under threshold
n\delta
.
Furthermore, one can establish useful bounds on the random count in the summation,
as the number of individuals born in each generation of the branching process
\big(\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(t;M)\big)_{t\geq 0}
is expected to contract geometrically fast
if

\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}\right\rVert\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sup_{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}\bm{x}\right\rVert<1.

Therefore, we are able to suitably apply Lemma 3.1 of
[
78
]
and prove Lemma
4.5
.
As an implication of Lemma
4.5
,

\bm{S}_{i}^{\leqslant}(n\delta)

is almost always bounded by
\bm{S}_{i}\mathbbm{I}\{\left\lVert\bm{S}_{i}\right\rVert\leq n\Delta\}
(under smaller enough
\delta
), thus
allowing us to apply Lemma 3.1 of
[
78
]
again and verify Lemma
4.6
.
We collect their proofs in Section
D
of the Appendix.
Here, we note that the proof of Lemma
4.5
becomes more involved if

\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}\right\rVert\geq 1:

in that case, inspired by the Gelfand’s formula based approach in
[
50
]
,
we identify some
r
with
\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}^{r}\right\rVert<1
,
and apply the same arguments to the sub-trees constructed by sampling the original branching tree every
r
generations.

Next, we discuss properties of
{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{j}}=\big(I^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k)\big)_{k\geq 1,\ i\in[d]}
defined in (
4.22
),
as well as the notion of generalized types in Definition
4.1
.
Analogous to
\alpha(\cdot)
defined in (
3.4
),
we let

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}1+\sum_{k\geq 1}\sum_{j\in[d]}I_{k,j}\cdot(\alpha^{*}(j)-1)

(4.29)

for any generalized type
\bm{I}=(I_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}

with
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}\neq 0

(i.e., there are some
k\geq 1
and
j\in[d]
such that
I_{k,j}\neq 0
).
If
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}=0
(i.e.,
I_{k,j}\equiv 0
),
we set
\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})=0
.
We stress again that Definition
4.1
generalizes Definition
3.1
as
\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}\supseteq\mathscr{I}
.
In particular, given a generalized type
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
,
for any
j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}
there could be multiple
k\geq 1
such that
I_{k,j}=1
.
However, this would not be the case for a type
\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}
.
Besides, recall that we work with Assumption
2
in this paper,
which ensures that
\alpha^{*}(j)>1\ \forall j\in[d]
in (
3.3
).
Therefore, for
\alpha(\cdot)
defined in (
3.4
),

\displaystyle\alpha(\bm{j}^{\bm{I}})

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I}),\qquad\forall\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}\text{ with }\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}\geq 1,

(4.30)

\displaystyle\alpha(\bm{j}^{\bm{I}})

\displaystyle=\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I}),\qquad\forall\bm{I}\in{\mathscr{I}}\text{ with }\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}\geq 1.

(4.31)

Similarly,
if there exists
j\in[d]
such that
\#\{k\geq 1:\ I_{k,j}=1\}\geq 2
,
then, by definitions in (
3.4
) and (
4.29
),
we must have
\alpha(\bm{j}^{\bm{I}})<\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})
.
As a result, for any generalized type
\bm{I}=(I_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
,

\displaystyle\alpha(\bm{j}^{\bm{I}})=\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\#\{k\geq 1:\ I_{k,j}=1\}\leq 1\ \forall j\in[d].

(4.32)

Besides, note that
the events
\{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\}

are mutually exclusive across different generalized types
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
.
Then, due to
\mathcal{K}^{n|\delta}_{j}<\infty
almost surely,
it holds for any
n\geq 1
,
\delta>0
,
i\in[d]
that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i},\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\in\ \cdot\ \Big)=\sum_{\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}}\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i},\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\in\ \cdot\ ,\ \bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\Big),

(4.33)

where
\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}n^{-1}\bm{S}_{i}
(see (
4.19
))
and
\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}
is defined in (
4.20
).

We also highlight the Markov property embedded in
\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}
.
Recall the probability space considered in Section
4.1

that supports the

\bm{B}^{(k,m,t,q)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}
’s in (
4.4
),
and define the
\sigma
-algebra

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{k}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sigma\Big\{\bm{B}^{(k^{\prime},m,t,q)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}:\ m,t,q\geq 1,\ j\in[d],\ k^{\prime}\in[k]\Big\},\qquad\forall k\geq 1.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{0}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\{\emptyset,\Omega\}
.
By (
4.5
) and (
4.6
),

(\bm{S}^{(k,m)}_{i})_{m\geq 1,i\in[d]}

and
the random vectors in (
4.8
)
are measurable w.r.t.
\mathcal{F}_{k}
.
Then, in the procedure (
4.9
)–(
4.17
),

\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k),\ \bm{S}^{n|\delta}_{j;\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(k),\text{ and }\bm{S}^{n|\delta}_{j}(k)

are measurable w.r.t.
\mathcal{F}_{k}
.
Besides,

M_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}^{n|\delta}(k)
is determined by
\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k-1)
(see (
4.14
)),
and hence measurable w.r.t.
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
.
Furthermore,
conditioned on the value of
\big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k-1)\big)_{i\in[d]}
,
the random vector

\big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k),\ \bm{S}^{n|\delta}_{j;\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(k)\big)_{i\in[d]}

is independent from
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
. (i.e., the history).
As a result, for each
k\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\Big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k),\ \bm{S}^{n|\delta}_{j;\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(k)\Big)_{i\in[d]}\in\ \bm{\cdot}\ \Bigg|\ \mathcal{F}_{k-1}\Bigg)

(4.34)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\Big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k),\ \bm{S}^{n|\delta}_{j;\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(k)\Big)_{i\in[d]}\in\ \bm{\cdot}\ \Bigg|\ \Big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;l}(k-1)\Big)_{l\in[d]}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\Bigg(\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;l}(k-1)}W^{>,(k,m)}_{l;i}\Big(\delta\cdot\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;l}(k-1)\Big),

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\quad\sum_{m=1}^{\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k-1)}\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(k,m)}_{i}\Big(\delta\cdot\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;i}(k-1)\Big)\Bigg)_{i\in[d]}\in\ \bm{\cdot}\ \Bigg|\ \Big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{j;l}(k-1)\Big)_{l\in[d]}\Bigg).

An immediate consequence of (
4.34
) is that,
given any generalized type
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I})

(4.35)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big(I^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(1)=1\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}\Big)\cdot\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{I}+1}\mathbf{P}\Big(I^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)=1\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\ \Big|\ I^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k-1)=1\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(1)>0\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}\Big)\cdot\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{I}+1}\mathbf{P}\Big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)>0\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\ \Big|\ \tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k-1)>0\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\Big),

where
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}
is the set of active indices at depth
k
of
\bm{I}
(see Definition
4.1
),
and the display above follows from (
4.34
)
as well as the definition of
I^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)
in (
4.22
).

In light of (
4.35
),
as well as the definitions of the
\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)
’s in (
4.11
),
the asymptotic analysis of events
\{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\}

boils down to characterizing the asymptotic law of (the sums of)
W^{>}_{i;j}(M)
and
N^{>}_{i;j}(M)

in (
3.21
)–(
3.24
).
This is the content of Lemma
4.7
,
which will be a key tool in our analysis.
In particular, independently for each
i\in[d]
, let

\displaystyle\bigg\{\Big(W^{>,(m)}_{i;1}(M),\ldots,W^{>,(m)}_{i;d}(M),N^{>,(m)}_{i;1}(M),\ldots,N^{>,(m)}_{i;d}(M)\Big):\ m\geq 1\bigg\}

(4.36)

be independent copies of

\big(W^{>}_{i;1}(M),\ldots,W^{>}_{i;d}(M),N^{>}_{i;1}(M),\ldots,N^{>}_{i;d}(M)\big).

Given any non-empty
\mathcal{I}\subseteq[d]
and any
\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})=(t_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}
with
t_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
for each
i\in\mathcal{I}
,
we write

\displaystyle N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i}),\qquad W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}W^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i}).

(4.37)

By (
4.1
), note that

\displaystyle{N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}}>0\qquad\iff\qquad{N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}}\geq 1\qquad\iff\qquad{W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}}>0.

(4.38)

Lemma 4.7
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.

1.

Under any
\delta>0
small enough, we have (as
n\to\infty
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)=1\Big)

\displaystyle\sim\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta),

(4.39)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)\geq 2\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathit{o}\big(\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\big),

(4.40)

where

l^{*}(\cdot)
and
\alpha^{*}(\cdot)
are defined in (
3.3
).
Besides, given
0<c<C<\infty
and
i\in[d],\ j\in[d]
,
it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}(n\delta)=1;\ N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)=0\ \forall l\neq l^{*}(j)\ \Big|\ N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)\geq 1\Big)

\displaystyle=1,

(4.41)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in[c,C]}\Bigg|\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(W^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)>nx\ \big|\ N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)}{({\delta}/{x})^{\alpha^{*}(j)}}-1\Bigg|

\displaystyle=0,

(4.42)

where
N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M)
,
N^{>}_{i;j}(M)
are defined in (
3.23
)–(
3.24
).

2.

There exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that the following holds for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
:
for each
\mathcal{J}\subseteq[d]
with
|\mathcal{J}|\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)\geq 1\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}\Big)=\mathit{o}\bigg(n^{|\mathcal{J}|-1}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\bigg),\qquad\text{ as }n\to\infty.

3.

Let

\mathcal{I}\subseteq\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
and

\mathcal{J}\subseteq\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
be non-empty.
There exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t_{i}\geq nc\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big({N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\big)}{\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}n\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta\big)}<\infty,\qquad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}),\ c>0,

(4.43)

where
{N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}}
is defined in (
4.37
).

We defer the proof of Lemma
4.7
to Section
4.4
.
In this section, we focus on applying Lemma
4.7
and establishing
Proposition
4.3
.
First, given
\bm{I},\bm{I}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
,
we define the following (partial) ordering:

\displaystyle\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{\prime}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad I_{k,j}=I^{\prime}_{k,j},\qquad\forall j\in[d],\ k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}].

(4.44)

That is,
\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{\prime}
if they match with each other up to the depth of
\bm{I}
.
Lemma
4.8
provides bounds for events of the form
\{\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\}
.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.
Given
\bm{I}=(I_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}

with
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}\geq 1
,
it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(n^{-1}\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta\big)\Bigg),\ \text{ as }n\to\infty.

(4.45)

Furthermore, if
|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}|\geq 2
, it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)=\mathit{o}\Bigg(n^{-1}\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta\big)\Bigg),\ \text{ as }n\to\infty.

(4.46)

Proof.

By the definition in (
4.22
),

\{\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\}=\bigcap_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\big\{I^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)=1\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\big\}=\bigcap_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\big\{\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)>0\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\big\}.

Then, analogous to (
4.35
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)

(4.47)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(1)>0\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}\big)\cdot\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\mathbf{P}\Big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)>0\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\ \Big|\ \tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k-1)>0\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(1)>n\delta\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\mathbf{P}\Big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)>0\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\ \Big|\ \tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k-1)>n\delta^{k-1}\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\Big)\quad\text{by \eqref{property: lower bound for tau n delta i j k, cluster size}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(1)>n\delta\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\mathbf{P}\Big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)>0\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\ \Big|\ \tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k-1)>n\delta^{k-1}\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\Big(W^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)>0\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\ \sup_{t_{l}\geq n\delta^{k-1}\ \forall l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sum_{l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}\sum_{m=1}^{t_{l}}W^{>,(m)}_{l;j}(\delta\cdot t_{l})>0\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\Bigg)\quad\text{by \eqref{def: layer zero, from pruned cluster to full cluster} and \eqref{def: from pruned cluster to full cluster, tau and S at step k + 1}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big(W^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)>0\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}\Big)\cdot\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\ \sup_{t_{l}\geq n\delta^{k-1}\ \forall l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}\mathbf{P}\Big(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1});j}>0\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\Big)

using notations in (
4.37
), where we write
\bm{t}(\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1})=(t_{l})_{l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)\geq 1\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}\Big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}p_{1}(n,\delta)}\cdot\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\ \underbrace{\sup_{t_{l}\geq n\delta^{k-1}\ \forall l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1});j}\geq 1\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\Big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}p_{k}(n,\delta)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{due to \eqref{property, W and N i M l j when positive, cluster size} and \eqref{property, sum of W and N i M l j when positive, cluster size}}.

We first analyze
p_{1}(n,\delta)
.
If
|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}|=1
(i.e., the set
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}
contains only one element),
we write
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}=\{j_{1}\}
.
Using (
4.39
) and (
4.40
) in part (i), Lemma
4.7
,
for any
\delta>0
small enough,

\displaystyle p_{1}(n,\delta)=\mathcal{O}\Big(\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j_{1}\leftarrow l^{*}(j_{1})}>n\delta\big)\Big)\text{ as }n\to\infty,\qquad\text{if }\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}=\{j_{1}\}.

(4.48)

When
|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}|\geq 2
,
by part (ii) of Lemma
4.7
,
it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle p_{1}(n,\delta)=\mathit{o}\Bigg(n^{-1}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}}n\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta\big)\Bigg)\text{ as }n\to\infty,\qquad\text{if }|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}|\geq 2.

(4.49)

On the other hand, by part (iii) of Lemma
4.7
under the choice of
c=\delta^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}
, it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle p_{k}(n,\delta)=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta\big)\Bigg)\ \text{ as }n\to\infty,\qquad\forall k=2,3,\ldots,\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}.

(4.50)

Combining (
4.48
) (resp. (
4.49
)) with (
4.50
),
we establish (
4.45
) (resp. (
4.46
)).
∎

Next, recall the definitions of
(\bar{R}^{n}_{j},\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{j})
and
(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{j},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{j})
in (
4.21
).
We prepare
Lemma
4.9
to
bound the probability that

(\bar{R}^{n}_{j},\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{j})
and
(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{j},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{j})
are not close to each other.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.
Given
i\in[d]
,
\gamma,\epsilon>0
,
\Delta\in(0,\epsilon]
, and
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Big(B^{n}_{i}(\delta,\bm{I},\epsilon,\Delta)\Big)=0,\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough},

with

\displaystyle B_{i}^{n}(\delta,\bm{I},\epsilon,\Delta)

(4.51)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.19441pt\raisebox{5.64096pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-3.19441pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\big\{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I},\ \bar{R}^{n}_{i}\vee\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}>\epsilon\big\}\cap\Bigg\{\frac{\bar{R}^{n}_{i}}{\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}}\notin[1-\Delta,1+\Delta]\text{ or }\left\lVert\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{i}-\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert>\Delta\Bigg\}.

Proof.

By Definition
4.1
,
the only generalized type
\bm{I}=(I_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,\ j\in[d]}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}

with depth
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}=0
is
I_{k,j}\equiv 0\ \forall k,j
.
We first discuss the case where
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}\geq 1
.
At the end of this proof, we address the case where
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}=0
.

We start by fixing some constants.
First,
recall the definition of
\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}=\mathbf{E}\bm{S}_{j}
in (
3.2
).
Since the branching process for
\bm{S}_{j}
contains at least the ancestor along the
j^{\text{th}}
dimension,
we have
\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert\geq 1
for each
j\in[d]
.
Therefore, for

\displaystyle\rho=\min_{j\in[d]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert\Big/\max_{j\in[d]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert,

(4.52)

we have
\rho\in(0,1)
.
Next, given
\gamma,\epsilon,\Delta>0
,
we fix
\tilde{\Delta}>0
small enough such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}<\frac{\epsilon}{2},\qquad\frac{\tilde{\Delta}}{\epsilon\cdot\rho/4}<\frac{\Delta}{4},\qquad\frac{\tilde{\Delta}}{\min_{j\in[d]}\left\lVert\bm{s}_{j}\right\rVert}<\frac{\Delta}{4},\qquad\tilde{\Delta}<\frac{1}{2}\min_{j\in[d]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert.

(4.53)

To proceed, recall that in Definition
4.1
, we use
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}
to denote the depth of the generalized type
\bm{I}
,
and

\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}
for the set of active indices at depth
k
.
By (
4.19
)–(
4.20
),
on the event
\{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\}
, it holds that
(using notations in (
4.11
)–(
4.14
))

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\cdot\bar{\bm{s}}_{j},

(4.54)

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\Bigg[\bm{S}_{i}^{\leqslant,(1,1)}(n\delta)+\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\sum_{m=1}^{\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)}\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(k+1,m)}_{j}\Big(\delta\cdot\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\Big)\Bigg].

Next, define the events

\displaystyle A^{n}_{0}(\delta,\bm{I})

\displaystyle=\Big\{\Big|\Big|\underbrace{n^{-1}\bm{S}_{i}^{\leqslant,(1,1)}(n\delta)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\bm{\Delta}_{0,i}}\Big|\Big|\leq\tilde{\Delta}\Big\},

(4.55)

and (for each
k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}
),

\displaystyle A^{n}_{k,j}(\delta,\bm{I})

\displaystyle=\left\{\rule{0.0pt}{25.6073pt}\left\lVert\underbrace{\big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\big)^{-1}\sum_{m=1}^{\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)}\bigg(\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(k+1,m)}_{j}\Big(\delta\cdot\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\Big)-\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\bigg)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\bm{\Delta}_{k,j}}\right\rVert\leq\tilde{\Delta}\right\}.

(4.56)

Also, define the event

\displaystyle A^{n}_{*}(\delta,\bm{I})=\big\{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I},\ \bar{R}^{n}_{i}\vee\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}>\epsilon\big\}\cap A_{0}^{n}(\delta,\bm{I})\cap\Bigg(\bigcap_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\bigcap_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}A_{k,j}^{n}(\delta,\bm{I})\Bigg).

(4.57)

Suppose we can show that

\displaystyle\text{
on the event $A^{n}_{*}(\delta,\bm{I})$, it holds that
}\quad\frac{\bar{R}^{n}_{i}}{\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}}\in[1-\Delta,1+\Delta]\text{ and }\left\lVert\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{i}-\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert\leq\Delta.

(4.58)

Then, by the definition in (
4.51
),
we have

B_{i}^{n}(\delta,\bm{I},\epsilon,\Delta)\cap A^{n}_{*}(\delta,\bm{I})=\emptyset,

and hence

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(B^{n}_{i}(\delta,\bm{I},\epsilon,\Delta)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(A_{*}^{n}(\delta,\bm{I})\big)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(A^{n}_{0}(\delta,\bm{I})\big)^{c}\Big)+\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(A^{n}_{k,j}(\delta,\bm{I})\big)^{c}\Big).

By Lemma
4.5
,
it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(A^{n}_{0}(\delta,\bm{I})\big)^{c}\Big)=\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\tilde{\Delta}\Big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma}).

Meanwhile, for each
k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]
and
j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(A^{n}_{k,j}(\delta,\bm{I})\big)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\left\lVert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(m)}_{j}(N\delta)-\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert>\tilde{\Delta}\text{ for some }N\geq\lceil n\delta^{k}\rceil\Bigg)\qquad\text{due to \eqref{property: lower bound for tau n delta i j k, cluster size}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{N\geq\lceil n\delta^{k}\rceil}\underbrace{\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\left\lVert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(m)}_{j}(N\delta)-\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert>\tilde{\Delta}\Bigg)}_{=p(N,\delta)}.

By Lemma
4.6
,
it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

p(N,\delta)=\mathit{o}(N^{-\gamma-2})

(as
N\to\infty
).
As a result, given any
\delta>0
sufficiently small,
there exists
\bar{N}(\delta)\in(0,\infty)
such that

p(N,\delta)\leq N^{-\gamma-2}

\forall N\geq\bar{N}(\delta)
.
Then, for any
n
large enough such that
n\delta^{k}\geq\bar{N}(\delta)
, we have

\sum_{N\geq\lceil n\delta^{k}\rceil}p(N,\delta)=\mathcal{O}(n^{-\gamma-1})=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma}).

In summary,
we have established that

\mathbf{P}\big(B^{n}_{i}(\delta,\bm{I},\epsilon,\Delta)\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})

for any
\delta>0
small enough.
This concludes the proof for the case where
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}\geq 1
.
Now, it remains to prove Claim (
4.58
),
under the choice of
\tilde{\Delta}
in (
4.53
).

Proof of Claim (
4.58
)
.
Using notations in (
4.55
), (
4.56
),
on the event
A^{n}_{*}(\delta,\bm{I})
we have

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}

\displaystyle=\bm{\Delta}_{0,i}+\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\cdot\big(\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}+\bm{\Delta}_{k,j}\big),

(4.59)

with
\left\lVert\bm{\Delta}_{k,j}\right\rVert\leq\tilde{\Delta}

for each
k=0,1,\ldots,\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}
and
j\in[d]
.
First, we show that on the event
A_{*}^{n}(\delta,\bm{I})
,

1.

\bar{R}^{n}_{i}\wedge\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}>\epsilon\rho/4;

2.

\left\lVert\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}-\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert\big/\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert\leq\Delta/2;

3.

\left\lVert\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{i}-\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert\leq\Delta.

To prove (i),
note that under the
L_{1}
norm
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert=\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\cdot\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert.

(4.60)

Likewise, in (
4.54
), the coordinates of each
\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(k,m)}_{i}(n\delta)
are non-negative by definition,
which implies

\displaystyle\bar{R}^{n}_{i}=\left\lVert\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\bm{\Delta}_{0,i}\right\rVert+\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\cdot\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}+\bm{\Delta}_{k,j}\right\rVert.

(4.61)

By definitions in (
4.57
),
on the event
A^{n}_{*}(\delta,\bm{I})
we have

\bar{R}^{n}_{i}>\epsilon
or
\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}>\epsilon
.
We first consider the case of
\bar{R}^{n}_{i}>\epsilon
.
By (
4.61
),
on the event
A^{n}_{*}(\delta,\bm{I})
we have

\epsilon<\tilde{\Delta}+\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\cdot\Big(\max_{j\in[d]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert+\tilde{\Delta}\Big).

Under the choice of
\tilde{\Delta}
in (
4.53
), it then holds on the event
A^{n}_{*}(\delta,\bm{I})

that

\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)>\frac{\epsilon}{4\max_{j\in[d]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert}.

Together with (
4.60
),
we confirm that (on the event
A^{n}_{*}(\delta,\bm{I})
)

\displaystyle\hat{R}^{n|\delta}

\displaystyle\geq\min_{j\in[d]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)>\frac{\epsilon}{4}\cdot\frac{\min_{j\in[d]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert}{\max_{j\in[d]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert}=\epsilon\rho/4;\quad\text{see \eqref{proof: constant tilde Delta, 1, lemma: distance between bar s and hat S given type, cluster size}.}

Similarly, if
\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}>\epsilon
,
then by (
4.60
),
we get

\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)>\frac{\epsilon}{\max_{j\in[d]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert},

and hence

\displaystyle\bar{R}^{n}_{i}\geq\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\cdot\bigg(\min_{j\in[d]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert-\tilde{\Delta}\bigg)

\displaystyle>\epsilon\rho/2\qquad\text{ by \eqref{proof: expression for bar R, lemma: distance between bar s and hat S given type, cluster size}.}

This concludes the proof of Claim (i).
Next,
it follows
from (
4.54
) and (
4.59
)
that

\left\lVert\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}-\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert\leq\tilde{\Delta}+\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\cdot\tilde{\Delta}.

This leads to

\displaystyle\frac{\left\lVert\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}-\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{\Delta}}{\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert}+\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\cdot\tilde{\Delta}}{\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{\Delta}}{\epsilon\cdot\rho/4}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)}{\min_{j\in[d]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)}\ \ \text{by Claim (i) and \eqref{proof: expression for hat R, lemma: distance between bar s and hat S given type, cluster size}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Delta}{4}+\frac{\Delta}{4}=\frac{\Delta}{2}\qquad\text{ by our choice of $\tilde{\Delta}$ in \eqref{proof: constant tilde Delta, 2, lemma: distance between bar s and hat S given type, cluster size}.}

This verifies Claim (ii).
For Claim (iii),
note again that
\bar{R}^{n}_{i}\wedge\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}>0
on the event
A^{n}_{*}(\delta,\bm{I})

by Claim (i).
Then, by the definition in (
4.21
),

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{i}-\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\frac{\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}}{\left\lVert\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}\right\rVert}-\frac{\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}}{\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\frac{\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}-\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}}{\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert}-\frac{1}{\left\lVert\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}\right\rVert}\bigg)\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\frac{\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}-\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert}+\left\lVert\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}\right\rVert\cdot\frac{\Big|\left\lVert\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}\right\rVert-\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert\Big|}{\left\lVert\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}\right\rVert\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert}\leq\frac{\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}-\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert}+\frac{\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}-\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert}

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot\frac{\Delta}{2}=\Delta\qquad\text{by Claim (ii)}.

This verifies Claim (iii).
Lastly, Claims (ii) and (iii) imply that, on the event
A_{*}^{n}(\delta,\bm{I})
, we have

{\bar{R}^{n}_{i}}\big/{\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}}\in[1-\Delta,1+\Delta]

and

\left\lVert\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{i}-\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert\leq\Delta.

This concludes the proof of Claim (
4.58
).

Proof of the case with
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}=0
.
If
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}=0
(i.e.,
I_{k,j}\equiv 0\ \forall k\geq 1,j\in[d]
),
it holds on the event
\{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\}
that

\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{0}
and
\bar{\bm{S}}^{n}_{i}=n^{-1}\bm{S}_{i}^{\leqslant,(1,1)}(n\delta)
.
Therefore,
on the event

\big\{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I},\ \bar{R}^{n}_{i}\vee\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}>\epsilon\big\},

we have
\left\lVert n^{-1}\bm{S}_{i}^{\leqslant,(1,1)}(n\delta)\right\rVert>\epsilon
.
Applying Lemma
4.5
again,
we get

\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{S}_{i}^{\leqslant,(1,1)}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\epsilon\Big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})

for any
\delta>0
small enough.
∎

We are now ready to state the proof of Proposition
4.3
.

Proof of Proposition
4.3
.

Define the event

\displaystyle E^{n}_{\delta}(\Delta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{\bar{R}^{n}_{i}\vee\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}>\Delta\big\}\cap\bigg\{\frac{\bar{R}^{n}_{i}}{\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i}}\notin[1-\Delta,1+\Delta]\text{ or }\left\lVert\bar{\Theta}^{n}_{i}-\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i}\right\rVert>\Delta\bigg\}.

First,
due to the arbitrariness of
\Delta>0
in Claim (
4.25
)
and the simple fact (for any
c\in(0,1)
and
r,r^{\prime}\geq 0
)

\displaystyle r\vee r^{\prime}>0,\ \frac{r}{r^{\prime}}\notin[1-c,1+c]\quad\Longrightarrow\quad r\vee r^{\prime}>0,\ \frac{r^{\prime}}{r}\notin[1/(1+c),1/(1-c)],

it is equivalent to show that, given
\Delta\in(0,1)
,
it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\mathbf{P}\big(E^{n}_{\delta}(\Delta)\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma}).

Next,
recall the definitions of
\tilde{\alpha}(\cdot)
in (
4.29
)
and
\alpha(\cdot)
in (
3.4
).
Due to

\mathbf{P}\big(E^{n}_{\delta}(\Delta)\big)=\mathbf{P}\Big(E^{n}_{\delta}(\Delta)\cap\big\{\tilde{\alpha}\big(\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)>\alpha(\bm{j})\big\}\Big)+\mathbf{P}\Big(E^{n}_{\delta}(\Delta)\cap\big\{\tilde{\alpha}\big(\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\leq\alpha(\bm{j})\big\}\Big),

it suffices to show that (for any
\delta>0
small enough)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\Big(\tilde{\alpha}\big(\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)>\alpha(\bm{j})\Big)

\displaystyle=0,

(4.62)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\Big(E^{n}_{\delta}(\Delta)\cap\big\{\tilde{\alpha}\big(\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\leq\alpha(\bm{j})\big\}\Big)

\displaystyle=0.

(4.63)

Proof of Claim (
4.62
)
.
We first define the set (where the partial ordering for generalized types is defined in (
4.44
))

\displaystyle\partial\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Big\{\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}:\ \tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})>\alpha(\bm{j});\ \tilde{\alpha}(\hat{\bm{I}})\leq\alpha(\bm{j})\text{ for any $\hat{\bm{I}}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}$ with }\hat{\bm{I}}\subseteq\bm{I},\ \hat{\bm{I}}\neq\bm{I}\Big\},

and stress the following:
for any
\bm{I}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
with
\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I}^{\prime})>\alpha(\bm{j})
, there must be some
\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{\prime}
such that
\bm{I}\in\partial\mathscr{I}(\bm{j}).

To see why, note that
the function
\tilde{\alpha}(\cdot)
in (
4.29
) is linear w.r.t. the
I_{k,j}
’s, and the coefficients
(\alpha^{*}(j)-1)_{j\in[d]}
are strictly positive under Assumption
2
.
Then, given any
\bm{I}^{\prime}=(I^{\prime}_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
with
\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I}^{\prime})>\alpha(\bm{j})
,
by identifying the smallest
K\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}^{\prime}}]
satisfying

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j\in[d]}I^{\prime}_{k,j}\big(\alpha^{*}(j)-1\big)>\alpha(\bm{j}),

and setting
\bm{I}=(I_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}
with
I_{k,j}=I^{\prime}_{k,j}\ \forall j
if
k\leq K
and
I_{k,j}\equiv 0
if
k>K
,
we have

\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{\prime}
and
\bm{I}\in\partial\mathscr{I}(\bm{j}).

Also, due to
\alpha^{*}(j)-1>0
for each
j\in[d]
,
the set
\partial\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
contains only finitely many elements.
In summary, we get

\big\{\tilde{\alpha}\big(\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)>\alpha(\bm{j})\big\}\subseteq\bigcup_{\bm{I}\in\partial\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})}\big\{\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big\},

and it suffices to show that, given
\bm{I}\in\partial\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
, it holds for all
\delta>0
small enough that

\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)=\mathit{o}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)

as
n\to\infty
.
To proceed, let

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}^{\bm{I}}(n)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}n^{-1}\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta\big),\qquad\forall\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}},

(4.64)

and fix some
\bm{I}\in\partial\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
.
First, due to
\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})>\alpha(\bm{j})
, we have
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}\geq 1
(otherwise, we get
\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})=0
by definition).
Then,
by Lemma
4.8
,
it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)=\mathcal{O}\big(\tilde{\lambda}^{\bm{I}}(n)\big).

Due to
\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha(\bm{j})}(n)
,

\tilde{\lambda}^{\bm{I}}(n)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})}(n),

and

\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})>\alpha(\bm{j}),

we get

\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)=\mathit{o}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big).

This concludes the proof of Claim (
4.62
).

Proof of Claim (
4.63
)
.
Due to
\alpha^{*}(j)>1
for each
j\in[d]
(see (
3.3
) and Assumption
2
),
there are only finitely many generalized types
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
such that
\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})\leq\alpha(\bm{j})
.
Therefore, it suffices to fix one of such
\bm{I}
and show that

\mathbf{P}\big(\{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\}\cap E^{n}_{\delta}(\Delta)\big)=\mathit{o}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)

(as
n\to\infty
) for any
\delta>0
small enough.
Applying Lemma
4.9
, we conclude the proof of
Claim (
4.63
).
∎

4.3
Proof of Proposition
4.4

We first prepare a few technical lemmas.
Lemma
4.10
states useful properties of
types (Definition
3.1
) and
generalized types (Definition
4.1
).

Lemma 4.10
.

Let Assumption
2
hold.
For any
\bm{j}\subseteq[d]
that is non-empty, let

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.19441pt\raisebox{5.64096pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-3.19441pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}:\ \bm{j}^{\bm{I}}=\bm{j}\},\quad\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.19441pt\raisebox{5.64096pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-3.19441pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}:\ \bm{j}^{\bm{I}}=\bm{j},\ \tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})=\alpha(\bm{j})\},

(4.65)

where
\mathscr{I}
is the set of all types,
\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
is the set of all generalized types,

\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}
is the set of active indices of
\bm{I}
(see Definition
4.1
),
and
\tilde{\alpha}(\cdot)
,
\alpha(\cdot)
are defined in (
4.29
) and (
3.4
), respectively.
The following claims hold for any non-empty
\bm{j}\subseteq[d]
:

1.

\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})\subseteq\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})
;

2.

It holds for any
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})\setminus\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
that
|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}|\geq 2
.

Lemma
4.11
studies geometric properties
of sets that are bounded away from
\mathbb{C}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})
.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let Assumption
2
hold.
Let
\bm{j}\subseteq[d]
be non-empty, and
B\subseteq[0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}
be a Borel set that is bounded away from
\mathbb{C}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})
(see (
4.24
)) under
\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}
(see (
2.6
)).

1.

There exist
\bar{\epsilon}>0
and
\bar{\delta}>0
such that the following claims hold:
given
\bm{x}=\sum_{i\in\bm{j}}w_{i}\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}
with
w_{i}\geq 0\ \forall i\in\bm{j}
,
if
\Phi(\bm{x})\in B
(with
\Phi(\cdot)
defined in (
2.7
)),
then

•

\min_{j\in\bm{j}}w_{j}\geq\bar{\epsilon}
,

•

{w_{j}}/{w_{i}}\geq\bar{\delta}
for any
j\in\bm{j}
and
i\in\bm{j}
;

2.

\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}\circ\Phi^{-1}(B)<\infty

for any
\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}
with
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}=\bm{j}
,
where

\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}
is the set of active indices of type
\bm{I}
in Definition
3.1
,

\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}
is defined in (
3.11
),
and
\mu\circ\Phi^{-1}(B)=\mu\big(\Phi^{-1}(B)\big)
.

These two results follow directly from the definitions of types, the functions
\tilde{\alpha}(\cdot)
,
\alpha(\cdot)
, and the measure
\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}
.
We collect the proofs of Lemmas
4.10
and
4.11
in Section
D
of the Appendix.
Next, we prepare results for the asymptotic analysis of
\big(n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\big)_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}
.
For each
\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}
and
M,c>0
, we define the set

\displaystyle E^{\bm{I}}(M,c)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Bigg\{(w_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}\in[0,\infty)^{\infty\times d}:

(4.66)

\displaystyle w_{k,j}>M\text{ and }\min_{k^{\prime}\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],j^{\prime}\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k^{\prime}}}\frac{w_{k,j}}{w_{k^{\prime},j^{\prime}}}\geq c\ \forall k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k};\ w_{k,j}=0\ \forall k\geq 1,\ j\notin\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\Bigg\},

where
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}
is the depth of type
\bm{I}
, and
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}
is the set of active indices of type
\bm{I}
at depth
k
; see Definition
3.1
.
In particular, for any
k>\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}
we have
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}=\emptyset
by definition.
Meanwhile, recall the definitions of
\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)
in (
4.9
) and (
4.11
).
In this section,
we write

\displaystyle\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\Big)_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}.

(4.67)

Lemma
4.12
bounds
the probability that
n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}
lies outside of a bounded set.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.
Let
i\in[d]
,
c\in(0,1)
, and let the type
\bm{I}=(I_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}\in\mathscr{I}
be such that
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}\geq 1
(i.e.,
I_{k,j}\geq 1
for some
k,j
).
There exists some function
C^{\bm{I}}_{i}(\cdot,c)
with
\lim_{M\to\infty}C^{\bm{I}}_{i}(M,c)=0
such that,
for each
M>0
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\Big(\lambda_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}}(n)\Big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\Big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}(M,c)\Big)\leq C^{\bm{I}}_{i}(M,c),\quad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough},

where
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}
is the set of active indices of type
\bm{I}
(see Definition
3.1
),

\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)
is defined in (
3.5
),

{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}}=\big(I^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\big)_{k\geq 1,\ j\in[d]}
is defined in (
4.22
),
and
E^{\bm{I}}(M,c)
is defined in (
4.66
).

Next, for each type
\bm{I}=(I_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}\in\mathscr{I}
with
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}\geq 1
and each
i\in[d]
, define

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}(\cdot)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\int\mathbbm{I}\bigg\{(w_{k,j})_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j\in\bm{j}_{k}^{\bm{I}}}\in\ \bm{\cdot}\ \bigg\}\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1}g_{\bm{j}_{k}^{\bm{I}}\leftarrow\bm{j}_{k+1}^{\bm{I}}}(\bm{w}_{k})\bigg)\nu^{\bm{I}}(d\bm{w}),\quad{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}_{i}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j^{\bm{I}}_{1})}\cdot{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}_{i}}

(4.68)

where

{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}
is the set of active indices at depth
k
of type
\bm{I}
,
the mapping

g_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}(\bm{w})
is defined in (
3.9
),

\nu^{\bm{I}}(d\bm{w})
is defined in (
3.10
),
and, as noted in Remark
2
,
j_{1}^{\bm{I}}
is the unique index in
\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
such that
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}=\{j^{\bm{I}}_{1}\}
.
Also, for any type
\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}
with
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}\geq 1
, let

\displaystyle h^{\bm{I}}(\bm{w})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}w_{k,j}\bar{\bm{s}}_{j},\qquad\forall\bm{w}=(w_{k,j})_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}.

(4.69)

By the definition of
\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}_{i}
in (
3.12
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}_{i}(B)={\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}}_{i}\Big((h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}(B)\Big),\qquad\forall\text{ Borel measurable }B\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}.

(4.70)

Lemma
4.13

studies the (asymptotic) law of

n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}
when restricted on compact sets.

Lemma 4.13
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.
Let
i\in[d]
and
0<c<C<\infty
.
Let
\bm{j}\subseteq[d]
be non-empty,
and
\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}
be such that
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}=\bm{j}
.
There exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that
for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})

and any

\bm{x}=(x_{k,j})_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}
,

\bm{y}=(y_{k,j})_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}

with
c\leq x_{k,j}<y_{k,j}\leq C\ \forall k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\Big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in A^{\bm{I}}(\bm{x},\bm{y})\Big)=\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\Bigg(\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}(x_{k,j},y_{k,j}]\Bigg),

(4.71)

where

\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}
is defined in (
4.67
),

\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}_{i}
is defined in (
4.68
),

\bar{s}_{i,j}=\mathbf{E}S_{i,j}
(see (
3.2
)),

j^{\bm{I}}_{1}
is the unique index
j\in[d]
such that
I_{j,1}=1
(see Remark
2
),
and

\displaystyle A^{\bm{I}}(\bm{x},\bm{y})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.19441pt\raisebox{5.64096pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-3.19441pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\Big\{(w_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}\in[0,\infty)^{\infty\times d}:

(4.72)

\displaystyle w_{k,j}\in(x_{k,j},y_{k,j}]\ \forall k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k};\ w_{k,j}=0\ \forall k\geq 1,\ j\notin\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\Big\}.

In essence, Lemmas
4.12
and
4.13
refine the asymptotics in Lemma
4.8
.
Their proofs follow the same spirit as Lemma
4.8

and proceed by
combining the asymptotics in Lemma
4.7
with the Markov property (
4.34
).
The key difference is that, this time, we apply (
4.41
) and (
4.42
)
to characterize the asymptotic law of
n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}
,
rather than relying on the other cruder estimates in Lemma
4.7
and only obtaining the asymptotics of

\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}

defined in (
4.22
),
which simply indicates the positivity of the coordinates in
n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}
.
To avoid repetition,
we defer the proofs of Lemmas
4.12
and
4.13
to Section
D
of the Appendix.

Now, we state the proof of Proposition
4.4
using the technical tools introduced above.
We first prove part (iii) of Proposition
4.4
,
and then move onto parts (i) and (ii).

Proof of Proposition
4.4
, Part (iii).

Follows from
part (b) of Lemma
4.11
,
as well as

\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\circ\Phi^{-1}=\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j_{1})}\cdot\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}\circ\Phi^{-1};

see (
3.12
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.4
, Part (i).

Since there are only finitely many elements in
\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
,
it suffices to fix some
\Delta>0
and type
\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
, and then verify (
4.26
) for any
\delta>0
small enough.
Also, since the set
B
(and hence its closure
B^{-}
) is bounded away from
\mathbb{C}_{\leqslant}^{d}(\bm{j})
under
\bm{d}_{\text{U}}
,
by part (a) of Lemma
4.11

there exists
\bar{\epsilon}\in(0,1)
such that
for any
\bm{x}=\sum_{j\in\bm{j}}w_{j}\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}
with
w_{j}\geq 0\ \forall j\in\bm{j}
,

\displaystyle\Phi(\bm{x})\in B^{-}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\min_{j\in\bm{j}}w_{j}>\bar{\epsilon},\text{ and }\min_{j,j^{\prime}\in\bm{j}}\frac{w_{j}}{w_{j^{\prime}}}>\bar{\epsilon}.

(4.73)

Define

\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}:[0,\infty)^{\infty\times d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}

by
\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}(\bm{w})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}w_{k,j}\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}

for any
\bm{w}=(w_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}
.
That is,
\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}
trivially extends the domain of
h^{\bm{I}}

to
[0,\infty)^{\infty\times d}
.
By the definition of
h^{\bm{I}}
in (
4.69
) and
that
{(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{j},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{j})}=\Phi(\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{j})

(see (
4.20
)–(
4.21
)),
on the event
\{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\}

we have

(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{j},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{j})=\Phi\Big(\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\Big).

Meanwhile, regarding the type
\bm{I}=(I_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}\in\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
fixed at the beginning of the proof,
by the third bullet point in Definition
3.1
,
for each
j\in\bm{j}
there uniquely exists some
k(j)\geq 1
such that
I_{k(j),j}=1
.
Then,
by (
4.73
)
and the definition of
{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}}=\big(I^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\big)_{k\geq 1,\ j\in[d]}
in (
4.22
),
we have

\displaystyle\Phi\Big(\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\Big)\in B,\ \bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\Phi\Big(\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\Big)\in B,\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}(\bar{\epsilon},\bar{\epsilon}),

where the set
E^{\bm{I}}(M,c)
is defined in (
4.66
).
Therefore, for any
M>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big((\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i})\in B,\ \bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Phi\Big(\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\Big)\in B,\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}(\bar{\epsilon},\bar{\epsilon})\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\Big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}(M,\bar{\epsilon})\Big)+\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Phi\Big(\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\Big)\in B,\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}(\bar{\epsilon},\bar{\epsilon})\setminus E^{\bm{I}}(M,\bar{\epsilon})\bigg).

By defining

\displaystyle E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}E^{\bm{I}}(\bar{\epsilon},\bar{\epsilon})\setminus E^{\bm{I}}(M,\bar{\epsilon}),

(4.74)

we obtain the upper bound (for each
M>0
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big((\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i})\in B,\ \bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\Big)

(4.75)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\Big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}(M,\bar{\epsilon})\Big)+\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Phi\Big(\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\Big)\in B,\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\bigg).

Likewise, we get the lower bound

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big((\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i})\in B,\ \bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\Big)\geq\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Phi\Big(\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\Big)\in B,\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\bigg).

(4.76)

Recall that we arbitrarily picked some
\Delta>0
at the beginning.
Suppose there exists some
\bar{M}=\bar{M}(\Delta)>0
such that, given
M>\bar{M}
,
it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\Big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}(M,\bar{\epsilon})\Big)<\Delta,

(4.77)

and

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Phi\Big(\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\Big)\in B,\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\circ\Phi^{-1}(B^{\Delta}),

(4.78)

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Phi\Big(\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\Big)\in B,\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\circ\Phi^{-1}(B_{\Delta})-\Delta.

Then, by plugging these claims into the upper and lower bounds (
4.75
)–(
4.76
),
we conclude the proof for part (i) of Proposition
4.4
.
Now, it remains to verify Claims (
4.77
)–(
4.78
).

Proof of Claim (
4.77
)
.
This is exactly the content of Lemma
4.12
.
In particular, it suffices to prick
\bar{M}
large enough such that, in Lemma
4.12
,
C_{i}^{\bm{I}}(M,\bar{\epsilon})<\Delta
for any
M>\bar{M}
.

Proof of Claim (
4.78
)
.
For any
\bm{w}=(w_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}\in[0,\infty)^{\infty\times d}
,
we define
\psi^{\bm{I}}(\bm{w})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}(w_{k,j})_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}
.
That is,

\psi^{\bm{I}}
is the projection mapping from
[0,\infty)^{\infty\times d}
onto the coordinates corresponding to the active indices of
\bm{I}
.
Also, recall the definition of the set
E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)
in (
4.74
).
Given
M>0
,
Lemma
4.13
shows that
for any
\delta>0
small enough,

\displaystyle\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\bigg(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M);\ \psi^{\bm{I}}\Big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\Big)\in\ \cdot\ \bigg)\Rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\bigg(\ \cdot\ \cap\psi^{\bm{I}}\Big(E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\Big)\bigg)

(4.79)

(as
n\to\infty
)
in terms of weak convergence of finite measures.
To see why, it suffices to note the following.

•

By Lemma
4.8
,
we confirm that for any
\delta>0
small enough,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\Big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\Big)\leq\sup_{n\geq 1}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)<\infty;

In other words,
the LHS of (
4.79
) is a sequence of finite measures with a uniform upper bound on their masses.

•

Fix some
\bm{w}=(w_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}\in E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)
.
By the definitions of
E^{\bm{I}}(M,c)
in (
4.66
)
and
E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)
in (
4.74
),
there exists some
k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]
and
j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}
such that

w_{k,j}\in(\bar{\epsilon},M].

Then, by the condition that

w_{k,j}/w_{k^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\geq\bar{\epsilon}

for any
k,k^{\prime}\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]
and
j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}
,
j^{\prime}\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k^{\prime}}
(see (
4.66
)),
we must have
w_{k,j}\in(\bar{\epsilon},M/\bar{\epsilon}]
for each
k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]
,
j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}
.

•

Furthermore,
the sets of the form

\bigtimes_{i\in m}(x_{i},y_{i}]

studied in Lemma
4.13

constitute a convergent determining class for the weak convergence of finite measures on
(\bar{\epsilon},M/\bar{\epsilon}]^{m}
.
This allows us to apply Lemma
4.13
and verify (
4.79
) for any
\delta>0
small enough.

To apply the weak convergence in (
4.79
),
we make a few observations.
First,
by definitions of
\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}
and
\psi^{\bm{I}}
, we have

\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}(\bm{w})=h^{\bm{I}}\big(\psi^{\bm{I}}(\bm{w})\big)

for any
\bm{w}\in[0,\infty)^{\infty\times d}
,
which implies

\displaystyle\Big\{\Phi\Big(\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\Big)\in B,\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\Big\}

(4.80)

\displaystyle=\Big\{\psi^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\in(h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B)\big),\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\Big\}.

Next, note that
\Phi\circ h^{\bm{I}}(\cdot)
is continuous at any
(w_{k,j})_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}

with
w_{k,j}>0\ \forall k,j
,
and that

B^{-}\subseteq\Phi\circ h^{\bm{I}}\Big(\Big\{(w_{k,j})_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}:\ w_{k,j}>0\ \forall k,j\Big\}\Big);

see (
4.73
).
This implies that

(h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B^{-})\big)

is closed and

(h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B^{\circ})\big)

is open, and hence

\displaystyle\Big((h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B)\big)\Big)^{-}\subseteq(h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B^{-})\big),\ \Big((h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B)\big)\Big)^{\circ}\supseteq(h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B^{\circ})\big).

(4.81)

As a result, for any
\delta>0
small enough,
