\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Phi\Big(\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\Big)\in B,\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\psi^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\in(h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B)\big),\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\bigg)\ \text{by \eqref{proof, equiavlence for projection psi and extension bar h, proposition, M convergence for hat S, tail asymptotics for cluster size, part i}}

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\bigg(\Big((h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B^{-})\big)\Big)\cap\psi^{\bm{I}}\Big(E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\Big)\bigg)\quad\text{by \eqref{proof: goal, weak convergence, proposition, M convergence for hat S, tail asymptotics for cluster size} and \eqref{proof, closure and interior under Phi h composition, proposition, M convergence for hat S, tail asymptotics for cluster size, part i}}

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\Big((h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B^{-})\big)\Big)=\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\Big(\Phi^{-1}(B^{-})\Big)=\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\bm{I}}\circ\Phi^{-1}(B^{-})\quad\text{by \eqref{property: connection between C I and hat C I measures, cluster size}.}

This verifies the upper bound in Claim (
4.78
) for any
M>0
.
Likewise,
using (
4.80
), (
4.81
), and the weak convergence in (
4.79
),
given
M>0

we obtain the lower bound

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Phi\Big(\bar{h}^{\bm{I}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\Big)\in B,\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\bigg)

(4.82)

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\bigg(\Big((h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B^{\circ})\big)\Big)\cap\psi^{\bm{I}}\Big(E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\Big)\bigg)

for any
\delta>0
small enough.
To further bound the RHS of (
4.82
),
we make a few observations.
First,
(
4.73
) implies that for any

\bm{w}=(w_{k,j})_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}

with
w_{k,j}\geq 0\ \forall k,j

and

\Phi\big(h^{\bm{I}}(\bm{w})\big)\in B
,
we must have

\bm{w}\in\psi^{\bm{I}}\big(E^{\bm{I}}(\bar{\epsilon},\bar{\epsilon})\big).

As a result,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\bigg(\Big((h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B^{\circ})\big)\Big)\cap\psi^{\bm{I}}\big(E^{\bm{I}}(\bar{\epsilon},\bar{\epsilon})\big)\bigg)=\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\Big((h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B^{\circ})\big)\Big).

Next,
the sequence of sets
E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)
in (
4.74
)
is monotone increasing w.r.t.
M
,
with

\bigcup_{M>0}E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)=E^{\bm{I}}(\bar{\epsilon},\bar{\epsilon}).

On the other hand,
by (
4.70
) we get

\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\big((h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}(\Phi^{-1}(B^{\circ}))\big)=\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\bm{I}}\circ\Phi^{-1}(B^{\circ}).

Also,
part (iii) of Proposition
4.4
,
which we established earlier,
confirms that
\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\bm{I}}\circ\Phi^{-1}(B^{\circ})<\infty
.
Then, by continuity of measures, there exists
\bar{M}=\bar{M}(\Delta)
such that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}_{i}\bigg(\Big((h^{\bm{I}})^{-1}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B^{\circ})\big)\Big)\cap\psi^{\bm{I}}\Big(E^{\bm{I}}_{\leqslant}(M)\Big)\bigg)>\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\bm{I}}\circ\Phi^{-1}(B^{\circ})-\Delta,\quad\forall M>\bar{M}.

Plugging this into (
4.82
), we conclude the proof for the lower bound in Claim (
4.78
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.4
, Part (ii).

Recall the definitions of
\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})
in (
4.65
),
and that we have fixed some non-empty
\bm{j}\subseteq\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
in the statement of this proposition.
Note that if, for some generalized type
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})
, we have
\bm{I}\notin\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
,
then, there are only three possibilities:
(1)
\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})>\alpha(\bm{j})
;
(2)
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})\setminus\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
;
or
(3)
\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})\leq\alpha(\bm{j}),\ \bm{I}\notin\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})
.
Therefore, to prove part (ii), it suffices to show that (for any
\delta>0
small enough)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\Big(\tilde{\alpha}\big(\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)>\alpha(\bm{j})\Big)

\displaystyle=0,

(4.83)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\Big((\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i})\in B,\ \bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})\setminus{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})\Big)

\displaystyle=0,

(4.84)

\displaystyle\Big\{(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i})\in B,\ \tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})\leq\alpha(\bm{j}),\ \bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\notin\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})\Big\}

\displaystyle=\emptyset.

(4.85)

Proof of Claim (
4.83
)
.
This is verified
in the proof of Proposition
4.3
; see Claim (
4.62
).

Proof of Claim (
4.84
)
.
Due to
\alpha^{*}(j)\in(1,\infty)\ \forall j\in[d]
(see Assumption
2
and (
3.3
)),
we must have
|\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})\setminus{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})|<\infty
,
so
it suffices to fix some
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})\setminus{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})
and show that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\big)\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)

\displaystyle=0,\quad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough,}

where the partial ordering
\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{\prime}
is defined in (
4.44
).
By part (ii) of Lemma
4.10
,
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})\setminus{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})
implies that
|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}|\geq 2
.
This allows us to apply the Claim (
4.46
) of Lemma
4.8

and get

\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)=\mathit{o}\big(\tilde{\lambda}^{\bm{I}}(n)\big)

for any
\delta>0
small enough,
with

\tilde{\lambda}^{\bm{I}}(n)
defined in (
4.64
).
Also,
by the definition of
\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})
, we have
\alpha(\bm{j})=\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})
and
\bm{j}=\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}
.
Then, by property (
4.32
)
and the definition of
\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)
in (
3.5
),
we must have

\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)=\tilde{\lambda}^{\bm{I}}(n).

This verifies

\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{I}\subseteq\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)=\mathit{o}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)

for any
\delta>0
small enough and
concludes the proof of Claim (
4.84
).

Proof of Claim (
4.85
)
.
We arbitrarily pick some generalized type
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}

such that
\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})\leq\alpha(\bm{j})
and
\bm{I}\notin\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})
.
By the definition of
\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})
in (
4.65
),
for such
\bm{I}
we either have
\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})<\alpha(\bm{j})
,
or
\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})=\alpha(\bm{j}),\ \bm{j}^{\bm{I}}\neq\bm{j}
,
where
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}
is the set of active indices in
\bm{I}
(see Definition
4.1
),
and
\bm{j}\subseteq\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
is the non-empty set prescribed in the statement of this proposition.
In both cases,
due to (
4.30
), we must have

\displaystyle\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}\neq\bm{j},\qquad{\alpha}(\bm{j}^{\bm{I}})\leq\alpha(\bm{j}).

(4.86)

Meanwhile,
it holds on the event
\{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\}
that

\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\cdot\bar{\bm{s}}_{j};

see (
4.20
).
Then,
by (
4.86
)
and the definition of
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})=\bigcup_{\bm{j}^{\prime}\neq\bm{j},\ \alpha(\bm{j}^{\prime})\leq\alpha(\bm{j})}\mathbb{R}^{d}(\bm{j}^{\prime})
in (
2.3
),
on the event
\{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\}

we must have
\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})
,
and hence

\Phi(\hat{\bm{S}}^{n|\delta}_{i})=\big(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\in\mathbb{C}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j});

see (
4.24
).
Since
B
is bounded away from
\mathbb{C}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})
, we have just confirmed that

\big\{\big(\hat{R}^{n|\delta}_{i},\hat{\Theta}^{n|\delta}_{i}\big)\in B,\ \bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I}\big\}=\emptyset.

Repeating this argument for each generalized type
\bm{I}

satisfying
\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})\leq\alpha(\bm{j})
and
\bm{I}\notin\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})
,
we conclude the proof of Claim (
4.85
).
∎

4.4
Proof of Lemma
4.7

Recall
the definitions of
W^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M)
,
\ W^{>}_{i;j}(M)
,
N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M)
, and
N^{>}_{i;j}(M)

in (
3.21
)–(
3.24
), and that
\bar{s}_{i,j}=\mathbf{E}S_{i,j}
.
To prove Lemma
4.7
, we prepare the following result.

Lemma 4.14
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.

1.

Let
\delta>0
. As
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)=1\Big)

\displaystyle\sim\bar{s}_{i,l}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta),

(4.87)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 2\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathit{o}\big(\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)\big).

(4.88)

2.

There exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that
for any
\mathcal{T}\subseteq[d]^{2}
with
|\mathcal{T}|\geq 2
and any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1\ \forall(l,j)\in\mathcal{T}\Big)=\mathit{o}\bigg(n^{|\mathcal{T}|-1}\cdot\prod_{(l,j)\in\mathcal{T}}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)\bigg),\quad\text{ as }n\to\infty.

Proof.

(i)
Suppose that we can verify (as
n\to\infty
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\big[N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\big]\sim\bar{s}_{i,l}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta),

(4.89)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)\sim\bar{s}_{i,l}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta).

(4.90)

Then, note that for a sequence of random variables
Z_{n}
taking non-negative integer values,
by the elementary bound
\mathbf{E}Z_{n}=\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{P}(Z_{n}\geq k)\geq\mathbf{P}(Z_{n}\geq 2)+\mathbf{P}(Z_{n}\geq 1)
,
we get

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}Z_{n}}{a_{n}}=c,\ \lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}(Z_{n}\geq 1)}{a_{n}}=c\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}(Z_{n}=1)}{a_{n}}=c,\ \lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}(Z_{n}\geq 2)}{a_{n}}=0

for any
c>0
and any sequence of strictly positive real numbers
a_{n}
.
Therefore, the asymptotics stated in (
4.87
) and (
4.88
) follow from Claims (
4.89
) and (
4.90
).
Next, we prove these two claims.

Proof of Claim (
4.89
)
.
By definitions in (
3.19
), (
3.20
), (
3.23
), (
3.24
),

N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M)
counts the number of type-
l
nodes with pruned type-
j
children under threshold
M

in the branching process
\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(t;M)
.
Therefore, the
N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M)
’s solve the fixed-point equations

\displaystyle N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbbm{I}\{i=l,\ B_{j\leftarrow l}>M\}+\sum_{k\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{B_{k\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\{B_{k\leftarrow i}\leq M\}}N^{>,(m)}_{k;j\leftarrow l}(M),\qquad i,j,l\in[d],

where the
N^{>,(m)}_{k;j\leftarrow l}(M)
’s are independent copies of
N^{>}_{k;j\leftarrow l}(M)
.
Let
\bar{\textbf{B}}^{\leqslant M}=(\bar{b}^{\leqslant M}_{j\leftarrow i})_{i,j\in[d]}
:
that is, the element on the
i^{\text{th}}
row and
j^{\text{th}}
column is

\bar{b}^{\leqslant M}_{j\leftarrow i}=\mathbf{E}B_{j\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\{B_{j\leftarrow i}\leq M\}
.
Provided that the spectral radius of
\bar{\textbf{B}}^{\leqslant M}
is strictly less than 1,
we can
apply Proposition 1 of
[
1
]
and get

\bar{\bm{n}}^{j\leftarrow l}=\bar{\bm{q}}^{j\leftarrow l}+\bar{\textbf{B}}^{\leqslant M}\bar{\bm{n}}^{j\leftarrow l},

where the vectors
\bar{\bm{n}}^{j\leftarrow l}=(\bar{n}^{j\leftarrow l}_{1},\ldots,\bar{n}^{j\leftarrow l}_{d})^{\top},\ \bar{\bm{q}}^{j\leftarrow l}=(\bar{q}^{j\leftarrow l}_{1},\ldots,\bar{q}^{j\leftarrow l}_{d})^{\top}
are defined by

{\bar{n}}^{j\leftarrow l}_{k}=\mathbf{E}\big[N^{>}_{k;j\leftarrow l}(M)\big]

and

\bar{q}^{j\leftarrow l}_{k}=\mathbbm{I}\{k=l\}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>M).

This implies

\bar{\bm{n}}^{j\leftarrow l}=(\textbf{I}-\bar{\textbf{B}}^{\leqslant M})^{-1}\bar{\bm{q}}^{j\leftarrow l},

and hence

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\big[N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M)\big]=\mathbf{E}\big[S^{\leqslant}_{i,l}(M)\big]\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>M),\qquad\forall i,l,j\in[d].

(4.91)

In particular,
due to Assumption
1
and monotone convergence,
it holds for any
M
large enough that
\bar{\textbf{B}}^{\leqslant M}
has a spectral radius less than
1
.
Besides,
applying monotone convergence
to
\sum_{t\geq 0}\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{i}(t;M)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M)

and

\sum_{t\geq 0}\bm{X}_{i}(t)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bm{S}_{i}

(see (
3.16
)–(
3.20
)),
we get

\lim_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{E}S^{\leqslant}_{i,l}(M)=\mathbf{E}S_{i,l}=\bar{s}_{i,l}.

By setting
M=n\delta
in (
4.91
) and sending
n\to\infty
,
we conclude the proof of Claim (
4.89
),
where we must have
\bar{s}_{i,l}>0
under Assumption
3
.

Proof of Claim (
4.90
)
.
Combining (
4.89
) with Markov inequality, we are able to obtain the upper bound

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)\big/\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)\leq\bar{s}_{i,l}.

Now, we focus on establishing

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)\big/\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)\geq\bar{s}_{i,l}.

(4.92)

First,
the definition of
N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M)

in (
3.23
)
is equivalent to

\displaystyle N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M)

\displaystyle=\#\Big\{(t,m)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}:\ m\leq X^{\leqslant}_{i,l}(t-1;M),\ B^{(t,m)}_{j\leftarrow l}>M\Big\}.

(4.93)

Next, given
M^{\prime}>M>0
,
the stochastic comparison in (
4.2
), (
4.3
) implies

\displaystyle N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M^{\prime})\geq\underbrace{\#\Big\{(t,m)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}:\ m\leq X^{\leqslant}_{i,l}(t-1;M),\ B^{(t,m)}_{j\leftarrow l}>M^{\prime}\Big\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\hat{N}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M,M^{\prime})}.

(4.94)

Furthermore, the branching process
(\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{i}(t;M))_{t\geq 0}
is independent
from the actual value of any
B_{j\leftarrow l}^{(t,m)}
if
B_{j\leftarrow l}^{(t,m)}\in\{0\}\cup(M,\infty)
:
indeed, the pruning mechanism in (
3.19
) would always result in

B_{j\leftarrow l}^{(t,m)}\mathbbm{I}\{B_{j\leftarrow l}^{(t,m)}\leq M\}=0

in such cases.
This leads to a coupling between
(\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{i}(t;M))_{t\geq 0}
and the
B^{(t,m)}_{j\leftarrow l}
’s,
where we first generate the branching process
(\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{i}(t;M))_{t\geq 0}
under offspring counts

\hat{B}_{j\leftarrow l}^{(t,m)}(M)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}B_{j\leftarrow l}^{(t,m)}\mathbbm{I}\{B_{j\leftarrow l}^{(t,m)}\leq M\}
,
and then, independently for each
(t,m,j,l)
, recover
B_{j\leftarrow l}^{(t,m)}
based on the value of
\hat{B}_{j\leftarrow l}^{(t,m)}
.
More specifically,
given
M^{\prime}>M>0
,
the term

\hat{N}^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M,M^{\prime})
in (
4.94
) can be generated as follows:

1.

first, we generate
(\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{i}(t;M))_{t\geq 0}
as a branching process under offspring counts
\hat{B}_{j\leftarrow l}^{(t,m)}
, which are independent copies of
B_{j\leftarrow l}\mathbbm{I}\{B_{j\leftarrow l}\leq M\}
;

2.

next, independently for any
(t,m)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
with
m\leq X^{\leqslant}_{i,l}(t-1;M)
and
\hat{B}_{j\leftarrow l}^{(t,m)}(M)=0

(that is, the
m^{\text{th}}
type-
l
node in the
(t-1)^{\text{th}}
generation of the branching process
(\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{i}(t;M))_{t\geq 0}
did not give birth to any type-
j
child in the
t^{\text{th}}
generation),
we sample
B^{(t,m)}_{j\leftarrow l}
under the conditional law

\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\cdot\ \big|\ B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(M,\infty)\big);

3.

lastly, we count the number of pairs
(t,m)
in step
(2)
with
B^{(t,m)}_{j\leftarrow l}>M^{\prime}
.

In particular, by setting

\displaystyle Z_{M}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\#\Big\{(t,m)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}:\ m\leq X^{\leqslant}_{i,l}(t-1;M),\ \hat{B}^{(t,m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(M)=0\Big\},

the coupling described above and
(
4.94
) and imply that (for any
M,\delta
, and any
n
large enough with
n\delta>M
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{P}\big(\hat{N}^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M,n\delta)\geq 1\big)\geq\mathbf{P}\big(\hat{N}^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(M,n\delta)=1\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{P}\big(Z_{M}=k\big)\cdot\binom{k}{1}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(M,\infty)\big)}\cdot\Bigg(\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(M,n\delta]\big)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(M,\infty)\big)}\Bigg)^{k-1},

and hence

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)}{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{k\mathbf{P}(Z_{M}=k)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(M,\infty)\big)}\Bigg(\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(M,n\delta]\big)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(M,\infty)\big)}\Bigg)^{k-1}.

(4.95)

By the regularly varying conditions in Assumption
2
,
we have
\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>M)>0
for any
M>0
.
Also, we obviously have
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)=0
.
Consequently,
given
\rho\in(0,1)
and
\delta,M>0
,
in (
4.95
)
it holds for any
n
large enough that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)}{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{k\mathbf{P}(Z_{M}=k)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(M,\infty)\big)}\cdot\rho^{k-1}=\frac{\mathbf{E}\big[Z_{M}\rho^{Z_{M}-1}\big]}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(M,\infty)\big)}.

(4.96)

Note that
Z_{M}\rho^{Z_{M}-1}\leq Z_{M}
for any
\rho\in(0,1)
.
By monotone convergence, we get

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)}{\mathbf{P}(B_{l,j}>n\delta)}

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{\rho\uparrow 1}\frac{\mathbf{E}\big[Z_{M}\rho^{Z_{M}-1}\big]}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(M,\infty)\big)}=\frac{\mathbf{E}Z_{M}}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(M,\infty)\big)}

(4.97)

for any
M,\delta>0
.
Moreover, by repeating the arguments in (
4.91
) based on Proposition 1 of
[
1
]
,
we get

\mathbf{E}Z_{M}=\mathbf{E}\big[S^{\leqslant}_{i,l}(M)\big]\cdot\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(M,\infty)\big).

Then, in (
4.97
), we have

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1)}{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)}\geq\mathbf{E}\big[S^{\leqslant}_{i,l}(M)\big]

for any
\delta,M>0
.
Lastly, we have established earlier that

\lim_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{E}[S^{\leqslant}_{i,l}(M)]=\bar{s}_{i,l}.

Sending
M\to\infty
, we verify (
4.92
).

(ii)

Since there are only finitely many possible choices for such
\mathcal{T}
,
it suffices to fix some
\mathcal{T}\subseteq[d]^{2}

with
|\mathcal{T}|\geq 2
and prove the claim.
For clarity of the proof, we focus on the case where
|\mathcal{T}|=2
.
That is, we fix some
(l,j)\neq(l^{\prime},j^{\prime})
and show that, for all
\delta>0
small enough,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1,\ N^{>}_{i;j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}(n\delta)\geq 1\Big)=\mathit{o}\Big(n\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}>n\delta)\Big)

as
n\to\infty
.
However, we stress that this approach can be easily applied to more general cases,
at the cost of more involved notations.
Also, since each
B_{j\leftarrow l}
is a non-negative integer-valued random variable,
the essential lower bound

\underline{b}_{j\leftarrow l}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\min\big\{k\geq 0:\ \mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}=k)>0\big\}

is well-defined for each pair
(l,j)
.
We first consider the case where
\underline{b}_{j\leftarrow l}=0
and
\underline{b}_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}=0
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}=0)>0,\qquad\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}=0)>0.

(4.98)

Towards the end of this proof, we address the cases where (
4.98
) does not hold.

Let

\displaystyle Z_{n}(\delta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\#\Big\{(t,m)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}:\ t\geq 1,\ m\leq X^{\leqslant}_{i,l}(t-1;n\delta),\ B^{(t,m)}_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(n\delta,\infty)\Big\},

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}_{n}(\delta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\#\Big\{(t,m)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}:\ t\geq 1,\ m\leq X^{\leqslant}_{i,l^{\prime}}(t-1;n\delta),\ B^{(t,m)}_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}\in\{0\}\cup(n\delta,\infty)\Big\}.

Take
\Delta>0
.
Using the coupling constructed in the proof of Claim (
4.90
) in part
(i)
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1,\ N^{>}_{i;j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)

(4.99)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}\sum_{k^{\prime}\geq 1}\sum_{s\geq 1}\mathbf{P}\Big(Z_{n}(\delta)=k,\ Z^{\prime}_{n}(\delta)=k^{\prime},\ \left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert=s\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{p=1}^{k}\binom{k}{p}\cdot\Bigg(\frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(n\delta,\infty)\big)}\Bigg)^{p}\cdot\Bigg(\frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}=0)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(n\delta,\infty)\big)}\Bigg)^{k-p}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{p^{\prime}=1}^{k^{\prime}}\binom{k^{\prime}}{p^{\prime}}\cdot\Bigg(\frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}>n\delta)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}\in\{0\}\cup(n\delta,\infty)\big)}\Bigg)^{p^{\prime}}\cdot\Bigg(\frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}=0)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}\in\{0\}\cup(n\delta,\infty)\big)}\Bigg)^{k^{\prime}-p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>\lfloor n\Delta\rfloor\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}\sum_{k^{\prime}\geq 1}\sum_{s\leq\lfloor n\Delta\rfloor}\mathbf{P}\Big(Z_{n}(\delta)=k,\ Z^{\prime}_{n}(\delta)=k^{\prime},\ \left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert=s\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\text{Binomial}\bigg(k,\ \frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(n\delta,\infty)\big)}\bigg)\geq 1\Bigg)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\text{Binomial}\bigg(k^{\prime},\ \frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}>n\delta)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}\in\{0\}\cup(n\delta,\infty)\big)}\bigg)\geq 1\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>\lfloor n\Delta\rfloor\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}\sum_{k^{\prime}\geq 1}\sum_{s\leq\lfloor n\Delta\rfloor}\mathbf{P}\Big(Z_{n}(\delta)=k,\ Z^{\prime}_{n}(\delta)=k^{\prime},\ \left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert=s\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot k\cdot\frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(n\delta,\infty)\big)}\cdot k^{\prime}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}>n\delta)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}\in\{0\}\cup(n\delta,\infty)\big)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\text{ by the preliminary bound }\mathbf{P}\big(\text{Binomial}(k,p)\geq 1\big)\leq\mathbf{E}\big[\text{Binomial}(k,p)\big]=kp

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Delta\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbf{E}\bigg[\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert^{2}\mathbbm{I}\Big\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert\leq n\Delta\Big\}\bigg]\cdot\prod_{(p,q)=(l,j)\text{ or }(l^{\prime},j^{\prime})}\frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{q\leftarrow p}>n\delta)}{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{q\leftarrow p}\in\{0\}\cup(n\delta,\infty)\big)}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}I(n,\Delta,\delta)}.

The last inequality follows from
Z_{n}(\delta)\leq\left\lVert\bm{S}_{i}^{\leqslant}(n\delta)\right\rVert
and

Z_{n}^{\prime}(\delta)\leq\left\lVert\bm{S}_{i}^{\leqslant}(n\delta)\right\rVert
.
Applying Lemma
4.5
,
we fix some
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\Delta)>0
such that for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Delta\Big)=\mathit{o}\Big(n\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}>n\delta)\Big).

(4.100)

Meanwhile,
by our running assumption (
4.98
),
there exists
C\in(0,\infty)
such that for any
\delta>0
and any
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle I(n,\Delta,\delta)

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot\mathbf{E}\bigg[\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert^{2}\mathbbm{I}\Big\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert\leq n\Delta\Big\}\bigg]\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}>n\delta).

(4.101)

Let
\alpha^{*}=\min\{\alpha_{q\leftarrow p}:\ p,q\in[d]\}
.
Under Assumption
2
, we have
\alpha^{*}>1
.
Then, by Theorem 2 of
[
1
]
,
we get
\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{S}_{i}\right\rVert>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha^{*}}(x)
as
x\to\infty
.
Now, we consider two different cases.
If
\alpha^{*}>2
, then

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\bigg[\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert^{2}\mathbbm{I}\Big\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert\leq n\Delta\Big\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}[\left\lVert\bm{S}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}]<\infty\quad\text{due to \eqref{property: stochastic comparison, S and pruned S} and $\alpha^{*}>2$}.

Plugging this bound into (
4.101
), we verify that

\displaystyle I(n,\Delta,\delta)=\mathit{o}\Big(n\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}>n\delta)\Big)\qquad\text{when }\alpha^{*}>2.

(4.102)

If
\alpha^{*}\in(1,2]
,
we obtain

\mathbf{E}\Big[\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert^{2}\mathbbm{I}\Big\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert\leq n\Delta\Big\}\Big]\leq\int_{0}^{n\Delta}2x\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{S}_{i}\right\rVert>x)dx\in\mathcal{RV}_{2-\alpha^{*}}(n)

using (
4.3
) and Karamata’s Theorem
(see, e.g., Theorem 2.1 of
[
71
]
).
Due to
\alpha^{*}>1
,
any
\mathcal{RV}_{2-\alpha^{*}}(n)
function is of order
\mathit{o}(n)
.
Plugging this into (
4.101
), we get

\displaystyle I(n,\Delta,\delta)=\mathit{o}\Big(n\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}>n\delta)\Big)\qquad\text{when }\alpha^{*}\in(1,2].

(4.103)

Plugging (
4.100
), (
4.102
), and (
4.103
) into (
4.99
), we conclude the proof of part
(ii)
under condition (
4.98
).

Lastly, we explain how to extend the proof to the cases where the condition (
4.98
) does not hold.
Recall the definition of the essential lower bounds

\underline{b}_{j\leftarrow l}=\min\big\{k\geq 0:\ \mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}=k)>0\big\},

and consider the following branching process

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{X}}_{i}(t;M)=\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{\tilde{X}_{i,j}(t-1;M)}\tilde{\bm{B}}^{(t,m)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}(M),\qquad\forall t\geq 1,

under initial values
\tilde{\bm{X}}_{i}(0)=\bm{e}_{i}
, where

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{l\leftarrow j}^{(t,m)}=\underline{b}_{l\leftarrow j}\vee\big(B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow j}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow j}\leq M\}\big),\quad\tilde{\bm{B}}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}^{(t,m)}=(\tilde{B}_{1\leftarrow j}^{(t,m)},\ldots,\tilde{B}_{d\leftarrow j}^{(t,m)})^{\top}.

That is,
\tilde{\bm{X}}_{i}(t;M)
modifies the process
\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{i}(t;M)
defined in (
3.19
) by pruning down to the essential lower bound of each
B_{l\leftarrow j}
instead of
0
.
Obviously,
\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{i}(t;n\delta)\leq\tilde{\bm{X}}_{i}(t;n\delta)
for each
t,n
.
Then, from the definition of
N_{i;j\leftarrow l}^{>}(n\delta)
in (
4.93
), we get

\displaystyle N_{i;j\leftarrow l}^{>}(n\delta)\leq\#\Big\{(t,m)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}:\ t\geq 1,\ m\leq\tilde{X}_{i,l}(t-1;n\delta),\ B^{(t,m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(M)>n\delta\Big\}.

Using the coupling constructed when proving Claim (
4.90
) in part
(i)
,
we arrive at upper bounds analogous to those in the display (
4.99
),
with the key difference being that the terms
\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{0\}\cup(n\delta,\infty))
and
\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}\in\{0\}\cup(n\delta,\infty))
in the denominators
are substituted by

\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}\in\{\underline{b}_{j\leftarrow l}\}\cup(n\delta,\infty))
and
\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}\in\{\underline{b}_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}\}\cup(n\delta,\infty))
.
In particular, by the definition of the essential lower bounds, we must have
\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}=\underline{b}_{j\leftarrow l})>0
and

\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}}=\underline{b}_{j^{\prime}\leftarrow l^{\prime}})>0
,
so an upper bound of the form (
4.101
) would still hold, and the subsequent calculations would follow.
We omit the details here to avoid repetition.
∎

Utilizing Lemma
4.14
,
we provide the proof of Lemma
4.7
.

Proof of Lemma
4.7
.

(i)
By the definition of
N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)=\sum_{l\in[d]}N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)
in (
3.24
),
we have

\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)=1\big)\leq\sum_{l\in[d]}\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)=1\big).

By (
4.87
) in part (i) of Lemma
4.14
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)=1\big)\sim\bar{s}_{i,l}\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha_{j\leftarrow l}}(n),\qquad\forall l\in[d],

(4.104)

where
\bar{s}_{i,l}>0\ \forall l\in[d]
; see Assumptions
2
and
3
.
Also,
under Assumption
4
,
the argument minimum
l^{*}(j)
in (
3.3
) is uniquely defined for each
j\in[d]
, and we have

\alpha^{*}(j)=\alpha_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)},

\alpha^{*}(j)<\alpha_{j\leftarrow l}
for any
l\neq l^{*}(j)
.
This leads to

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}{\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)=1\big)}\Big/{\Big(\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\Big)}\leq 1.

(4.105)

On the other hand, observe the lower bound

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)=1\big)\geq\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}(n\delta)=1,\ N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)=0\ \forall l\neq l^{*}(j)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\underbrace{\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}(n\delta)=1\big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}p_{1}(n)}-\underbrace{\sum_{l\in[d]:\ l\neq l^{*}(j)}\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}(n\delta)=1,\ N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}p_{2}(n)}.

For the term
p_{1}(n)
,
it follows from
(
4.104
) that

p_{1}(n)\sim\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)

as
n\to\infty
.
As for the term
p_{2}(n)
,
we apply part (ii) of Lemma
4.14
and get
(for any
\delta>0
small enough)

\displaystyle p_{2}(n)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in[d]:\ l\neq l^{*}(j)}\mathit{o}\Big(n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>n\delta)\Big)=\mathit{o}\Big(\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\Big).

(4.106)

The last equality follows from
\alpha_{j\leftarrow l}>1\ \forall l\in[d]
; see Assumption
2
.
In summary, we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}{\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)=1\big)}\Big/{\Big(\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\Big)}\geq 1.

(4.107)

Combining (
4.105
) and (
4.107
),
we conclude the proof of Claim (
4.39
).
Next, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)\geq 2\big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l\in[d]}\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 2\big)

(4.108)

\displaystyle+\sum_{l,l^{\prime}\in[d]:\ l\neq l^{\prime}}\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1,\ N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l^{\prime}}(n\delta)\geq 1\big).

Claim (
4.40
) then follows from part (i), Claim (
4.88
) and part (ii) of Lemma
4.14
.

To prove Claims (
4.41
) and (
4.42
),
we
define the event

A(n,\delta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}(n\delta)=1;\ N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)=0\ \forall l\neq l^{*}(j)\big\}.

By (
3.21
)–(
3.24
),
the law of
W^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)
conditioned on the event
A(n,\delta)

is the same as

\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}\in\ \cdot\ \big|B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta\big).

As a result,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(W^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)>nx\ \Big|\ A(n,\delta)\Big)=\frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>nx)}{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)},\qquad\forall x\geq\delta.

(4.109)

Next, given
\delta\in(0,c)
and
x\geq\delta
,
by conditioning on
A(n,\delta)
or
\big(A(n,\delta)\big)^{c}
,
we get

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in[c,C]}\Bigg|\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(W^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)>nx\ \big|\ N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)}{({\delta}/{x})^{\alpha^{*}(j)}}-1\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{x\in[c,C]}\Bigg|\frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>nx)\big/\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)}{({\delta}/{x})^{\alpha^{*}(j)}}\cdot\mathbf{P}\big(A(n,\delta)\ \big|\ N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)-1\Bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+\lim_{n\to\infty}\bigg(\frac{C}{\delta}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(A(n,\delta)\big)^{c}\ \Big|\ N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)\geq 1\Big)\qquad\text{due to \eqref{proof: equality for conditional law of W, part i, lemma: cluster size, asymptotics, N i | n delta, cdot j, refined estimate}}.

Suppose that
Claim (
4.41
) holds for any
\delta>0
small enough:
that is,
\mathbf{P}\big(A(n,\delta)\big|N_{i;j}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)\to 1
as
n\to\infty
.
Then,
by applying uniform convergence theorem (e.g., Proposition 2.4 of
[
71
]
)
to
\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha^{*}(j)}(x)

in the display above,
we verify Claim (
4.42
) for any
\delta>0
.
Now, it only remains to prove Claim (
4.41
).
In particular,
note that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)

\displaystyle\geq\underbrace{\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}(n\delta)=1;\ N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)=0\ \forall l\neq l^{*}(j)\big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\underline{p}(n,\delta)},

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\bar{p}_{*}(n,\delta)}+\sum_{l\in[d]:\ l\neq l^{*}(j)}\underbrace{\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\geq 1\big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\bar{p}_{l}(n,\delta)}.

Repeating the calculations in (
4.105
)–(
4.108
),
we can show that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\underline{p}(n,\delta)\big/\bar{p}_{*}(n,\delta)=1

and

\lim_{n\to\infty}\bar{p}_{l}(n,\delta)/\bar{p}_{*}(n,\delta)=0

(for each
l\in[d],\ l\neq l^{*}(j)
)
under any
\delta>0
small enough.
This concludes the proof of Claim (
4.41
).

(ii)
By the definition of
N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)=\sum_{l\in[d]}N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)
,

\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)\geq 1\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}\big)\leq\sum_{l_{j}\in[d]\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l_{j}}(n\delta)\geq 1\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}\big).

Applying part (ii) of Lemma
4.14
,
for each
(l_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\in[d]^{|\mathcal{J}|}

we have

\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j\leftarrow l_{j}}(n\delta)\geq 1\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}\big)=\mathit{o}\bigg(n^{|\mathcal{J}|-1}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l_{j}}>n\delta)\bigg)\text{ as }n\to\infty,

under any
\delta>0
small enough.
Lastly, by Assumption
4
and the definitions in (
3.3
),
we have

\mathit{o}\big(n^{|\mathcal{J}|-1}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l_{j}}>n\delta)\big)=\mathit{o}\big(n^{|\mathcal{J}|-1}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\big).

This establishes part (ii).

(iii)
Note that it suffices to prove the claim for the case of

|\mathcal{I}|=1
, i.e.,
\mathcal{I}=\{i\}
for some
i\in[d]
.
Specifically,
let
\delta_{0}>0
be characterized as in part (ii).
It suffices to show that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{T\geq nc}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\sum_{m=1}^{T}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta T)\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\big)}{\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)}<\infty,\quad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}),\ c>0.

(4.110)

To see why (
4.110
) implies (
4.43
),
we use
\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}

to denote the set of all assignment from
\mathcal{J}
to
\mathcal{I}
,
allowing for replacements
:
that is,

\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}

contains all
\{\mathcal{J}(i)\subseteq\mathcal{J}:\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}

satisfying
\bigcup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathcal{J}(i)=\mathcal{J}
.
Observe that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big({N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\Big)=\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\Bigg)\quad\text{by \eqref{def, proof cluster size, N W mathcal I mathcal J bcdot j}}

(4.111)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\Bigg).

The last equality follows from the independence of the random vectors

\big\{\big(N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(M)\big)_{j\in[d]}:\ m\geq 1\big\}

across
i\in[d]
; see (
4.36
).
Applying (
4.110
) to each term

\mathbf{P}\big(\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\big)

in (
4.111
),
we verify Claim (
4.43
) for any
|\mathcal{I}|\geq 2
.

Now, it only remains to prove (
4.110
) (i.e., part (iii) with
\mathcal{I}=\{i\}
).
To proceed,
we say that
\mathscr{J}=\{\mathcal{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{J}_{k}\}
is a partition of
\mathcal{J}
if:
(i)
\emptyset\neq\mathcal{J}_{l}\subseteq\mathcal{J}
for each
l\in[k]
, and

\cup_{l\in[k]}\mathcal{J}_{l}=\mathcal{J}
; (ii)

\mathcal{J}_{p}\cap\mathcal{J}_{q}=
for any
p\neq q
(that is,
\mathcal{J}_{l}
’s are disjoint).
Let
{\mathbb{J}}
be the set of all partitions of
\mathcal{J}
,
and note that
|\mathbb{J}|<\infty
.
Given partition
\mathscr{J}=\{\mathcal{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{J}_{k}\}

and some

T\in\mathbb{N}
,
define the event

\displaystyle A^{\mathscr{J}}_{n}(T,\delta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Big\{\exists\{m_{1},\cdots,m_{k}\}\subseteq[T]\text{ such that }N^{>,(m_{l})}_{i;j}(\delta T)\geq 1\ \forall l\in[k],\ j\in\mathcal{J}_{l}\Big\}.

(4.112)

First, note that
for any
T\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\sum_{m=1}^{T}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta T)\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\Bigg\}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathscr{J}\in\mathbb{J}}A_{n}^{\mathscr{J}}(T,\delta).

(4.113)

Next, given
T\in\mathbb{N}
and some partition
\mathscr{J}=\{\mathcal{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{J}_{k}\}
, note that
(in the display below we write
p(i,M,\mathcal{T})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j}(M)\geq 1\ \forall j\in\mathcal{T}\big)
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(A_{n}^{\mathscr{J}}(T,\delta)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{l\in[k]}\mathbf{P}\Big(\text{Binomial}\big(T,p(i,\delta T,\mathcal{J}_{l})\big)\geq 1\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{l\in[k]}\mathbf{E}\Big[\text{Binomial}\big(T,p(i,\delta T,\mathcal{J}_{l})\big)\Big]=\prod_{l\in[k]}T\cdot p(i,\delta T,\mathcal{J}_{l}).

(4.114)

Furthermore,
by applying either part (i), Claims (
4.39
)–(
4.40
) (if
|\mathcal{J}_{l}|=1
) or part (ii) (if
|\mathcal{J}_{l}|\geq 2
)
of Lemma
4.7

for each
l\in[k]
,
we identify some
\delta_{0}>0
such that, given any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,
there exists
\bar{n}=\bar{n}(\delta)\in(0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle p(i,n\delta,\mathcal{J}_{l})\leq\underbrace{\max_{q,q^{\prime}\in[d]}2\bar{s}_{q,q^{\prime}}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\bar{C}}\cdot n^{|\mathcal{J}_{l}|-1}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{l}}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta),\quad\forall n\geq\bar{n},\ l\in[k].

(4.115)

Recall that
c>0
is the constant fixed in (
4.110
).
Given
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
and any
n
with
nc>\bar{n}(\delta)
,
by (
4.114
) and (
4.115
),
it holds for each
T\geq nc
that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(A_{n}^{\mathscr{J}}(T,\delta)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\bar{C}^{k}\prod_{l\in[k]}T^{|\mathcal{J}_{l}|}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{l}}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>T\delta)

\displaystyle=\bar{C}^{k}T^{|\mathcal{J}|}\prod_{l\in[k]}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}_{l}}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>T\delta)=\bar{C}^{k}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}T\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>T\delta).

(4.116)

The last line follows from
the definition of the partition

\mathscr{J}=\{\mathcal{J}_{1},\ldots\mathcal{J}_{k}\}
.
Furthermore, for each
j\in[d]
,
note that

f_{j,\delta}(x)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}x\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>x\delta)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-(\alpha^{*}(j)-1)}(x)

with
\alpha^{*}(j)>1
.
Using Potter’s bound, we have (by picking a larger
\bar{n}=\bar{n}(\delta)
if necessary)

f_{j,\delta}(y)\leq 2f_{j,\delta}(x)

for any
y\geq x\geq\bar{n}(\delta),\ j\in[d].

Then, in (
4.116
),
it holds for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
and any
n
with
nc\geq\bar{n}(\delta)
that

\sup_{T\geq nc}\mathbf{P}\big(A_{n}^{\mathscr{J}}(T,\delta)\big)=\mathcal{O}\big(\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}n\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\big).

Applying this bound for any partition
\mathscr{J}\in\mathbb{J}

in (
4.113
),
we conclude the proof of Claim (
4.110
).
∎

Appendix A
Additional Auxiliary Results

For completeness, we collect in this section the proofs of several useful results.
The first lemma provides concentration inequalities for truncated regularly varying random vectors,
and the proof is similar to that of Lemma 3.1 in
[
78
]
.
Recall that, throughout this paper, we consider the
L_{1}
norm
\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert=\sum_{i=1}^{k}|x_{i}|
for any vector
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
.
For any
c>0
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
, let
\phi_{c}(x)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}x\wedge c
,
and

\psi_{c}(x)=(x\wedge c)\vee(-c)
.
That is,
\psi_{c}
is the projection mapping onto the interval
[-c,c]
,
and
\phi_{c}(x)
truncates
x
under threshold
c
.
For any
\bm{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, let

\displaystyle\phi^{(k)}_{c}(\bm{x})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big(\phi_{c}(x_{1}),\ldots,\phi_{c}(x_{k})\big),\quad\psi^{(k)}_{c}(\bm{x})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big(\psi_{c}(x_{1}),\ldots,\psi_{c}(x_{k})\big).

Under any
c>0
, note that

\displaystyle\psi^{(k)}_{c}(\bm{x})=\phi^{(k)}_{c}(\bm{x}),\qquad\forall\bm{x}\in[0,\infty)^{k}.

(A.1)

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\bm{Z}_{i}
’s be independent copies of a random vector
\bm{Z}
in
\mathbb{R}^{k}
.
Suppose that

\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{Z}\right\rVert>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}(x)
as
x\to\infty
for some
\alpha>1
.
Given any
\epsilon,\ \gamma\in(0,\infty)
, there exists
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\epsilon,\gamma)>0
such that
for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\max_{t\leq n}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\bm{Z}_{i}\mathbbm{I}\{\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert\leq n\delta\}-\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}\right\rVert>\epsilon\Bigg)

\displaystyle=0,

(A.2)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\max_{t\leq n}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\psi^{(k)}_{n\delta}(\bm{Z}_{i})-\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}\right\rVert>\epsilon\Bigg)

\displaystyle=0.

(A.3)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we take
\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}=\bm{0}
.
Also, the proof of Claim (
A.2
)
is a rather straightforward adaptation of the proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
78
]
,
and is almost identical to the proof of Claim (
A.3
) given below.
To avoid repetition, in this proof we focus on establishing Claim (
A.3
).

Take
\beta
such that
\frac{1}{2\wedge\alpha}<\beta<1
. Let

\displaystyle\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\psi^{(k)}_{n\delta}(\bm{Z}_{i})\mathbbm{I}\{\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert\leq n^{\beta}\},\quad\hat{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}-\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i},\quad\bm{Z}^{(2)}_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\psi^{(k)}_{n\delta}(\bm{Z}_{i})\mathbbm{I}\{\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert>n^{\beta}\}.

Due to

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\psi^{(k)}_{n\delta}(\bm{Z}_{i})\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\hat{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\bm{Z}}^{(2)}_{i}\right\rVert+\frac{t}{n}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert,

it suffices to find
\delta_{0}>0
such that for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert

\displaystyle<\frac{\epsilon}{3},

(A.4)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\max_{t\leq n}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\hat{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert>\frac{\epsilon}{3}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=0,

(A.5)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\max_{t\leq n}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\bm{Z}}^{(2)}_{i}\right\rVert>\frac{\epsilon}{3}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=0.

(A.6)

We show that Claim (
A.4
) holds for any
\delta>0
.
To this end, we make a few observations.
First, given
\delta>0
, it holds for any
n
large enough such that
n\delta>n^{\beta}
due to our choice of
\beta<1
.
For such
n
, note that any vector
\bm{x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})
,
\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert\leq n^{\beta}
implies that

|x_{j}|\leq n^{\beta}<n\delta

for each
j\in[k]
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}=\psi^{(k)}_{n\delta}(\bm{Z}_{i})\mathbbm{I}\{\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert\leq n^{\beta}\}=\bm{Z}_{i}\mathbbm{I}\{\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert\leq n^{\beta}\},\quad\text{whenever }n^{\beta}<n\delta.

(A.7)

Then, for such large
n
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\big[\bm{Z}_{i}\mathbbm{I}\{\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert\leq n^{\beta}\}\big]\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\big[\bm{Z}_{i}\mathbbm{I}\{\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert>n^{\beta}\}\big]\right\rVert\leq\mathbf{E}\Big[\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert\mathbbm{I}\{\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert>n^{\beta}\}\Big]\quad\text{due to }\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}=\bm{0}

\displaystyle=\int^{\infty}_{n^{\beta}}\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert>x)dx+n^{\beta}\cdot\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert>n^{\beta})\in\mathcal{RV}_{-(\alpha-1)\beta}(n).

(A.8)

The last inequality follows from
\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{Z}\right\rVert>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}(x)
and Karamata’s Theorem.
Due to
\alpha>1
, we have
(\alpha-1)\beta>0
in (
A.8
), which verifies Claim (
A.4
).
Also, by (
A.7
),
under any
n
sufficiently large
we must have
\left\lVert\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert<n^{\beta}
.
As a result, given
\delta>0
,
it holds for all
n
large enough that
\left\lVert\hat{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert<2n^{\beta}
.
Henceforth in this proof, we only consider such large
n
.

Next, we show that Claim (
A.5
) holds for any
\delta>0
.
Fix some
p
such that

\displaystyle p\geq 1,\quad\ p>\frac{2\gamma}{\beta},\quad\ p>\frac{2\gamma}{1-\beta},\quad\ p>\frac{2\gamma}{(\alpha-1)\beta}>\frac{2\gamma}{(2\alpha-1)\beta}.

(A.9)

We write
\hat{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}_{i}=(\hat{Z}^{(1)}_{i,j})_{j\in[k]}
,
and note that
under
L_{1}
norm, we have

\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert=\sum_{j=1}^{k}|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{Z}^{(1)}_{i,j}|.

Furthermore, for each
j\in[k]
,
n\geq 1
, and
y\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{n}\hat{Z}^{(1)}_{i,j}\bigg|^{p}>\frac{y}{n^{2\gamma}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{n}\hat{Z}^{(1)}_{i,j}\bigg|>\frac{y^{1/p}}{n^{2\gamma/p}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\Bigg(-\frac{\frac{1}{2}y^{2/p}\cdot n^{-4\gamma/p}}{\frac{2}{3}{y^{1/p}}\cdot{n^{-(1-\beta+2\gamma/p)}}+n\cdot\frac{1}{n^{2}}\cdot\mathbf{E}\big[|\hat{Z}^{(1)}_{i,j}|^{2}\big]}\Bigg)\quad\text{by Bernstein's inequality and }\left\lVert\hat{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert<2n^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\Bigg(-\frac{\frac{1}{2}y^{2/p}\cdot n^{-4\gamma/p}}{\frac{2}{3}{y^{1/p}}\cdot{n^{-(1-\beta+2\gamma/p)}}+\frac{1}{n}\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\left\lVert\hat{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\Big]}\Bigg)\qquad\text{due to }|\hat{Z}^{(1)}_{i,j}|\leq\left\lVert\hat{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert.

(A.10)

Our next goal is to show that

\frac{1}{n}\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\left\lVert\hat{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\Big]<\frac{1}{3}{n^{-(1-\beta+2\gamma/p)}}

for all
n
large enough.
First, due to
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\Big[\left\lVert\hat{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\Big[\left\lVert\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}-\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\Big]\leq 2\mathbf{E}\Big[\left\lVert\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\Big]+2\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}.

Also, as established in (
A.8
),

\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}
is upper bounded by some
\mathcal{RV}_{-2(\alpha-1)\beta}(n)
function.
By the choice of
p
in (
A.9
) that
p>\frac{2\gamma}{(2\alpha-1)\beta}
,
we have

1+2(\alpha-1)\beta>1-\beta+\frac{2\gamma}{p},

and hence

\frac{2}{n}\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}<\frac{1}{6}n^{-(1-\beta+\frac{2\gamma}{p})},\qquad\text{ for any $n$ large enough}.

Next, using (
A.7
),
for any
n
large enough we have

\mathbf{E}\Big[\left\lVert\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\Big]=\int^{\infty}_{0}2x\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert>x\Big)dx\leq\int^{n^{\beta}}_{0}2x\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert>x)dx.

If
\alpha\in(1,2]
, Karamata’s theorem gives

\int^{n^{\beta}}_{0}2x\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert>x)dx\in\mathcal{RV}_{(2-\alpha)\beta}(n)
.
In (
A.9
),
we have chosen
p
large enough such that

p>\frac{2\gamma}{(\alpha-1)\beta},

and hence

1-(2-\alpha)\beta>1-\beta+\frac{2\gamma}{p}
.
As a result,
for all
n
large enough
we have

\frac{2}{n}\mathbf{E}\Big[\left\lVert\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\Big]<\frac{1}{6}n^{-(1-\beta+\frac{2\gamma}{p})}
.
If
\alpha>2
, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int^{n^{\beta}}_{0}2x\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert>x)dx=\int^{\infty}_{0}2x\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert>x)dx<\infty
.
Also, (
A.9
) implies that
1-\beta+\frac{2\gamma}{p}<1
.
Again, for any
n
large enough
we have

\frac{2}{n}\mathbf{E}\Big[\left\lVert\bm{Z}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\Big]<\frac{1}{6}n^{-(1-\beta+\frac{2\gamma}{p})}
.
In summary, we have shown that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\cdot\mathbf{E}\Big[\left\lVert\hat{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert^{2}\Big]<\frac{1}{3}{n^{-(1-\beta+2\gamma/p)}},\qquad\text{ for all $n$ large enough}.

(A.11)

Along with (
A.10
),
we obtain that for all
n
large enough,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{n}\hat{Z}^{(1)}_{i,j}\bigg|^{p}>\frac{y}{n^{2\gamma}}\Bigg)\leq 2\exp\Bigg({-\frac{1}{2}{y^{1/p}}}\cdot{n^{1-\beta-\frac{2\gamma}{p}}}\Bigg)\leq 2\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}{y^{1/p}}\Big),\qquad\forall y\geq 1,\ j\in[k].

Here, the last inequality follows from our choice of
p
in (
A.9
)
with
p>\frac{2\gamma}{1-\beta}
,
and hence
1-\beta-\frac{2\gamma}{p}>0
.
Moreover, since
C^{(1)}_{p}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(-\frac{1}{2}{y^{1/p}})dy<\infty
,
the display above implies

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[k]}n^{2\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{n}\hat{Z}^{(1)}_{i,j}\bigg|^{p}\Bigg]<C^{(1)}_{p}<\infty,\qquad\text{ for all $n$ large enough}.

Therefore, for such large
n
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\max_{t\leq n}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\hat{\bm{Z}}^{(1)}_{i}\right\rVert>\frac{\epsilon}{3}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[k]}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\max_{t\leq n}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{n}\hat{Z}^{(1)}_{i,j}\bigg|>\frac{\epsilon}{3k}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[k]}\frac{\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{n}\hat{Z}^{(1)}_{i,j}\bigg|^{p}\Bigg]}{(\epsilon/3k)^{p}}\qquad\text{by Doob's Inequality}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k}{(\epsilon/3k)^{p}}C^{(1)}_{p}\cdot\frac{1}{n^{2\gamma}}.

This concludes the proof of Claim (
A.5
) (under any
\delta>0
).

Finally, for Claim (
A.6
), recall that we have chosen
\beta
in such a way that
\alpha\beta-1>0
.
Fix a constant
J=\lceil\frac{\gamma}{\alpha\beta-1}\rceil+1
,
and define

I(n)=\#\big\{i\leq n:\ \bm{Z}^{(2)}_{i}\neq\bm{0}\big\}
.
Besides, fix
\delta_{0}=\frac{\epsilon}{3Jk}
.
For any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,
by the definition of the projection mapping
\psi_{c}^{(k)}
,
we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{Z}^{(2)}_{i}\right\rVert\leq nk\delta<n\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{3J}.

Then, for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,
on the event
\{I(n)<J\}
,
we have

\max_{t\leq n}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\bm{Z}}^{(2)}_{i}\right\rVert<\frac{1}{n}\cdot J\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{3J}<\epsilon/3
.
On the other hand, (let
H(x)=\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{Z}\right\rVert>x)
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(I(n)\geq J\big)\leq\binom{n}{J}\cdot\Big(H(n^{\beta})\Big)^{J}\leq n^{J}\cdot\Big(H(n^{\beta})\Big)^{J}\in\mathcal{RV}_{-J(\alpha\beta-1)}(n)\text{ as }n\to\infty.

Our choice of
J=\lceil\frac{\gamma}{\alpha\beta-1}\rceil+1
guarantees that
J(\alpha\beta-1)>\gamma
,
and hence,

\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\max_{t\leq n}\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\bm{Z}}^{(2)}_{i}\right\rVert>\frac{\epsilon}{3}\Bigg)\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}{n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}(I(n)\geq J)}=0.

This concludes the proof.
∎

The next lemma verifies equality (
1.3
) regarding
\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{j}(M)
and
\bm{S}_{j}
.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
W^{>}_{j;i}(M)
be defined as in (
3.21
)–(
3.22
)
on the probability space supporting the collection of independent random vectors
\bm{B}^{(t,m)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}
in (
3.16
),
and let
\bm{S}_{j}^{\leqslant}(M)
be defined as in (
3.20
).
Under Assumption
1
,
it holds for each
j\in[d]
and
M>0
that

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{j}(M)+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{W^{>}_{j;i}(M)}\bm{S}^{(m)}_{i},

where, for each
i\in[d]
, the
\bm{S}^{(m)}_{i}
’s are i.i.d. copies of
\bm{S}_{i}
and are independent from the random vector
\big(\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{j}(M),W^{>}_{j;1}(M),\ldots,W^{>}_{j;d}(M)\big)
.

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we fix some
M\in(0,\infty)
,
and lighten the notations by writing

\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{j}=\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{j}(M),

W^{>}_{j;i}=W^{>}_{j;i}(M),

W^{>}_{j;i\leftarrow l}=W^{>}_{j;i\leftarrow l}(M),

and
\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(t)=\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(t;M)
,

X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(t)=X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(t;M);

see (
3.19
) and (
3.21
)–(
3.22
).
Besides, henceforth in this proof,
notations

B^{(t,m,k)}_{l\leftarrow i}

are saved for i.i.d. copies of
B_{l\leftarrow i}

that are also independent from the
B^{(t,m)}_{i\leftarrow j}
’s,
and notations

\bm{S}^{(t,m,k)}_{i}
,
\tilde{\bm{S}}_{i}^{(t,m,k)}
, and
\bm{S}_{i}^{(t,m,k,k^{\prime})}
,
\tilde{\bm{S}}_{i}^{(t,m,k,k^{\prime})}
are for i.i.d. copies of
\bm{S}_{i}
whose law is independent from that of the
B^{(t,m)}_{i\leftarrow j}
’s and
B^{(t,m,k)}_{i\leftarrow j}
’s.
This is made rigorous through proper augmentation of the underlying probability space.
In particular, we note that:
(i)
the vector

\big(\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{j},W^{>}_{j;1},\ldots,W^{>}_{j;d}\big)

and the variables
X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(t)
are measurable w.r.t. the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the

B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}
’s in (
3.16
);
and
(ii)
since the

B^{(t,m,k)}_{l\leftarrow i}
’s
and

\bm{S}^{(t,m,k)}_{i}
,
\tilde{\bm{S}}_{i}^{(t,m,k)}
,
\bm{S}_{i}^{(t,m,k,k^{\prime})}
,
\tilde{\bm{S}}_{i}^{(t,m,k,k^{\prime})}

are independent from the
B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}
’s,
they are
also independent from
the vector

\big(\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{j},W^{>}_{j;1},\ldots,W^{>}_{j;d}\big)

and
X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(t)
.

By (
1.1
),

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bm{e}_{j}+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{B^{(1,1)}_{i\leftarrow j}}\bm{S}_{i}^{(1,1,m)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bm{e}_{j}+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{B^{(1,1)}_{i\leftarrow j}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(1,1)}_{i\leftarrow j}\leq M\}}\tilde{\bm{S}}_{i}^{(1,1,m)}+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{B^{(1,1)}_{i\leftarrow j}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(1,1)}_{i\leftarrow j}>M\}}{\bm{S}}_{i}^{(1,1,m)}.

Furthermore, for each
T\geq 1
we define

\displaystyle I_{1}(T)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(t),

\displaystyle I_{2}(T)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(T-1)}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(T,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(T,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\leq M\}}\tilde{\bm{S}}_{l}^{(T,m,k)},

\displaystyle I_{3}(T)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(t-1)}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}>M\}}\bm{S}_{l}^{(t,m,k)}.

Due to
\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(0)=\bm{e}_{j}
,
we have

I_{1}(1)=\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(0)=\bm{e}_{j}
,

I_{2}(1)=\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(1,1)}_{i\leftarrow j}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(1,1)}_{i\leftarrow j}\leq M\}}\tilde{\bm{S}}_{i}^{(1,1,k)},

and

I_{3}(1)=\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(1,1)}_{i\leftarrow j}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(1,1)}_{i\leftarrow j}>M\}}{\bm{S}}_{i}^{(1,1,k)}.

This confirms that

\bm{S}_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}I_{1}(1)+I_{2}(1)+I_{3}(1).

Next, we consider an inductive argument, and
suppose that

\bm{S}_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}I_{1}(T)+I_{2}(T)+I_{3}(T)

for some positive integer
T
.
Then,
using (
1.1
) again,
we get

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}I_{1}(T)+I_{3}(T)+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(T-1)}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(T,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(T,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\leq M\}}\Bigg(\bm{e}_{l}+\sum_{l^{\prime}\in[d]}\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{B^{(T+1,m,k)}_{l^{\prime}\leftarrow l}}\tilde{\bm{S}}_{l^{\prime}}^{(T+1,m,k,k^{\prime})}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=I_{1}(T)+I_{3}(T)+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(T-1)}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(T,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(T,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\leq M\}}\bm{e}_{l}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(T-1)}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(T,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(T,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\leq M\}}\sum_{l^{\prime}\in[d]}\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{B^{(T+1,m,k)}_{l^{\prime}\leftarrow l}}\tilde{\bm{S}}_{l^{\prime}}^{(T+1,m,k,k^{\prime})}.

By (
3.19
),
we have

\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(T-1)}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(T,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(T,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\leq M\}}\bm{e}_{l}=\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(T),

Also,
in the display above,
note that:
(i) the
\tilde{\bm{S}}_{l^{\prime}}^{(T+1,m,k,k^{\prime})}
’s are independent from the
\bm{S}^{(t,m,k)}_{l}
’s
and the variables
X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(t)
and
B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}
;
(ii) the sequence
\big(B^{(T+1,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\big)_{l,i,m}
is independent from
I_{1}(T)
and
I_{3}(T)
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\bm{S}_{j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}I_{3}(T)+I_{1}(T)+\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(T)+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(T)}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(T+1,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(T+1,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\leq M\}}\tilde{\bm{S}}_{l}^{(T+1,m,k)}

\displaystyle=I_{3}(T)+I_{1}(T+1)+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(T)}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(T+1,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}}\tilde{\bm{S}}_{l}^{(T+1,m,k)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}I_{1}(T+1)+I_{3}(T)+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(T)}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(T+1,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(T+1,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}>M\}}{\bm{S}}_{l}^{(T+1,m,k)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(T)}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(T+1,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(T+1,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\leqslant M\}}\tilde{\bm{S}}_{l}^{(T+1,m,k)}

\displaystyle=I_{1}(T+1)+I_{3}(T+1)+I_{2}(T+1).

Proceeding inductively, we conclude that

\bm{S}_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}I_{1}(T)+I_{2}(T)+I_{3}(T)

hold for any
T\geq 1
.
Now, it suffices to show that

I_{2}(T)\Rightarrow\bm{0}

and

I_{1}(T)+I_{3}(T)\Rightarrow\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{j}+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{W^{>}_{j;i}}\bm{S}^{(m)}_{i}

as
T\to\infty

Proof of
I_{2}(T)\Rightarrow\bm{0}
.
We prove the claim in terms of convergence in probability. The sub-criticality condition in Assumption
1
implies
\bm{S}_{j}<\infty
almost surely.
Then, due to
\bm{X}_{j}^{\leqslant}(t)\leq\bm{X}_{j}(t)
for each
t
(see (
3.17
)–(
3.19
)),
almost surely we have
\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(T)=\bm{0}
eventually for any
T
large enough, and hence

\displaystyle\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\big(I_{2}(T)=\bm{0}\big)\geq\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(T-1)=\bm{0}\big)=1.

Proof of
I_{1}(T)+I_{3}(T)\Rightarrow\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{j}+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{W^{>}_{j;i}}\bm{S}^{(m)}_{i}.

Applying monotone convergence theorem along each of the
d
dimensions and by the definition in (
3.20
),
we get

\displaystyle I_{1}(T)+I_{3}(T)\rightarrow\bm{S}_{j}^{\leqslant}+\sum_{t\geq 1}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(t-1)}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}>M\}}\bm{S}_{l}^{(t,m,k)},\qquad\text{as }T\to\infty

almost surely.
By the definitions in (
3.21
)–(
3.22
),
it holds for each
l\in[d]
that

W^{>}_{j;l}=\sum_{t\geq 1}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(t-1)}B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\big\{B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}>M\big\}.

Then, since the
\bm{S}^{(t,m,k)}_{l}
’s are independent from the
B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}
’s
(and hence the
\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{j}
and
W^{>}_{j;i}
’s),
we get

\displaystyle I_{1}(T)+I_{3}(T)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bm{S}_{j}^{\leqslant}+\sum_{t\geq 1}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{j,i}(t-1)}\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(t,m)}_{l\leftarrow i}>M\}}\bm{S}_{l}^{(t,m,k)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bm{S}_{j}^{\leqslant}+\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{W^{>}_{j;l}}\bm{S}^{(m)}_{l}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Counterexample

This section presents an example to illustrate that in Theorem
3.2
,
it is not trivial to uplift
the condition of
A
being bounded away from
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\epsilon)
for some
\epsilon>0
(i.e.,
\mathbb{M}
-convergence under polar transform, as shown in Lemma
2.4
) to
A
being bounded away from
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})
(i.e.,
\mathbb{M}
-convergence under Cartesian coordinates).

Specifically, we assume
d=2
and
impose Assumptions
1
–
4
.
Also, for clarity of the presentation, we consider a strict power-law version of Assumption
2
:

\displaystyle\lim_{x\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow i}>x)\cdot x^{\alpha_{j\leftarrow i}}=c_{i,j}\in(0,\infty),\qquad\text{for each index pair $(i,j)$,}

(B.1)

and assume that

\alpha_{1\leftarrow 2}>\alpha_{1\leftarrow 1}>2
,

\alpha_{2\leftarrow 1}\wedge\alpha_{2\leftarrow 2}>2\alpha_{1\leftarrow 1}
,
and

\mathbf{P}(B_{2\leftarrow 1}=0)>0
.
By the definitions in (
3.3
), we have

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}(1)=\alpha_{1\leftarrow 1}>2,\quad\alpha^{*}(2)>2\alpha^{*}(1).

(B.2)

We are interested in the asymptotics of
\mathbf{P}(n^{-1}\bm{S}_{1}\in A)
,
where
A=A(1)
with

\displaystyle A(r)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{(x_{1},x_{2})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}:\ \exists w\geq 0\text{ s.t. }x_{1}=w\bar{s}_{1,1},\ |x_{2}-w\bar{s}_{1,2}|>r\big\},

(B.3)

with
\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}=(\bar{s}_{i,1},\bar{s}_{i,2})^{\top}=\mathbf{E}\bm{S}_{i}
.
That is, the set
A(r)
is the tube around the ray
\mathbb{R}^{2}(\{1\})=\{w\bar{\bm{s}}_{1}:\ w\geq 0\}
with a (vertical) radius
r
,
restricted in
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
.
We stress that this is almost equivalent to considering

\displaystyle\breve{A}(r)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\bigg\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}:\inf_{\bm{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}(\{1\})}\left\lVert\bm{x}-\bm{y}\right\rVert>r\bigg\},\quad r>0.

In particular,
given any
r>0
, one can find
r_{1},r_{2}>0
such that

A(r_{1})\subseteq\breve{A}(r)\subseteq A(r_{2}).

This will allow us to apply the subsequent analysis onto
\breve{A}(r)
.

For clarity, we focus on the case with
r=1
in (
B.3
) (i.e., with
A=A(1)
).
Under Assumption
1
, it is easy to verify that
\bar{\bm{s}}_{1}
and
\bar{\bm{s}}_{2}
are linearly independent.
By the definition in (
B.3
), we must have
A\cap\mathbb{R}^{2}(\{2\})\neq\emptyset
, where
\mathbb{R}^{2}(\{i\})=\{w\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}:\ w\geq 0\}
.
Also, by (
B.2
), we have
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\leqslant}(\{2\})=\mathbb{R}^{2}(\{1\})
, which is bounded away from
A
.
Therefore, suppose that the asymptotics (
3.13
) stated in Theorem
3.2
hold for sets bounded away from
\mathbb{R}^{d}(\bm{j})
(instead of
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon)
), then we are led to believe that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(n^{-1}\bm{S}_{1}\in A)\sim n^{-\alpha^{*}(2)},\quad\text{ as }n\to\infty.

(B.4)

However, our analysis below disproves (
B.4
), indicating that it is non-trivial to relax in Theorem
3.2
the condition that
A
needs to be bounded away from
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon)
for some
\epsilon>0
.

For the type-
1
ancestor of
\bm{S}_{1}
, we use
B_{j\leftarrow 1}
to denote the count of its type-
j
children.
Conditioned on the event

\displaystyle E\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{B_{1\leftarrow 1}\in(n^{2},2n^{2}];\ B_{2\leftarrow 1}=0\big\},

\bm{S}_{1}
admits the law of

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}+\sum_{k=1}^{B_{1\leftarrow 1}}\bm{S}^{(k)}_{1},

(B.5)

where the
\bm{S}^{(k)}_{1}
’s are i.i.d. copies of
\bm{S}_{1}
.
Furthermore,
let
\hat{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{x}-w\bar{\bm{s}}_{1}\right\rVert>2\ \forall w\geq 0\big\}
.
Obviously, for each
n\geq 1
we have

n^{-1}\left\lVert(1,0)^{\top}\right\rVert=1/n\leq 1
.
Then, on the event

\displaystyle E\cap\bigg\{n^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{B_{1\leftarrow 1}}\bm{S}^{(k)}_{1}\in\hat{A}\bigg\},

by (
B.5
) we must have

\displaystyle n^{-1}\Bigg((1,0)^{\top}+\sum_{k=1}^{B_{1\leftarrow 1}}\bm{S}^{(k)}_{1}\Bigg)\in\big\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{x}-w\bar{\bm{s}}_{1}\right\rVert>1\ \forall w\geq 0\big\},

and hence

n^{-1}\bm{S}_{1}\in A.

In summary,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(n^{-1}\bm{S}_{1}\in A)\geq\mathbf{P}\big(B_{1\leftarrow 1}\in(n^{2},2n^{2}];\ B_{2\leftarrow 1}=0\big)\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(n^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{B_{1\leftarrow 1}}\bm{S}^{(k)}_{1}\in\hat{A}\ \bigg|\ B_{1\leftarrow 1}\in(n^{2},2n^{2}]\Bigg).

(B.6)

To proceed, we make a few observations.
First,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(B_{1\leftarrow 1}\in(n^{2},2n^{2}];\ B_{2\leftarrow 1}=0\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\big(B_{1\leftarrow 1}\in(n^{2},2n^{2}]\big)\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{2\leftarrow 1}=0)\qquad\text{by Assumption~\ref{assumption: heavy tails in B i j}}

\displaystyle=c\cdot\mathbf{P}\big(B_{1\leftarrow 1}\in(n^{2},2n^{2}]\big)\qquad\text{for some $c>0$ due to }\mathbf{P}(B_{2\leftarrow 1}=0)>0,

which implies

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{2\alpha^{*}(1)}\cdot{\mathbf{P}\big(B_{1\leftarrow 1}\in(n^{2},2n^{2}];\ B_{2\leftarrow 1}=0\big)}

\displaystyle=c\cdot\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{2\alpha^{*}(1)}\cdot\mathbf{P}\big(B_{1\leftarrow 1}\in(n^{2},2n^{2}]\big)=c\cdot\Bigg(c_{1\leftarrow 1}-\frac{c_{1\leftarrow 1}}{2^{\alpha^{*}(1)}}\Bigg)>0\qquad\text{by \eqref{example, power law assumtpion, tail asymptotics without polar transform} and \eqref{example, index alpha *, tail asymptotics without polar transform}.}

Second, under the tail indices specified in (
B.2
),
Theorem 2 of
[
1
]
confirms that

\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{S}_{1}\right\rVert>n)=\mathcal{O}(n^{-\alpha^{*}(1)}).

Since
\alpha^{*}(1)>2
, we have
\mathbf{E}\left\lVert\bm{S}_{1}\right\rVert^{2}<\infty
, hence the covariance matrix for the random vector
\bm{S}_{1}
is a well-defined symmetric and positive semi-definite matrix, which we denote by
\bm{\Sigma}
.
Obviously, our heavy-tailed assumption (
B.1
) prevents the trivial case of
\bm{\Sigma}=\textbf{0}
.
Now,
let

\displaystyle A^{*}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{\bm{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}:\ \bm{y}+\bar{\bm{s}}_{1}\in\hat{A}\big\}.

Note that
A^{*}
is open and non-empty.
Furthermore,
we write
\bm{x}+E=\{\bm{x}+\bm{y}:\ \bm{y}\in E\}
for any set
E\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}
and vector
\bm{x}
, and note the following:
due to
\bm{x}+\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\subseteq\bm{y}+\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
for any
\bm{x}\leq\bm{y}
(i.e.,
x_{1}\leq y_{1}
and
x_{2}\leq y_{2}
),
we have
\bm{y}\in A^{*}\Longrightarrow\bm{y}+w\bar{\bm{s}}_{1}\in\hat{A}\ \forall w\geq 1
.
As a result,

\displaystyle\big\{B_{1\leftarrow 1}\in(n^{2},2n^{2}]\big\}\cap\bigg\{n^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor m\rfloor}\big(\bm{S}^{(k)}_{1}-\bar{\bm{s}}_{1}\big)\in A^{*}\ \forall m\in(n^{2},2n^{2}]\bigg\}\subseteq\bigg\{n^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{B_{1\leftarrow 1}}\bm{S}^{(k)}_{1}\in\hat{A}\bigg\}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(n^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{B_{1\leftarrow 1}}\bm{S}^{(k)}_{1}\in\hat{A}\ \bigg|\ B_{1\leftarrow 1}\in(n^{2},2n^{2}]\Bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(n^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor m\rfloor}\big(\bm{S}^{(k)}_{1}-\bar{\bm{s}}_{1}\big)\in A^{*}\ \forall m\in(n^{2},2n^{2}]\Bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{B}(t)\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\in A^{*}\ \forall t\in[1,2]\Big)\ \ \text{by multivariate Donsker's theorem; see, e.g., Theorem 4.3.5 of \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{whitt2002stochastic}{}{}]}}

\displaystyle>0\quad\text{since $\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\neq\textbf{0}$ and $A^{*}$ is non-empty and open}.

In summary, from (
B.6
) we get

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}n^{2\alpha^{*}(1)}\cdot\mathbf{P}(n^{-1}\bm{S}_{1}\in A)>0.

In light of the condition
2\alpha^{*}(1)<\alpha^{*}(2)
in (
B.2
),
we arrive at a contradiction to Claim (
B.4
).
This concludes the example and confirms that the asymptotics (
3.13
) in Theorem
3.2
generally fails when relaxing the bounded-away condition.

Appendix C
Proofs for
\mathbb{M}
-Convergence and Asymptotic Equivalence

This section collects the proof of Lemmas
4.2
and
2.4
.

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

Throughout this proof, we write
\mathbb{S}=[0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}
.
We arbitrarily pick some Borel measurable
B\subseteq\mathbb{S}
that is bounded away from
\mathbb{C}
under
\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}
.
This allows us to fix some
\bar{\epsilon}\in(0,1)

such that

\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}(B,\mathbb{C})>\bar{\epsilon}.

Let

\displaystyle B_{\theta}=\big\{\bm{w}\in\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}:\ (r,\bm{w})\in B\text{ for some }r>0\big\},\quad\mathbb{C}_{\theta}=\big\{\bm{w}\in\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}:\ (r,\bm{w})\in\mathbb{C}\text{ for some }r>0\big\}.

(C.1)

We must have

\displaystyle\inf_{\bm{w}\in B_{\theta},\ \bm{w}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}_{\theta}}\left\lVert\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\right\rVert\geq\bar{\epsilon}.

(C.2)

Otherwise, there exist
(r,\bm{w})\in B
and
(r^{\prime},\bm{w}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{C}
such that

r,r^{\prime}>0

yet

\left\lVert\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\right\rVert<\bar{\epsilon}.

By condition (
4.23
),
we also have
(r,\bm{w}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{C}
, and hence

\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}\big((r,\bm{w}),\ (r,\bm{w}^{\prime})\big)=\left\lVert\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\right\rVert<\bar{\epsilon},

which contradicts

\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}(B,\mathbb{C})>\bar{\epsilon}.

Also, since
(0,\bm{w})\in\mathbb{C}
for any
\bm{w}\in\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}
,
by

\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}(B,\mathbb{C})>\bar{\epsilon}

we have

\displaystyle(r,\bm{w})\in B\quad\Longrightarrow\quad r>\bar{\epsilon}.

(C.3)

For any
M\in(0,\infty)
, let

B(M)=\big\{(r,\bm{w})\in B:\ r\leq M\big\}.

For any
\Delta,M,n,\delta>0
, observe that

\displaystyle\Big\{(R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B\Big\}

\displaystyle\supseteq\Big\{(R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B(M)\Big\}

\displaystyle\supseteq\Big\{(R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B(M);\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\in\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big],\ \left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert\leq\Delta\Big\}.

Furthermore, for any
\bar{\Delta}>0
, and any
\Delta>0
,
M\geq 1
satisfying
M\Delta<\bar{\Delta}
,

\displaystyle{\hat{r}}/{r}\in[1-\Delta,1+\Delta],\ r\in[0,M],\ \left\lVert\bm{w}-\hat{\bm{w}}\right\rVert\leq\Delta\ \Longrightarrow\ |r-\hat{r}|\vee\left\lVert\bm{w}-\hat{\bm{w}}\right\rVert\leq M\Delta<\bar{\Delta}.

(C.4)

Therefore, for any
\bar{\Delta}\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})
, and any
\Delta\in(0,1)
,
M\geq 1
such that
M\Delta<\bar{\Delta}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B(M);\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\in\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big],\ \left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert\leq\Delta\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B(M),\ R_{n}\leq M/(1-\Delta);\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\in\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big],\ \left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert\leq\Delta\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{by the definition of }B(M)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B(M),\ R_{n}\leq M/(1-\Delta);\
