\displaystyle\qquad\quad\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\in\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big],\ \left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert\leq\Delta,\ \bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}\big((R_{n},\Theta_{n}),\ (\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\big)\leq\bar{\Delta}\Big)\quad\text{by \eqref{proof: result 1, lemma: asymptotic equivalence, MRV in Rd}}.

Also, recall that for any metric space
(\mathbb{S},\bm{d})
and
r>0
, we use

{E^{r}}=\{y\in\mathbb{S}:\ \bm{d}(E,y)\leq r\}
to denote the
r
-enlargement of the set
E
,
and

{E_{r}}=((E^{c})^{r})^{c}=\{y\in\mathbb{S}:\ \bm{d}(E^{c},y)>r\}

for the
r
-shrinkage
of
E
.
Given any
\bar{\Delta}\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})
, and any
\Delta\in(0,1)
,
M\geq 1
such that
M\Delta/(1-\Delta)<\bar{\Delta}
, we then have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{P}\Bigg((\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in\big(B(M)\big)_{\bar{\Delta}},\ R_{n}\leq\frac{M}{1-\Delta};

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\in\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big],\ \left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert\leq\Delta,\ \bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}\big((R_{n},\Theta_{n}),\ (\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\big)\leq\bar{\Delta}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Bigg((\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in\big(B(M)\big)_{\bar{\Delta}};\ R_{n}\leq\frac{M}{1-\Delta},\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\in\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big],\ \left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert\leq\Delta\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{by \eqref{proof: result 1, lemma: asymptotic equivalence, MRV in Rd} and }M\Delta/(1-\Delta)<\bar{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big((\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in\big(B(M)\big)_{\bar{\Delta}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbf{P}\Bigg((\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in\big(B(M)\big)_{\bar{\Delta}};\ R_{n}>\frac{M}{1-\Delta}\text{ or }\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\notin\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big]\text{ or }\left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert>\Delta\Bigg)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{\geq}}\mathbf{P}\Big((\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in\big(B(M)\big)_{\bar{\Delta}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\hat{R}_{n}^{\delta}\in(\Delta,M];\ R_{n}>\frac{M}{1-\Delta}\text{ or }\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\notin\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big]\text{ or }\left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert>\Delta\Bigg)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\dagger)}}{{=}}\mathbf{P}\Big((\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in\big(B(M)\big)_{\bar{\Delta}}\Big)-\mathbf{P}\Big(\hat{R}_{n}^{\delta}\in(\Delta,M];\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\notin\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big]\text{ or }\left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert>\Delta\Big).

Here, the step
(*)
follows from
\text{\eqref{proof, lower bound for r in B, lemma: asymptotic equivalence, MRV in Rd} and }\Delta<\bar{\epsilon}
,
and the step
(\dagger)
follows from

\big\{\hat{R}_{n}^{\delta}\in(\Delta,M],\ R_{n}>\frac{M}{1-\Delta}\big\}\subseteq\big\{\hat{R}_{n}^{\delta}\in(\Delta,M],\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\notin\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big]\big\}.

Then, by condition (i), for any
M\geq 1
and
\bar{\Delta}>0
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B\Big)\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\Big((\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in\big(B(M)\big)_{\bar{\Delta}}\Big),\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough}.

By condition (ii), given
M\geq 1
and
\bar{\Delta}>0
it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B\Big)\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\Big((\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in\big(B(M)\big)_{\bar{\Delta}}\Big)\geq-|\mathcal{V}|\bar{\Delta}+\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\mu_{v}\Big(\big(B(M)\big)_{2\bar{\Delta}}\Big).

(C.5)

Furthermore, note that

\bigcup_{M>0}B(M)=B

and
|\mathcal{V}|<\infty
.
By sending
M\to\infty
and then
\bar{\Delta}\to 0
, we get

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B\Big)\geq\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\mu_{v}(B^{\circ}).

(C.6)

Meanwhile, for any
\Delta\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})
, we have the upper bound

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B;\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\in\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big],\ \left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert\leq\Delta\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B;\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\notin\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big]\text{ or }\left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert>\Delta\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B;\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\in\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big],\ \left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert\leq\Delta\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbf{P}\Big(R_{n}>\Delta;\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\notin\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big]\text{ or }\left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert>\Delta\Big)\quad\text{by \eqref{proof, lower bound for r in B, lemma: asymptotic equivalence, MRV in Rd} and }\Delta<\bar{\epsilon}.

By condition (i),

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B;\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\in\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big],\ \left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert\leq\Delta\Big).

On the other hand,
recall the definition of
B_{\theta}
in (
C.1
),
and
let

\displaystyle\breve{B}(M,\delta)=\Big\{(r,\bm{w})\in[0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}:\ r\geq M,\ \left\lVert\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq\delta\text{ for some }\bm{w}^{\prime}\in B_{\theta}\Big\}.

Also, recall that we picked
\bar{\epsilon}>0
such that
\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}(B,\mathbb{C})>\bar{\epsilon}.

For any
\bar{\Delta}\in(0,\frac{\bar{\epsilon}}{2}\wedge\frac{1}{2})
and all
M\geq 1,\Delta>0
with
M\Delta<\bar{\Delta}
, note that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B;\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\in\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big],\ \left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert\leq\Delta\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B(M);\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\in\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big],\ \left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert\leq\Delta\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B\setminus B(M);\ \hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\in\big[(1-\Delta)R_{n},(1+\Delta)R_{n}\big],\ \left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert\leq\Delta\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\diamond)}}{{\leq}}\mathbf{P}\bigg((\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in\big(B(M)\big)^{\bar{\Delta}}\bigg)+\mathbf{P}\bigg(\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n}\geq(1-\Delta)M,\ \left\lVert\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n}-\Theta_{n}\right\rVert\leq\Delta\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\bigg(\underbrace{(\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in\big(B(M)\big)^{\bar{\Delta}}}_{=\text{(I)}}\bigg)+\mathbf{P}\bigg(\underbrace{(\hat{R}^{\delta}_{n},\hat{\Theta}^{\delta}_{n})\in\breve{B}\big((1-\bar{\Delta})M,\bar{\Delta}\big)}_{=\text{(II)}}\bigg).

Here, the step
(\diamond)
follows from (
C.4
) and the definition of
B(M)
.
For the event (I),
by our choice of
\bar{\Delta}<\bar{\epsilon}/2
,
it follows from

\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}(B,\mathbb{C})>\bar{\epsilon}

that
\big(B(M)\big)^{2\bar{\Delta}}\subseteq B^{2\bar{\Delta}}
is still bounded away from
\mathbb{C}
under
\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}
;
then by condition (ii), it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(I)}\big)\leq|\mathcal{V}|\bar{\Delta}+\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\mu_{v}\bigg(\big(B(M)\big)^{2\bar{\Delta}}\bigg).

Analogously, for the event (II),
note that (
C.2
) implies that

\breve{B}(M,\Delta)

is bounded away from
\mathbb{C}
under
\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}
for any
M>0
and
\Delta<\bar{\epsilon}
.
Then by condition (ii), it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(II)}\big)\leq|\mathcal{V}|\bar{\Delta}+\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\mu_{v}\bigg(\Big(\breve{B}\big((1-\bar{\Delta})M,\ \bar{\Delta}\big)\Big)^{2\bar{\Delta}}\bigg).

Note that

\bigcap_{M>0}\breve{B}(M,\bar{\Delta})=\emptyset

and
|\mathcal{V}|<\infty
.
By sending
M\to\infty
and then
\bar{\Delta}\to 0
,
we get

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}\Big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in B\Big)\leq\sum_{v\in\mathcal{V}}\mu_{v}(B^{-}).

(C.7)

In light of Theorem
2.2
—the Portmanteau theorem for
\mathbb{M}
-convergence—and the arbitrariness in our choice of
B
,
we combine (
C.6
) and (
C.7
),
concluding the proof.
∎

Next, to prove Lemma
2.4
, we recall the definition of
\Phi
in (
2.7
).
In particular, given
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
that does not contain the origin, note that

\displaystyle\bm{x}\in A\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\Phi(\bm{x})\in\Phi(A).

(C.8)

In addition, the following properties follow from the fact that the polar transform is a homeomorphism between
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
and
(0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}
:
given
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
that is bounded away from
\bm{0}
(i.e.,
\inf_{\bm{x}\in A}\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>0
),

\displaystyle\text{$A$ is open }\iff\text{ $\Phi(A)$ is open},\qquad\text{$A$ is closed }\iff\text{ $\Phi(A)$ is closed}.

(C.9)

We prepare the following lemma.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
\mathbb{C}
be a closed cone in
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
.
Let
\mathbb{C}_{\Phi}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.19441pt\raisebox{5.64096pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-3.19441pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\big\{(r,\bm{\theta})\in[0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}:\ r\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{C}\big\},

and let
\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)
be defined as in (
2.11
).
For any Borel set
B\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
,
the following two conditions are equivalent:

1.

B
is bounded away from
\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)
for some (and hence all)
\epsilon>0
small enough;

2.

\Phi(B)
is bounded away from
\mathbb{C}_{\Phi}
under
\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}
.

Proof.

Proof of
(i)\Rightarrow(ii)
.
Fix some
\epsilon,\Delta>0
such that
\inf\{\left\lVert\bm{x}-\bm{y}\right\rVert:\ \bm{x}\in B,\ \bm{y}\in\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)\}>\Delta
.
Since
\mathbb{C}
is a cone, we have
\bm{0}\in\mathbb{C}\subseteq\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)
, and hence
\inf_{\bm{x}\in B}\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>\Delta
.
Next, we consider a proof by contradiction.
Suppose there are sequences
(r^{\bm{x}}_{n},\theta^{\bm{x}}_{n})\in\Phi(B)
and
(r^{\bm{y}}_{n},\theta^{\bm{y}}_{n})\in\mathbb{C}_{\Phi}
such that

\displaystyle\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}\big((r^{\bm{x}}_{n},\theta^{\bm{x}}_{n}),(r^{\bm{y}}_{n},\theta^{\bm{y}}_{n})\big)=\left\lVert r^{\bm{x}}_{n}-r^{\bm{y}}_{n}\right\rVert\vee\left\lVert\theta^{\bm{x}}_{n}-\theta^{\bm{y}}_{n}\right\rVert\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(C.10)

By property (
C.8
), there exists a sequence
\bm{x}_{n}\in B
such that
(r^{\bm{x}}_{n},\theta^{\bm{x}}_{n})=\Phi(\bm{x}_{n})
for each
n\geq 1
,
and hence
\bm{x}_{n}=r^{\bm{x}}_{n}\theta^{\bm{x}}_{n}
due to
\left\lVert\bm{x}_{n}\right\rVert>\Delta
.
By (
C.10
), for any
n
large enough we have

\left\lVert\theta^{\bm{x}}_{n}-\theta^{\bm{y}}_{n}\right\rVert<\epsilon.

Since
\mathbb{C}
is a cone, by the definition in (
2.11
) we arrive at the contradiction
\bm{x}_{n}=r^{\bm{x}}_{n}\theta^{\bm{x}}_{n}\in\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)
for all
n
large enough.
This concludes the proof of
(i)\Rightarrow(ii)
.

Proof of
(ii)\Rightarrow(i)
.
Fix some
\Delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\inf\big\{\left\lVert r^{\bm{x}}-r^{\bm{y}}\right\rVert\vee\left\lVert\theta^{\bm{x}}-\theta^{\bm{y}}\right\rVert:\ (r^{\bm{x}},\theta^{\bm{x}})\in\Phi(B),\ (r^{\bm{y}},\theta^{\bm{y}})\in\mathbb{C}_{\Phi}\big\}>\Delta.

(C.11)

First, note that
r^{\bm{x}}>\Delta
for any
(r^{\bm{x}},\theta^{\bm{x}})\in\Phi(B)
.
To see why, simply note that
\bm{0}\in\mathbb{C}
, and hence
(0,\theta)\in\mathbb{C}_{\Phi}
for any
r\in\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}
.
As a result, we have
\inf_{\bm{x}\in B}\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>\Delta
.
Furthermore, note that

\displaystyle\inf\big\{\left\lVert\theta^{\bm{x}}-\theta^{\bm{y}}\right\rVert:\ (r^{\bm{x}},\theta^{\bm{x}})\in\Phi(B),\ (r^{\bm{y}},\theta^{\bm{y}})\in\mathbb{C}_{\Phi},\ r^{\bm{y}}>0\big\}>\Delta.

(C.12)

To see why, note that for any

(r^{\bm{x}},\theta^{\bm{x}})\in\Phi(B)
and
(r^{\bm{y}},\theta^{\bm{y}})\in\mathbb{C}_{\Phi}
with
r^{\bm{y}}>0
,
we have
(r^{\bm{x}},\theta^{\bm{y}})\in\mathbb{C}_{\Phi}
since
\mathbb{C}
is a cone.
Claim (
C.12
) then follows from (
C.11
).
On the other hand, by the definition of
\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)
, for any
\epsilon,\delta>0
and any
B\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
with
\inf_{\bm{x}\in B}\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>\delta
,
the claim
B\cap\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)=\emptyset
would imply that
B
is bounded away from
\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon/2)
.
Indeed,
\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon/2)\cap\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert<\delta/2\}
is clearly bounded away from
B
due to

\inf_{\bm{x}\in B}\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>\delta
;
as for
\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon/2)\cap\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert\geq\delta/2\}
,
one only needs to note that this set is bounded away from
\big(\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)\big)^{c}
.
In summary, it suffices to find some
\epsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle B\cap\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)=\emptyset.

To this end, we fix some
\epsilon\in(0,\Delta)
.
Since
\inf_{\bm{x}\in B}\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>\Delta
, it suffices to consider some
\bm{y}\in\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)
with
\bm{y}\neq\bm{0}
.
Let
(r,\theta^{\prime})=\Phi(\bm{y})
. Note that
r>0
due to
\bm{y}\neq\bm{0}
.
Besides,
by the definition of
\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)
, there exists some
\theta\in\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}
such that
\left\lVert\theta-\theta^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq\epsilon<\Delta

and
(r,\theta)\in\mathbb{C}_{\Phi}
.
Then, by the property (
C.12
) and our choice of
\epsilon\in(0,\Delta)
, we must have
\bm{y}\notin B
.
By the arbitrariness of
\bm{y}\in\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
,
we yield
B\cap\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)=\emptyset
and conclude the proof of
(ii)\Rightarrow(i)
.
∎

Next, we state the proof of Lemma
2.4
.

Proof of Lemma
2.4
.

To prove
(i)\Rightarrow(ii)
,
we fix some
closed
F\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
and open
O\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
such that
F
and
O
are both bounded away from
\bar{\mathbb{C}}(\epsilon)
for some
\epsilon>0
.
Due to
\bm{0}\in\mathbb{C}
, we must have that
\bm{0}
is bounded away from both
F
and
O
.
Furthermore, by Lemma
C.1
, we get

\displaystyle\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}\big(\Phi(F),\mathbb{C}_{\Phi}\big)>0,\qquad\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}\big(\Phi(O),\mathbb{C}_{\Phi}\big)>0.

(C.13)

Now, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(X_{n}\in F)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\big((R_{n},\Theta_{n})\in\Phi(F)\big)\qquad\text{by \eqref{property: polar transform, 1}},

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\limsup_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}{\mathbf{P}(X_{n}\in F)}

\displaystyle\leq\mu\circ\Phi^{-1}\Big((\Phi(F))^{-}\Big)\qquad\text{
by \eqref{condition: M convergence for polar coordinates, lemma: M convergence for MRV} and \eqref{proof: bounded away condition under d U, lemma: M convergence for MRV}
}

\displaystyle=\mu\circ\Phi^{-1}\big(\Phi(F)\big)\qquad\text{by \eqref{property: polar transform, 2}}

\displaystyle=\mu(F)\qquad\text{by the definitions in \eqref{def: mu composition Phi inverse measure}}.

Furthermore, condition (
2.9
) implies that
\mu\circ\Phi^{-1}\in\mathbb{M}\big([0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}\setminus\mathbb{C}_{\Phi}\big)
,
and hence
\mu\circ\Phi^{-1}(E)<\infty
for any Borel set
E\subseteq[0,\infty)\times\mathfrak{N}^{d}_{+}
that is bounded away from
\mathbb{C}_{\Phi}
.
Since
\Phi(F)
is bounded away from
\mathbb{C}_{\Phi}
, we verify that

\mu\circ\Phi^{-1}\big(\Phi(F)\big)=\mu(F)<\infty.

Analogously, one can show that

\liminf_{n\to\infty}\epsilon^{-1}_{n}\mathbf{P}(X_{n}\in O)\geq\mu(O).

To conclude the proof of
(i)\Rightarrow(ii)
, we pick
O=A^{\circ}
and
F=A^{-}
in (
2.10
).
Lastly, we note that the proof of
(ii)\Rightarrow(i)
is almost identical and follows from a reverse applicaton of Lemma
C.1
.
We omit the details here to avoid repetition.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs of Technical Lemmas

D.1
Proofs of Lemmas
4.5
and
4.6

Recall the definition of
{\bar{b}_{j\leftarrow i}}=\mathbf{E}B_{j\leftarrow i}
,
as well as the mean offspring matrix
{\bar{\textbf{B}}}=(\bar{b}_{j\leftarrow i})_{j,i\in[d]}
.
We adopt the operator norm

\left\lVert\textbf{A}\right\rVert=\sup_{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert\textbf{A}\bm{x}\right\rVert

for any
d\times d
real-valued matrix under the
L_{1}
norm for vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
We first provide the proofs of
Lemmas
4.5
and
4.6
under the condition that
\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}\right\rVert<1
.
Then, inspired by the approach in
[
50
]
based on Gelfand’s formula, we extend the proof to general cases.

Proof of Lemma
4.5
(
\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}\right\rVert<1
).

By considering the transform
N=n\Delta
(and hence
n\delta=N\frac{\delta}{\Delta}
), it suffices to prove the claim for
\Delta=1
.
Besides, since the index
i
takes finitely many possible values from
[d]=\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
, we only need to fix some
i\in[d]
in this proof and and show the existence of some
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\gamma)>0
such that

\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})

for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
.
Also, recall that we work with the condition that

\rho\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}\right\rVert<1
.
We fix some
\epsilon>0
small enough such that

\displaystyle 2d\epsilon+\rho(1+2d\epsilon)+d\epsilon(1+2d\epsilon)<1.

(D.1)

Henceforth in the proof, we only consider
n
large enough such that
n\epsilon>1
.
Now,
we are able to fix some integer
K_{\epsilon}
and a collection of vectors

\{\bm{z}(k)=(z_{1}(k),\ldots,z_{d}(k))^{\top}:\ k\in[K_{\epsilon}]\}

such that the following claims hold:

(i)
for each
k\in[K_{\epsilon}]
, we have
z_{j}(k)\geq 0\ \forall j\in[d]
and
\sum_{j=1}^{d}z_{j}(k)=1
;

(ii)
given any
\bm{z}=(z_{1},\cdots,z_{d})^{\top}\in[0,\infty)^{d}
with
\sum_{j=1}^{d}z_{j}=1
,
there exists some
k\in[K_{\epsilon}]
such that

\displaystyle|z_{j}-z_{j}(k)|<\epsilon,\qquad\forall j\in[d].

(D.2)

The vectors
\big(\bm{z}(k)\big)_{k\in[K_{\epsilon}]}
provide a finite covering of

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Bigg\{(z_{1},\ldots,z_{d})^{\top}\in[0,\infty)^{d}:\ \sum_{j=1}^{d}z_{j}=1\Bigg\}

(D.3)

with resolution
\epsilon
.

For each
j\in[d]
, let
\{{\bm{B}}^{(m)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}:\ m\geq 1\}

be i.i.d. copies of

\bm{B}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j},

which will be interpreted as the offspring count of the
m^{\text{th}}
type-
j
individual in the branching tree of
\bm{S}_{i}
.
More precisely,
in this proof we order nodes in a multi-type branching tree using a standard rule:
given
j\in[d]
, type-
j
nodes are numbered left to right, starting from generation 0, then continuing similarly in each subsequent generation.
For instance, (i) in the branching tree for
\bm{S}_{i}
, the first type-
i
node will always be the type-
i
root node in the
0^{\text{th}}
generation;
and
(ii) if there are
n
type-
j
nodes in the first
k
generations, the numbering in the
(k+1)^{\text{th}}
generation starts from
n+1
.
In doing so, the underlying branching processes (and hence the total progeny
\bm{S}_{i}
) are measurable functions of
({\bm{B}}^{(m)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j})_{j\in[d],m\geq 1}
.
Next, we set

\displaystyle\bm{B}^{\leqslant,(m)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}(M)=\big(B^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i\leftarrow j}(M)\big)_{i\in[d]},\quad\text{where }B^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i\leftarrow j}(M)=B^{(m)}_{i\leftarrow j}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(m)}_{i\leftarrow j}\leq M\}.

For each
M>0
, we consider a similar coupling between
({\bm{B}}^{\leqslant,(m)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}(M))_{j\in[d],m\geq 1}
and the branching tree for
\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M)
,
such that
{\bm{B}}^{\leqslant,(m)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}(M)
is the offspring count for the
m^{\text{th}}
type-
j
node in the branching tree for
\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M)
.
Now, observe the following
on the event
\Big\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Big\}
:
by considering the first
n
nodes in the tree
3
3

3

The exact counting of the first
n
nodes, across the
d
types, can be made precise by assuming the following:
within each generation, type-1 nodes reproduce first, followed by type-2, and so on; similarly, each node gives birth in order, first to type-1 children, then type-2, and so forth.

as well as their children,
we can find some
(n_{1},\ldots,n_{d})^{\top}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{d}
with
\sum_{j=1}^{d}n_{j}=n

such that

n_{j}\leq\mathbbm{I}\{j=i\}+\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{n_{l}}B^{\leqslant,(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)

holds for each
j\in[d]
.
Also, we fix the
\bm{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
(see (
D.3
)) such that
(n_{1},\ldots,n_{d})^{\top}=n\bm{z}
,
and
recall that we only consider
n
with
n\epsilon>1
.
By our choice of
\bm{z}(k)
’s in (
D.2
), there exists some
k\in[K_{\epsilon}]
such that

\displaystyle nz_{j}(k)-n\epsilon\leq n\epsilon+\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{\lceil nz_{l}(k)+n\epsilon\rceil}B^{\leqslant,(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta),\qquad\forall j\in[d].

In summary, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in[K_{\epsilon}]}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(nz_{j}(k)\leq 2n\epsilon+\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{\lceil nz_{l}(k)+n\epsilon\rceil}B^{\leqslant,(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\ \forall j\in[d]\Bigg).

(D.4)

Furthermore,
suppose that for each
\bm{z}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{d})^{\top}\in\mathcal{Z}
, we have
(for any
\delta>0
small enough)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\underbrace{nz_{j}\leq 2n\epsilon+\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{\lceil nz_{l}+n\epsilon\rceil}B^{\leqslant,(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\ \forall j\in[d]}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}A(n,\delta,\bm{z})}\Bigg)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma}),\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(D.5)

Then, by applying (
D.5
) for the finitely many
\bm{z}(k)
’s identified in (
D.2
),
we can find some
\delta_{0}>0
—depending only on
\epsilon
and
\gamma
—such that in (
D.4
),
we have

\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Big)\leq K_{\epsilon}\cdot o(n^{-\gamma})=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})

for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
.
Now, it only remains to prove Claim (
D.5
).

Proof of Claim (
D.5
)
.
Note that
\bm{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
implies
\sum_{j\in[d]}z_{j}=1
.
Also, recall that
\bar{b}_{j\leftarrow l}=\mathbf{E}B_{j\leftarrow l}\geq\mathbf{E}B^{\leqslant,(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)
.
Define the event

\displaystyle F(n,\delta,\bm{z})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\bigcap_{l\in[d],\ j\in[d]}\Bigg\{\sum_{m=1}^{\lceil n(z_{l}+\epsilon)\rceil}B^{\leqslant,(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\leq\lceil n(z_{l}+\epsilon)\rceil\cdot(\bar{b}_{j\leftarrow l}+\epsilon)\Bigg\}.

We first show that on the event
F(n,\delta,\bm{z})
,
we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert n\bm{z}\right\rVert>\left\lVert\bigg(2n\epsilon+\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{\lceil nz_{l}+n\epsilon\rceil}B^{\leqslant,(m)}_{1\leftarrow l}(n\delta),\ldots,2n\epsilon+\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{\lceil nz_{l}+n\epsilon\rceil}B^{\leqslant,(m)}_{d\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\bigg)^{\top}\right\rVert,

and hence
F(n,\delta,\bm{z})\cap A(n,\delta,\bm{z})=\emptyset
.
To see why, note that
on
F(n,\delta,\bm{z})
, we have

\displaystyle 2n\epsilon+\sum_{l\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{\lceil nz_{l}+n\epsilon\rceil}B^{\leqslant,(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\leq 2n\epsilon+\sum_{l\in[d]}\lceil n(z_{l}+\epsilon)\rceil\cdot(\bar{b}_{j\leftarrow l}+\epsilon),\quad\forall j\in[d].

(D.6)

To describe the implications of (
D.6
),
we first recall the notational conventions

(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})^{\top}\leq(y_{1},\ldots,y_{d})^{\top}
if
x_{j}\leq y_{j}\ \forall j\in[d]
,

\bm{1}=(1,\ldots,1)^{\top}
,
and

\textbf{X}+c=(x_{l,j}+c)_{l,j}
for a matrix
\textbf{X}=(x_{l,j})_{l,j}
.
For any
n
large enough such that
n\epsilon>1
(with
\epsilon
specified in (
D.1
)),
the vectorized version of the RHS of
Claim (
D.6
) is upper bounded by

\displaystyle 2n\epsilon\cdot\bm{1}+(\bar{\textbf{B}}+\epsilon)\big(\lceil n(z_{1}+\epsilon)\rceil,\ldots,\lceil n(z_{d}+\epsilon)\rceil\big)^{\top}

(D.7)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2n\epsilon\cdot\bm{1}+(\bar{\textbf{B}}+\epsilon)\big(n(z_{1}+2\epsilon),\ldots,n(z_{d}+2\epsilon)\big)^{\top}\quad\text{ due to $n\epsilon>1$}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2n\epsilon\cdot\bm{1}+\bar{\textbf{B}}\big(n(z_{1}+2\epsilon),\ldots,n(z_{d}+2\epsilon)\big)^{\top}+n\epsilon(1+2d\epsilon)\cdot\bm{1}\ \text{ since }\sum_{j\in[d]}z_{j}=1.

Due to
\rho=\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}\right\rVert<1

and
\sum_{j\in[d]}z_{j}=1
,
we get

\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}\big(n(z_{1}+2\epsilon),\ldots,n(z_{d}+2\epsilon)\big)^{\top}\right\rVert\leq\rho n\cdot\sum_{j\in[d]}(z_{j}+2\epsilon)=\rho n(1+2d\epsilon).

Combining this bound with (
D.7
), we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert 2n\epsilon\cdot\bm{1}+(\bar{\textbf{B}}+\epsilon)\big(\lceil n(z_{1}+\epsilon)\rceil,\ldots,\lceil n(z_{d}+\epsilon)\rceil\big)^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n\cdot\Big(2d\epsilon+\rho(1+2d\epsilon)+d\epsilon(1+2d\epsilon)\Big)<n=\left\lVert n\bm{z}\right\rVert\qquad\text{by \eqref{proof: pick epsilon, lemma: tail bound, pruned cluster size S i leq n delta}.}

In summary,
F(n,\delta,\bm{z})\cap A(n,\delta,\bm{z})=\emptyset
holds for any
n
large enough.
This implies

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(A(n,\delta,\bm{z})\big)\leq\sum_{l\in[d],j\in[d]}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\frac{1}{\lceil n(z_{l}+\epsilon)\rceil}\sum_{m=1}^{\lceil n(z_{l}+\epsilon)\rceil}B^{\leqslant,(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)>(1+\epsilon)\bar{b}_{j\leftarrow l}\Bigg).

(D.8)

Recall the
B^{\leqslant,(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)
’s are i.i.d. copies of
B_{j\leftarrow l}\mathbbm{I}\{B_{j\leftarrow l}\leq n\delta\}
.
By Assumption
2
,
we have
\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l}>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha_{j\leftarrow l}}(x)
with
\alpha_{j\leftarrow l}>1
.
Applying Lemma
A.1
,
we confirm that for any
\delta>0
small enough,
the RHS of (
D.8
)
is upper bounded by an
\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})
term.
This concludes the proof of Claim (
D.5
) for the case of
\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}\right\rVert<1
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.6
.

We first note that
this proof does not explicitly require the condition
\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}\right\rVert<1
.
That is, once we establish Lemma
4.5
for the case of
\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}\right\rVert\geq 1
,
the same proof below will follow, so there is no need to distinguish these two cases for the proof of Lemma
4.6
.
In addition,
it suffices to fix some
i,j\in[d]
and
\epsilon,\gamma>0
, and then prove the existence of
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\epsilon,\gamma)>0
such that the claims

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}S^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i,j}(n\delta)<\bar{s}_{i,j}-\epsilon\Bigg)

\displaystyle=0,

(D.9)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}S^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i,j}(n\delta)>\bar{s}_{i,j}+\epsilon\Bigg)

\displaystyle=0

(D.10)

hold for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,
where we write
\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i}(M)=\big(S^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i,j}(M)\big)_{j\in[d]}
.

Proof of Claim (
D.9
)
.
Take any
\delta>0
.
Monotone convergence implies
\lim_{M\to\infty}\mathbf{E}\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M)=\mathbf{E}\bm{S}_{i}=\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}
,
thus allowing us to fix
M>0
such that

\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}-\epsilon\bm{1}<\mathbf{E}\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M).

Furthermore, monotone convergence implies that for any
M^{\prime}
large enough, we have

\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}-\epsilon\bm{1}<\mathbf{E}\Big[\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M)\mathbbm{I}\Big\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M)\right\rVert\leq M^{\prime}\Big\}\Big].

The stochastic comparison property (
4.3
) then implies

\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M)\mathbbm{I}\Big\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M)\right\rVert\leq M^{\prime}\Big\}\leq\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(M)\underset{\text{s.t.}}{\leq}\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)

for any
n
large enough such that
n\delta\geq M
.
Therefore, it suffices to prove

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}S^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i,j}(M)\mathbbm{I}\Big\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i}(M)\right\rVert\leq M^{\prime}\Big\}<\bar{s}_{i,j}-\epsilon\bigg)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma}).

In particular, note that the i.i.d. copies
S^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i,j}(M)\mathbbm{I}\Big\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i}(M)\right\rVert\leq M^{\prime}\Big\}

have finite moment generating functions due to the truncation under
M^{\prime}
.
This allows us to apply Cramèr’s Theorem to conclude the proof of Claim (
D.9
).

Proof of Claim (
D.10
)
.
Take any
\Delta>0
.
For any
x,c\in\mathbb{R}
, let
\phi_{c}(x)=x\wedge c
.
Observe that

\displaystyle\bigg\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}S^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i,j}(n\delta)>\bar{s}_{i,j}+\epsilon\bigg\}

(D.11)

\displaystyle\subseteq\Big\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Delta\text{ for some }m\in[n]\Big\}\cup\bigg\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\phi_{n\Delta}\big(S^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i,j}(n\delta)\big)>\bar{s}_{i,j}+\epsilon\bigg\}.

On the one hand, given any
\Delta>0
, there exists
\delta_{0}(\Delta,\gamma)>0
such that

\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Delta\text{ for some }m\in[n]\Big)\leq n\cdot\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Delta\Big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})

(D.12)

cf. Lemma
4.5
.
On the other hand,
by the stochastic comparison in (
4.3
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\phi_{n\Delta}\big(S^{\leqslant,(m)}_{i,j}(n\delta)\big)>\bar{s}_{i,j}+\epsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\mathbf{P}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\phi_{n\Delta}(S_{i,j}^{(m)})>\bar{s}_{i,j}+\epsilon\bigg)}_{=p(n,\Delta)},

with the
S^{(m)}_{i,j}
’s being i.i.d. copies of

S_{i,j}
.
Suppose we can show that

\mathbf{P}(S_{i,j}>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}(x)
for some
\alpha>1
.
Then, by Claim (
A.3
) in Lemma
A.1
and property (
A.1
),
we fix some
\Delta>0
small enough such that

p(n,\Delta)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})

as
n\to\infty
.
Plugging this bound and (
D.12
) into (
D.11
), we conclude the proof of Claim (
D.10
).
Now, it only remains to verify the regular variation of
S_{i,j}
.
By Assumptions
2
and
4
,
there uniquely exists a pair
(l^{*},k^{*})\in[d]^{2}
such that

\alpha_{l^{*}\leftarrow k^{*}}=\alpha^{*}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\min_{l.k\in[d]}\alpha_{l\leftarrow k},

and
\alpha^{*}>1
,
\mathbf{P}(B_{l^{*}\leftarrow k^{*}}>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha^{*}}(x)
.
By Theorem 2 of
[
1
]
(under the choice of
Q(k)=\mathbbm{I}\{k=j\}
in Equation (6) of
[
1
]
),
there exists a constant
c^{*}_{i,j}>0
such that

\mathbf{P}(S_{i,j}>x)\sim c^{*}_{i,j}\mathbf{P}(B_{l^{*}\leftarrow k^{*}}>x)

as
x\to\infty
.
In particular,
translating our Assumption
3
into the context of
[
1
]
,
we have
m_{ik}>0
for any
i,k
in Equation (15) of
[
1
]
,
thus implying
d_{i}>0
for any
i
in Equation (15) of
[
1
]
.
Equivalently, this confirms
c^{*}_{i,j}>0
.
∎

D.2
Proof of Lemma
4.5
: General Case

Recall the definition of
{\bar{b}_{j\leftarrow i}}=\mathbf{E}B_{j\leftarrow i}
,
the mean offspring matrix
{\bar{\textbf{B}}}=(\bar{b}_{j\leftarrow i})_{j,i\in[d]}
,
and the operator norm

\left\lVert\textbf{A}\right\rVert=\sup_{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert\textbf{A}\bm{x}\right\rVert

for matrix
\textbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
under the
L_{1}
norm for vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
We provide the proof of Lemma
4.5
without the additional assumption that
\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}\right\rVert<1
.
We first prepare the following lemma.

Lemma D.1
.

Let Assumption
2
hold.
Let
\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(t;M)
be defined as in (
3.19
).
Given
t\geq 1
,
j\in[d]
,
\Delta>0
, and
\gamma>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\bigg(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(t;n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Delta\bigg)=0,\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ sufficiently small.}

(D.13)

Proof.

We first consider the case of
t=1
.
By definitions in (
3.19
) and that
\bm{X}_{j}^{\leqslant}(0;n\delta)=\bm{e}_{j}
,
we have

\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(1;n\delta)=\big(B^{(1,1)}_{i\leftarrow j}\mathbbm{I}\{B^{(1,1)}_{i\leftarrow j}\leq n\delta\}\big)_{i\in[d]}.

By picking
\delta\in(0,\Delta/d)
, we must have
\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(1;n\delta)\right\rVert\leq d\cdot n\delta<n\Delta
.
Next, we proceed inductively.
Specifically, we fix some
\gamma>0
,
j\in[d]
,
and suppose that there exists some positive integer
T
such that Claim (
D.13
) holds for any
t\in[T]
and
\Delta>0
.
Then, given
\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}>0
, by the definitions in (
3.19
) we have

\displaystyle\bigg\{\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(T+1;n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Delta\bigg\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\underbrace{\bigg\{\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(T;n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Delta^{\prime}\bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\text{(I)}}\cup\underbrace{\Bigg\{\left\lVert\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{\lfloor n\Delta^{\prime}\rfloor}{\bm{B}}^{\leqslant,(T+1,m)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Delta\Bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\text{(II)}}.

In particular,
recall that
\bar{b}_{l\leftarrow i}=\mathbf{E}B_{l\leftarrow i}
.
Given
\Delta>0
, we pick
\Delta^{\prime}>0
small enough such that

\displaystyle\Delta^{\prime}\cdot\max_{l\in[d],i\in[d]}\bar{b}_{l\leftarrow i}<\Delta/d^{2}.

(D.14)

On the one hand, by our assumption for the inductive argument, we have

\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(I)}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})

under any
\delta>0
small enough.
On the other hand, using
B^{(m)}_{l\leftarrow i}
to denote generic i.i.d. copies of
B_{l\leftarrow i}
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(II)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{l\in[d]}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\Bigg|\sum_{m=1}^{\lfloor n\Delta^{\prime}\rfloor}{B}^{(m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\mathbbm{I}\Big\{{B}^{(m)}_{l\leftarrow i}\leq n\delta\Big\}\Bigg|>\frac{n\Delta}{d^{2}}\Bigg).

Assumption
2
dictates that

\mathbf{P}(B_{l\leftarrow i}>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha_{l\leftarrow i}}(x)

with
\alpha_{l\leftarrow i}>1
.
With
\Delta^{\prime}
fixed in (
D.14
), we apply Claim (
A.2
) in Lemma
A.1

for each pair
(l,i)\in[d]^{2}
to obtain
\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(II)}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})
under any
\delta>0
small enough.
This confirms that, given
\Delta>0
, the claim

\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{j}(T+1;n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Delta\Big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})

holds
for any
\delta>0
small enough.
By proceeding inductively, we conclude the proof.
∎

Our proof of Lemma
4.5
(in the general case) is inspired by the strategy in

[
50
]
.
In particular, we show that, for some positive integer
r
, results analogous to Lemma
4.5
hold for the
r
-step sub-sampled verison
\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{i}(t;n\delta)
,
and we apply the bounds for each sub-tree.
To this end, we first precisely define the total progeny of the sub-sampled branching process (for every
r
generations):

\displaystyle\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant}_{j}(M)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{k\geq 0}\bm{X}_{j}^{\leqslant}(kr;M),\qquad j\in[d],\ M>0,\ r\in\mathbb{N},

(D.15)

with the multi-type branching process
\bm{X}_{j}^{\leqslant}(t;M)
defined in (
3.19
).
That is, we only inspect the original branching process for every
r
generations,
and use
\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant}_{j}(M)
to denote the total progeny of this
r
-step sub-sampled branching process.
Furthermore, let the random vectors
\bm{B}^{[r],\leqslant}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}(M)=\big(B^{[r],\leqslant}_{i\leftarrow j}(M)\big)_{i\in[d]}
have law

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}\Big(\bm{B}^{[r],\leqslant}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}(M)\Big)=\mathscr{L}\Big(\bm{X}_{j}^{\leqslant}(r;M)\Big),\qquad j\in[d],

(D.16)

and note that (with the
\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant;(k)}_{i}(M)
’s being i.i.d. copies of
\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant}_{i}(M)
)

\displaystyle\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant}_{j}(M)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bm{e}_{j}+\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum^{B^{[r],\leqslant}_{i\leftarrow j}(M)}_{k=1}\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant;(k)}_{i}(M),\qquad j\in[d].

In other words,
\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant}_{j}(M)
also represents the total progeny of a branching process, whose offspring distribution admits the law in (
D.16
) and coincides with the
r^{\text{th}}
generation offspring from a type-
j
ancestor in the branching process
\big(\bm{X}_{j}^{\leqslant}(t;M)\big)_{t\geq 0}
.

We use
\textbf{A}^{k}
to denote the
k
-fold product of
A
under matrix multiplication.
The next result establishes claims analogous to those in Lemma
4.5
, but for the sub-sampled
\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)
.

Lemma D.2
.

Let Assumptions
2
–
4
hold,
and suppose that
\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}^{r}\right\rVert<1
holds for some positive integer
r
.
Given any
\Delta,\ \gamma\in(0,\infty)
,
there exists
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\Delta,\gamma,r)>0
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Delta\Big)=0,\qquad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}),\ i\in[d].

Proof.

Repeating the arguments in the proof of Lemma
4.5
under the additional condition
\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}\right\rVert<1

(in particular, the derivation of the bound (
D.8
))
in Section
D.1
,
it suffices to show that given
\epsilon>0
and a vector
\bm{z}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{d})^{\top}\in[0,\infty)^{d}
with
\sum_{j\in[d]}z_{j}=1
,
the claim

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d],\ l\in[d]}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\frac{1}{\lceil n(z_{l}+\epsilon)\rceil}\sum_{m=1}^{\lceil n(z_{l}+\epsilon)\rceil}B^{[r],\leqslant;(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)>(1+\epsilon)\bar{b}^{[r]}_{j\leftarrow l}\Bigg)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})

holds for any
\delta>0
small enough.
Here,

\bm{B}^{[r],\leqslant;(m)}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow l}=\big(B^{[r],\leqslant;(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}\big)_{j\in[d]}

are i.i.d. copies of
\bm{B}^{[r],\leqslant}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow l}
under the law stated in (
D.16
),
and
\bar{b}^{[r]}_{j\leftarrow l}
is the element on the
j^{\text{th}}
row and
l^{\text{th}}
column of the matrix
\bar{\textbf{B}}^{r}
,
meaning that
\bar{b}^{[r]}_{j\leftarrow l}=\mathbf{E}\big[X_{l,j}(r)\big]

for the branching process
\big(\bm{X}_{j}(t)\big)_{t\geq 0}
defined in (
3.17
).
Now, let
\phi_{c}(x)=x\wedge c
, and note that

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\lceil n(z_{l}+\epsilon)\rceil}\sum_{m=1}^{\lceil n(z_{l}+\epsilon)\rceil}B^{[r],\leqslant;(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)>(1+\epsilon)\bar{b}^{[r]}_{j\leftarrow l}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\underbrace{\bigg\{\Big|B^{[r],\leqslant;(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\Big|>n\Delta\text{ for some }m\leq\lceil n(z_{l}+\epsilon)\rceil\bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\text{(I)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cup\underbrace{\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\lceil n(z_{l}+\epsilon)\rceil}\sum_{m=1}^{\lceil n(z_{l}+\epsilon)\rceil}\phi_{n\Delta}\Big(B^{[r],\leqslant;(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\Big)>(1+\epsilon)\bar{b}^{[r]}_{j\leftarrow l}\Bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\text{(II)}}.

As a result, it suffices to fix a pair
(l,j)\in[d]^{2}
and find some
\Delta>0
such that
\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(I)}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})
and

\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(II)}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})

hold
under any
\delta>0
small enough.

Proof of
\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(I)}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})
.
This claim holds for any
\Delta>0
, due to
the law stated in (
D.16
) and Lemma
D.1
.

Proof of
\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(II)}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})
.
Suppose that we can find some random variable
\tilde{B}
such that
B^{[r],\leqslant}_{j\leftarrow l}(M)\underset{\text{s.t.}}{\leq}\tilde{B}
for any
M>0
,

\mathbf{P}(\tilde{B}>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}(x)
for some
\alpha>1
, and

\mathbf{E}\tilde{B}<(1+\epsilon)\bar{b}^{[r]}_{j\leftarrow l}.

Then, by combining

\sum_{m=1}^{k}\phi_{n\Delta}\big(B^{[r],\leqslant;(m)}_{j\leftarrow l}(n\delta)\big)\underset{\text{s.t.}}{\leq}\sum_{m=1}^{k}\phi_{n\Delta}\big(\tilde{B}^{(m)}\big)

(with the
\tilde{B}^{(m)}
’s being independent copies of
\tilde{B}
)
with Claim (
A.3
) in Lemma
A.1
(applied onto
\phi_{n\Delta}\big(\tilde{B}^{(m)}\big)
) and property (
A.1
),
we get
\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(II)}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})
for any
\Delta>0
small enough.

Now, it only remains to construct such
\tilde{B}
.
By (
D.16
) and the stochastic comparison stated in (
4.2
),
we have

B^{[r],\leqslant}_{j\leftarrow l}(M)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}X_{l,j}^{\leqslant}(r;M)\underset{\text{s.t.}}{\leq}X_{l,j}(r)

for each
M>0
.
Also, we obviously have
X_{l,j}(r)\underset{\text{s.t.}}{\leq}S_{l,j}
(since
\sum_{t\geq 0}\bm{X}_{l}(t)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bm{S}_{l}
).
By Theorem 2 of
[
1
]
,
we have

\mathbf{P}(S_{l,j}>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha^{*}}(x)
for some
\alpha^{*}>1

(in fact, this has already been established at the end of the proof of Lemma
4.6
).
To proceed, let
\bar{F}(x)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\mathbf{P}(X_{l,j}(r)>x)
, and pick some
\alpha\in(1,\alpha^{*})
.
We consider some random variable
\tilde{B}
with tail cdf
\tilde{F}(x)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\mathbf{P}(\tilde{B}>x)

with some parameter
L>0
:

\displaystyle\tilde{F}(x)=\begin{cases}\bar{F}(x)\qquad&\text{ if }x\leq L\\
\bar{F}(x)\vee\frac{L^{\alpha}\bar{F}(L)}{x^{\alpha}}\qquad&\text{ if }x>L\end{cases}.

(D.17)

To conclude the proof, we only need to note the following:
(i) by definition, we have
\tilde{F}(x)\geq\bar{F}(x)
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
, which implies
X_{l,j}(r)\underset{\text{s.t.}}{\leq}\tilde{B}
;
(ii) by Assumption
2
, the support of
X_{l,j}(r)
is unbounded, so
\bar{F}(L)>0
for any
L\in(0,\infty)
;
(iii) due to
X_{l,j}(r)\underset{\text{s.t.}}{\leq}S_{l,j}
,
\mathbf{P}(S_{l,j}>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha^{*}}(x)
, and our choice of
\alpha\in(1,\alpha^{*})
,
it follows from Potter’s bound (see, e.g., Proposition 2.6 of
[
71
]
) that

\mathbf{P}(X_{l,j}(r)>x)=\bar{F}(x)<\frac{L^{\alpha}\bar{F}(L)}{x^{\alpha}}

eventually for any
x
large enough,
meaning that under the law specified in (
D.17
),

\tilde{B}
has a power-law tail with index
\alpha>1
;
and
(iv) since the expectation of
\tilde{B}
converges to
\mathbf{E}X_{l,j}(r)=\bar{b}^{[r]}_{j\leftarrow l}
as
L\to\infty
,
by picking
L
large enough we ensure that
\mathbf{E}\tilde{B}<(1+\epsilon)\bar{b}^{[r]}_{j\leftarrow l}
.
∎

The next result is in the same spirit of Lemma
4.6
, but focuses on
\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)
in (
D.15
).

Lemma D.3
.

Let Assumptions
2
–
4
hold,
and suppose that
\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}^{r}\right\rVert<1
holds for some positive integer
r
.
Given
\gamma>0
, there exist
\delta_{0}>0
and
C_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant;(m)}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>C_{0}\Bigg)=0,\qquad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}),\ i\in[d],

(D.18)

where the
\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant;(m)}_{i}(M)
’s are independent copies of
\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant}_{i}(M)
defined in (
D.15
).

Proof.

It suffices to fix some
i\in[d]
and show the existence of
C_{0}>0
,
\delta_{0}>0

such that (
D.18
) holds.
The proof is almost identical to that of Claim (
D.10
) in the proof of Lemma
4.6
.
Specifically, we set

C_{0}=d+\sum_{j\in[d]}\bar{s}_{i,j},

let
\phi_{c}(x)=x\wedge c
,
and observe that

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant;(m)}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>C_{0}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\underbrace{\bigg\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant;(m)}_{i}(n\delta)\right\rVert>n\Delta\text{ for some }m\in[n]\bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\text{(I)}}\cup\Bigg(\bigcup_{j\in[d]}\underbrace{\Bigg\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\phi_{n\Delta}\Big(S^{[r],\leqslant;(m)}_{i,j}(n\delta)\Big)>\bar{s}_{i,j}+1\Bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\text{(II:$j$)}}\Bigg).

Therefore, it suffices to find some
\Delta>0

such that, under any
\delta>0
small enough,
the terms

\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(I)}\big)

and (for each
j\in[d]
)

\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(II:$j$)}\big)

are of order
\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})
.

Proof of
\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(I)}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})
.
Applying Lemma
D.2
,
we know that given any
\Delta>0
, this claim holds for all
\delta>0
sufficiently small.

Proof of
\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(II:$j$)}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})
and the choice of
\Delta
.
By definitions in (
D.15
) and the stochastic comparison in (
4.3
),
we have

S^{[r],\leqslant}_{i,j}(n\delta)\underset{\text{s.t.}}{\leq}S^{\leqslant}_{i,j}(n\delta)\underset{\text{s.t.}}{\leq}S_{i,j}.

Using
S^{(m)}_{i,j}

to denote independent copies of
S_{i,j}
,
it suffices to find some
\Delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\phi_{n\Delta}\Big(S^{(m)}_{i,j}\Big)>\bar{s}_{i,j}+1\Bigg)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma}),\qquad\forall j\in[d].

(D.19)

Again, by Theorem 2 of
[
1
]
, we get

\mathbf{P}(S_{i,j}>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha^{*}}(x)
for some
\alpha^{*}>1
.
By Claim (
A.3
) of Lemma
A.1
and property (
A.1
), we conclude that (
D.19
) holds for any
\Delta>0
small enough.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove Lemma
4.5
for the general case.

Proof of Lemma
4.5
(General Case).

Under Assumption
1
,
we are able to apply Gelfand’s formula (see, e.g., p. 195 of
[
54
]
) and identify some positive integer
r
such that

\left\lVert\bar{\textbf{B}}^{r}\right\rVert<1
.
Also, in this proof we adopt the same labeling rule considered in Section
D.1
for multi-type branching trees:
that is,
given
j\in[d]
, all type-
j
nodes are numbered left to right, starting from generation 0, then continuing similarly in each subsequent generation.
To proceed, we make a few observations regarding the branching tree for
\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)
.

1.

Given a positive integer
k
and
q=0,1,\ldots,r-1
,
any node in the
(kr+q)^{\text{th}}
generation uniquely belongs to the sub-tree rooted at one of the nodes at the
q^{\text{th}}
generation.
This is equivalent to saying that each node in the
(kr+q)^{\text{th}}
has exactly one (grand)parent in the

q^{\text{th}}
generation.
As a convention, we also say that any node belongs to the sub-tree rooted at itself.

2.

Let
S_{j,l}^{[r;t,m],\leqslant}(n\delta)
be the count of type-
l
nodes at generation
t,r+t,2r+t,3r+t,\ldots
that belong to the sub-tree rooted at the
m^{\text{th}}
type-
j
node in the
t^{\text{th}}
generation.
Let

\bm{S}_{j}^{[r;t,m],\leqslant}(n\delta)=\big(S_{j,l}^{[r;t,m],\leqslant}(n\delta)\big)_{l\in[d]}.

A direct consequence of the previous bullet point is that

\displaystyle\bm{S}^{\leqslant}_{i}(n\delta)=\bm{S}_{i}^{[r;0,1],\leqslant}(n\delta)+\sum_{t=1}^{r-1}\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{X^{\leqslant}_{i,j}(t-1;n\delta)}\bm{S}^{[r;t,m],\leqslant}_{j}(n\delta).

(D.20)

Also, by definitions in (
D.15
),
we have
\bm{S}_{i}^{[r;0,1],\leqslant}(n\delta)=\bm{S}_{i}^{[r],\leqslant}(n\delta)
.

3.

The next fact follows from the independence of the offspring counts across different nodes:
for each
t=1,2,\ldots,r-1
and
j\in[d]
,
the sequence
\big(\bm{S}_{j}^{[r;t,m],\leqslant}(n\delta)\big)_{m\leq X^{\leqslant}_{i,j}(t-1;n\delta)}

are independent copies of
\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant}_{j}(n\delta)
defined in (
D.15
).
Henceforth in this proof, for each
m>X^{\leqslant}_{i,j}(t-1;n\delta)
we independently generate
\bm{S}_{j}^{[r;t,m],\leqslant}(n\delta)
as a generic copy of
\bm{S}^{[r],\leqslant}_{j}(n\delta)
,
so that the infinite sequence
\big(\bm{S}_{j}^{[r;t,m],\leqslant}(n\delta)\big)_{m\geq 1}
is well-defined
for each
j\in[d]
and
t=1,2,\ldots,r-1
.

Now,
take any
\Delta^{\prime},C_{0}>0
.
On the event

\displaystyle\underbrace{\bigg\{\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\leqslant}_{i}(t;n\delta)\right\rVert\leq n\Delta^{\prime}\ \forall t=1,2,\ldots,r-1\bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\text{(I)}}

\displaystyle\cap\underbrace{\bigg\{\left\lVert\bm{S}_{i}^{[r;0,1],\leqslant}(n\delta)\right\rVert\leq n\Delta^{\prime}\bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\text{(II)}}\cap\Bigg(\bigcap_{t\in[r-1],\ j\in[d]}\underbrace{\Bigg\{\left\lVert\frac{1}{\lfloor n\Delta^{\prime}\rfloor}\sum_{m=1}^{\lfloor n\Delta^{\prime}\rfloor}\bm{S}^{[r;t,m],\leqslant}_{j}(n\delta)\right\rVert\leq C_{0}\Bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}(\text{III}:t,j)}\Bigg),

it follows from (
D.20
) that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{S}_{j}^{\leqslant}(n\delta)\right\rVert\leq n\Delta^{\prime}+(r-1)\cdot d\cdot n\Delta^{\prime}\cdot C_{0}=n\Delta^{\prime}\cdot\big[1+(r-1)d\cdot C_{0}\big].

(D.21)

Therefore,
to prove Claim (
4.28
) given
i\in[d]
and
\Delta>0
,
it suffices to find
C_{0},\Delta^{\prime}>0
such that

•

\Delta^{\prime}\cdot\big[1+(r-1)d\cdot C_{0}\big]<\Delta

(so the RHS of (
D.21
) is upper bounded by
n\Delta
);

•

for any
\delta>0
small enough,
the terms

\mathbf{P}\big((\text{(I)})^{c}\big),

\mathbf{P}\big((\text{(II)})^{c}\big),

and (for each
t\in[r-1]
,
j\in[d]
)

\mathbf{P}\big((\text{(III:$t,j$)})^{c}\big)

are of order
\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})
.

Proof of

\mathbf{P}\big((\text{(III:$t,j$)})^{c}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})

and the choice of
C_{0}
,
\Delta^{\prime}
.
Let
C_{0}
be characterized as in Lemma
D.3
,
based on which fix some
\Delta^{\prime}>0
small enough such that

\Delta^{\prime}\cdot\big[1+(r-1)d\cdot C_{0}\big]<\Delta.

By Lemma
D.3

and the observation (iii) above,
we have

\mathbf{P}\big((\text{(III:$t,j$)})^{c}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})

under any
\delta>0
small enough.

Proof of

\mathbf{P}\big((\text{(I)})^{c}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})
. This follows from Lemma
D.1
.

Proof of

\mathbf{P}\big((\text{(II)})^{c}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\gamma})
.
This follows from Lemma
D.2
.
∎

D.3
Proofs of Lemmas
4.10
–
4.13

Next, we provide the proofs of Lemmas
4.10
and
4.11
.

Proof of Lemma
4.10
.

Part (i) is an immediate consequence of
\mathscr{I}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
and property (
4.31
).
Next, we prove part (ii):
that is, given
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})\setminus\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
,
we must have
|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}|\geq 2
.
By (
4.31
),

\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})=\{\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}:\ \bm{j}^{\bm{I}}=\bm{j}\}=\{\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}:\ \bm{j}^{\bm{I}}=\bm{j},\ \tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})=\alpha(\bm{j})\}.

Then, due to
\mathscr{I}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}
,
for any
\bm{I}=(I_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})\setminus\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
we must have
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}\setminus\mathscr{I}
.
Due to
\bm{j}\neq\emptyset
,
by comparing Definition
3.1
with Definition
4.1
,
at least one of the following two cases must occur:

1.

the set
\{j\in[d]:\ I_{1,j}=1\}
contains at least two elements;

2.

there exists
j\in\bm{j}
such that
|\{k\geq 1:\ I_{k,j}=1\}|\geq 2
.

To prove part (ii), it suffices to show that case (b) cannot occur for any
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})
.
Specifically,
suppose that
|\{k\geq 1:\ I_{k,j^{*}}=1\}|\geq 2
for some
j^{*}\in\bm{j}
.
Then, by (
4.29
),

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}(\bm{I})

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\geq 1}I_{k,j}\cdot\big(\alpha^{*}(j)-1\big)

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{j\in\bm{j}}\big(\alpha^{*}(j)-1\big)\cdot\big|\{k\geq 1:\ I_{k,j}=1\}\big|\qquad\text{due to }\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}=\bm{j}

\displaystyle>1+\sum_{j\in\bm{j}}\big(\alpha^{*}(j)-1\big)\qquad\text{due to $|\{k\geq 1:\ I_{k,j^{*}}=1\}|\geq 2$ and $\alpha^{*}(j)>1\ \forall j\in[d]$}

\displaystyle=\alpha(\bm{j})\qquad\text{by definitions in \eqref{def: cost function, cone, cluster}}.

However, this leads to the contradiction
\bm{I}\notin\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})
.
In summary, case (a) must occur for any
\bm{I}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{I}}(\bm{j})\setminus\mathscr{I}(\bm{j})
, which verifies part (ii) of this lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.11
.

(a) The claims are equivalent to the following: there exist
\bar{\epsilon}>0
and
\bar{\delta}>0
such that for any

\bm{x}=\sum_{i\in\bm{j}}w_{i}\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}
with
w_{i}\geq 0\ \forall i\in\bm{j}
and
\Phi(\bm{x})\in B
, we must have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\bm{j}}w_{j}

\displaystyle>\bar{\epsilon},

(D.22)

\displaystyle\min_{j\in\bm{j}}\frac{w_{j}}{\sum_{i\in\bm{j}}w_{i}}

\displaystyle>\bar{\delta}.

(D.23)

First, since
B
is bounded away from
\mathbb{C}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})
under
\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}
,
there exists
r_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle(r,\theta)\in B\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad r>r_{0}.

(D.24)

Next, consider some
\bm{x}=\sum_{i\in\bm{j}}w_{i}\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}
with
w_{i}\geq 0\ \forall i\in\bm{j}
and
\Phi(\bm{x})\in B
.
By (
D.24
), we must have
\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>r_{0}
.
On the other hand, for the
L_{1}
norm
\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert
, we have

\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert\leq\max_{i\in\bm{j}}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}\right\rVert\cdot\sum_{j\in\bm{j}}w_{j},

and hence

\frac{r_{0}}{\max_{j\in\bm{j}}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert}<\sum_{j\in\bm{j}}w_{j}.

In summary, Claim (
D.22
) holds for any
\bar{\epsilon}>0
small enough such that
\bar{\epsilon}<\frac{r_{0}}{\max_{j\in\bm{j}}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert}
.

Next, since
B
is bounded away from
\mathbb{C}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})
under
\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}
, there exists some
\Delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}\big(B,\mathbb{C}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})\big)>\Delta.

(D.25)

We show that Claim (
D.23
) holds for any
\bar{\delta}>0
small enough that satisfies

\displaystyle\bar{\delta}\cdot\frac{\max_{j\in\bm{j}}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert}{\min_{j\in\bm{j}}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert}<\frac{\Delta}{2},\qquad\bar{\delta}<1.

(D.26)

To proceed,
we consider a proof by contradiction.
Suppose that for some
\bm{x}=\sum_{i\in\bm{j}}w_{i}\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}
with
w_{i}\geq 0\ \forall i\in\bm{j}
and
\Phi(\bm{x})\in B
,
there exists
j^{*}\in\bm{j}
such that Claim (
D.23
) does not hold, i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{w_{j^{*}}}{\sum_{i\in\bm{j}}w_{i}}\leq\bar{\delta}.

(D.27)

We first note that the set
\bm{j}\setminus\{j^{*}\}
cannot be empty;
otherwise, we have
\bm{j}=\{j^{*}\}
and arrive at the contradiction that
\frac{w_{j^{*}}}{\sum_{i\in\bm{j}}w_{i}}=1>\bar{\delta}
.
Next, we define

\bm{x}^{*}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}\setminus\{j^{*}\}}w_{j}\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}
,
and note that
\left\lVert\bm{x}^{*}\right\rVert>0
.
Let
(r,\theta)=\Phi(\bm{x})
and
(r^{*},\theta^{*})=\Phi(\bm{x}^{*})
, and observe that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\theta-\theta^{*}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\frac{\bm{x}}{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert}-\frac{\bm{x}^{*}}{\left\lVert\bm{x}^{*}\right\rVert}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\frac{\bm{x}}{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert}-\frac{\bm{x}^{*}}{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\frac{\bm{x}^{*}}{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert}-\frac{\bm{x}^{*}}{\left\lVert\bm{x}^{*}\right\rVert}\right\rVert\leq 2\cdot\frac{\left\lVert\bm{x}-\bm{x}^{*}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert}

\displaystyle=2\cdot\frac{w_{j^{*}}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j^{*}}\right\rVert}{\sum_{j\in\bm{j}}w_{j}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert}\leq 2\cdot\frac{w_{j^{*}}}{\sum_{j\in\bm{j}}w_{j}}\cdot\frac{\max_{j\in\bm{j}}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert}{\min_{j\in\bm{j}}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right\rVert}\leq 2\cdot\frac{\Delta}{2}=\Delta.

The last inequality in the display above follows from our choice of
\bar{\delta}
in (
D.26
)
and the condition (
D.27
) for the proof by contraction.
Now, consider
\bm{x}^{*}\cdot\frac{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert\bm{x}^{*}\right\rVert}
, i.e., a stretched version of the vector
\bm{x}^{*}
with
L_{1}
norm matching
\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert
.
Due to
\bm{x}^{*}=\sum_{j\in\bm{j}\setminus\{j^{*}\}}w_{j}\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}
, we have
\bm{x}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})

and
\Phi(\bm{x}^{*})\in\mathbb{C}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j})
,
thus implying

\Phi\big(\bm{x}^{*}\cdot\frac{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert\bm{x}^{*}\right\rVert}\big)\in\mathbb{C}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j});
see (
4.24
).
However, due to

\Phi(\bm{x}^{*}\cdot\frac{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert\bm{x}^{*}\right\rVert})=(r,\theta^{*}),

we arrive at

\bm{d}_{\textbf{U}}\big(\Phi(\bm{x}),\Phi(\bm{x}^{*}\cdot\frac{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert\bm{x}^{*}\right\rVert})\big)=\left\lVert\theta-\theta^{*}\right\rVert\leq\Delta,

which contradicts (
D.25
) since
\Phi(\bm{x})\in B
.3
This concludes the proof of Claim (
D.23
).

(b) We fix some type
\bm{I}\in\mathscr{I}
with active index set
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}=\bm{j}
.
Due to
\bm{j}\neq\emptyset
, we have
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}\geq 1
; see Definition
3.1
and Remark
2
.
Henceforth in this proof,
we write
\bm{w}_{k}=(w_{k,j})_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}
and
\bm{w}=(\bm{w}_{k})_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}
.
Using results in part (a), one can fix some constants
\bar{\epsilon}>0
and
\bar{\delta}\in(0,1)
such that the following holds:
for any
\bm{x}=\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\sum_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}w_{k,j}\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}
with
w_{k,j}\geq 0
and
\bm{x}\in\Phi^{-1}(B)
,
we must have
\bm{w}\in B(\bar{\epsilon},\bar{\delta})
where

\displaystyle B(\bar{\epsilon},\bar{\delta})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\left\{\rule{0.0pt}{25.6073pt}\bm{w}\in[0,\infty)^{|\bm{j}|}:\ \min_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}w_{k,j}\geq\bar{\epsilon};\ \min_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\\
k^{\prime}\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j^{\prime}\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\frac{w_{k,j}}{w_{k^{\prime},j^{\prime}}}\geq\bar{\delta}\right\}.

Then, by the definition of
\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}
in (
3.11
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}\circ\Phi^{-1}(B)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}\big(\Phi^{-1}(B)\big)\leq\int\mathbbm{I}\Big\{\bm{w}\in B(\bar{\epsilon},\bar{\delta})\Big\}\cdot\Bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1}g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{w}_{k})\Bigg)\nu^{\bm{I}}(d\bm{w}).

Note also that there uniquely exists some
j^{\bm{I}}_{1}\in[d]
such that
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}=\{j^{\bm{I}}_{1}\}
;
see Remark
2
.
We fix some
\rho\in(1,\infty)
, and let

\displaystyle B_{0}(\rho,\bar{\delta})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\left\{\rule{0.0pt}{25.6073pt}\bm{w}\in[0,\infty)^{|\bm{j}|}:\ w_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}\in[1,\rho),\ \min_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\\
k^{\prime}\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j^{\prime}\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\frac{w_{k,j}}{w_{k^{\prime},j^{\prime}}}\geq\bar{\delta}\right\},

\displaystyle B_{n}(\rho,\delta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\rho^{n}B_{0}(\rho,\bar{\delta})

\displaystyle=\left\{\rule{0.0pt}{25.6073pt}\bm{w}\in[0,\infty)^{|\bm{j}|}:\ w_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}\in[\rho^{n},\rho^{n+1}),\ \min_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\\
k^{\prime}\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j^{\prime}\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\frac{w_{k,j}}{w_{k^{\prime},j^{\prime}}}\geq\bar{\delta}\right\},\quad n\in\mathbb{Z}.

For any
N
large enough we have
\rho^{-N}<\bar{\epsilon}
.
This leads to
B(\bar{\epsilon},\bar{\delta})\subseteq\bigcup_{n=-N}^{\infty}B_{n}(\rho,\delta)
,
and

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\bm{I}}\circ\Phi^{-1}(B)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=-N}^{\infty}\underbrace{\int\mathbbm{I}\Big\{\bm{w}\in B_{n}(\rho,\bar{\delta})\Big\}\cdot\Bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1}g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{w}_{k})\Bigg)\nu^{\bm{I}}(d\bm{w})}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}c_{n}}.

Therefore, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle c_{0}<\infty,

(D.28)

and that there exists some
\hat{\rho}\in(1,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle c_{n}\leq\hat{\rho}^{-n}\cdot c_{0},\qquad\forall n\in\mathbb{Z}.

(D.29)

Proof of Claim (
D.28
)
.
Note that

B_{0}(\rho,\bar{\delta})\subseteq\big\{\bm{w}\in[0,\infty)^{|\bm{j}|}:w_{k,j}\in[\bar{\delta},\rho/\bar{\delta}]\ \forall k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\big\}.

Also, by the continuity of
g_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}(\cdot)
(see (
3.9
)),
we can fix some
M\in(0,\infty)
such that

0\leq g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{w}_{k})\leq M

for any
k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]
and
\bm{w}_{k}=(w_{k,j})_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}
with
w_{k,j}\in[\bar{\delta},\rho/\bar{\delta}]
for all
j
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle c_{0}

\displaystyle\leq M^{|\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}|-1}\int\mathbbm{I}\Big\{\bm{w}\in[0,\infty)^{|\bm{j}|}:w_{k,j}\in[\bar{\delta},\rho/\bar{\delta}]\ \forall k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\Big\}\nu^{\bm{I}}(d\bm{w})

\displaystyle=M^{|\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}|-1}\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\Big({\bar{\delta}^{-\alpha^{*}(j)}}-{(\rho/\bar{\delta})^{-\alpha^{*}(j)}}\Big)<\infty.

Proof of Claim (
D.29
)
.
By (
3.9
),
for any
a>0
we have

g_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}(a\bm{w})=g_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}(\bm{w})\cdot a^{|\mathcal{J}|}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle c_{n}

\displaystyle=\int_{\bm{w}\in B_{n}(\rho,\bar{\delta})}\Bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1}g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{w}_{k})\Bigg)\nu^{\bm{I}}(d\bm{w})=\int_{\bm{w}\in\rho^{n}B_{0}(\rho,\bar{\delta})}\Bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1}g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{w}_{k})\Bigg)\nu^{\bm{I}}(d\bm{w})

\displaystyle=\int_{\bm{x}\in B_{0}(\rho,\bar{\delta})}\Bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1}g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\rho^{n}\bm{x}_{k})\Bigg)\nu^{\bm{I}}(d\rho^{n}\bm{x})\qquad\text{by setting }\bm{w}=\rho^{n}\bm{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\bm{x}\in B_{0}(\rho,\bar{\delta})}\Bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1}\rho^{n|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}|}\cdot g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{x}_{k})\Bigg)\nu^{\bm{I}}(d\rho^{n}\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\int_{\bm{x}\in B_{0}(\rho,\bar{\delta})}\Bigg[\bigtimes_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1}\Bigg(\rho^{n|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}|}\cdot g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{x}_{k})\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\frac{\alpha^{*}(j)\rho^{n}dx_{k,j}}{(\rho^{n}x_{k,j})^{\alpha^{*}(j)+1}}\Bigg)\Bigg]\cdot\Bigg(\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}}\frac{\alpha^{*}(j)\rho^{n}dx_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j}}{(\rho^{n}x_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j})^{\alpha^{*}(j)+1}}\Bigg)

by definitions in (
3.7
) and (
3.10
)

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}}\rho^{-n\alpha^{*}(j)}\Bigg)\cdot\Bigg(\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\rho^{-n(\alpha^{*}(j)-1)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\underbrace{\int_{\bm{x}\in B_{0}(\rho,\bar{\delta})}\Bigg(\bigtimes_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1}g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{x}_{k})\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\frac{\alpha^{*}(j)dx_{k,j}}{x_{k,j}^{\alpha^{*}(j)+1}}\Bigg)\cdot\Bigg(\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}}\frac{\alpha^{*}(j)dx_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j}}{x_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j}^{\alpha^{*}(j)+1}}\Bigg)}_{=c_{0}}.

Therefore, to conclude the proof of Claim (
D.29
), one only needs to pick

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Bigg(\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}}\rho^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\Bigg)\cdot\Bigg(\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\rho^{(\alpha^{*}(j)-1)}\Bigg).

In particular, recall that
\alpha^{*}(j)>1\ \forall j\in[d]
; see Assumption
2
and (
3.3
).
Then, by our choice of
\rho\in(1,\infty)
, we get
\hat{\rho}\in(1,\infty)
.
∎

For the proofs of
Lemmas
4.12
and
4.13
, we prepare one more result.
Recall the definitions of
N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}
and
W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}
in (
4.37
),
which are sums of i.i.d. copies of
N^{>}_{i;j}(\cdot)
and
W^{>}_{i;j}(\cdot)
defined in (
3.21
)–(
3.24
).
Besides, recall the definition of the assignments of
\mathcal{J}
to
\mathcal{I}
in (
3.8
), and that we use
{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}
to denote the set of all assignments of
\mathcal{J}
to
\mathcal{I}
.
For any non-empty
\mathcal{I}\subseteq[d]
,
\mathcal{J}\subseteq[d]
, we define

\displaystyle C_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big((t_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\big)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\cdot t_{i}^{1-\alpha^{*}(j)}.

(D.30)

Under Assumption
2
, we have
\alpha^{*}(j)>1
for each
j\in[d]
(see (
3.3
)),
so
C_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big((t_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\big)
is monotone decreasing w.r.t. each
t_{i}
.
If
\mathcal{J}=\emptyset
,
we adopt the convention that

C_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\emptyset}\big((t_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\big)\equiv 1.

Likewise, for the function
g_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}
defined in (
3.9
),
we adopt the convention that

g_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\emptyset}(\bm{w})\equiv 1.

Also, we use
\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}

to denote the set of all assignment from
\mathcal{J}
to
\mathcal{I}
,
allowing for replacements
:
that is,

\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}

contains all
\{\mathcal{J}(i)\subseteq\mathcal{J}:\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}

satisfying
\bigcup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathcal{J}(i)=\mathcal{J}
.
Note that
\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\subset\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}

and
|\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}|<\infty
given
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J}\subseteq[d]
.
In the next result, we write
\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})=(t_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}
.

Lemma D.4
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.
Let
0<c<C<\infty
.
Let

\mathcal{I}\subseteq\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
be non-empty,
and let

\mathcal{J}\subseteq\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
.
There exists some
\delta_{0}>0
such that
for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I}):\ t_{i}\geq nc\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I}}\Bigg|\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\big)}{C_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)\cdot\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)}-1\Bigg|

\displaystyle=0,

(D.31)

where
C_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}(\bm{t})
is defined in (
D.30
),
and
l^{*}(j)
is defined in (
3.3
).
Furthermore,
if
\mathcal{J}\neq\emptyset
, for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t_{i}\in[nc,nC]\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I}}\ \sup_{{nx_{j}}/{t_{i}}\in[c,C]\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I},j\in\mathcal{J}}

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}>nx_{j}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}\ \big|\ N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\big)}{\frac{g_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}(n^{-1}\bm{t}(\mathcal{I}))}{C_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}(n^{-1}\bm{t}(\mathcal{I}))}\cdot\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}({\delta}/{x_{j}})^{\alpha^{*}(j)}}\ -1\Bigg|

(D.32)

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t_{i}\geq nc\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I}}\ \sup_{{nx_{j}}/{t_{i}}\in[c,C]\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I},j\in\mathcal{J}}

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}>nx_{j}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}\ \big|\ N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\big)}{\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}({\delta t_{i}}/{nx_{j}})^{\alpha^{*}(j)}}\leq 1

(D.33)

where
g_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}(\cdot)
is defined in (
3.9
).

Proof.

First,
we note that for the proof of Claim (
D.31
),
we only need to consider non-empty
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
.
To see why, note that to prove (
D.31
) under
\mathcal{J}=\emptyset
,
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\inf_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I}):\ t_{i}\geq nc\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}=0\ \forall j\in[d]\Big)=1.

(D.34)

Suppose that (
D.31
) holds for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
and any non-empty
\mathcal{J}\subseteq[d]
.
Then,
by Assumption
2
and definitions in (
3.3
),
in (
D.31
) it holds for any
T\geq nc
that

\displaystyle n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>T\delta)\leq n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\cdot c\delta)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-(\alpha^{*}(j)-1)}(n),\quad\text{with }\alpha^{*}(j)>1.

Then, (
D.31
) implies

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\max_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I}):\ t_{i}\geq nc\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\Big)=0,\qquad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}),\ \emptyset\neq\mathcal{J}\subseteq[d].

Under any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,
the Claim (
D.34
) then follows from the preliminary bound

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}=0\ \forall j\in[d]\Big)\geq 1-\sum_{\mathcal{J}\subseteq[d]:\ \mathcal{J}\neq\emptyset}\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\Big).

Next,
we note that it suffices to prove (
D.31
)–(
D.33
)
for
\mathcal{I}=\{i\}
with
i\in[d]
(i.e., the case of
|\mathcal{I}|=1
).
In particular,
it suffices to identify
\delta_{0}>0
such that for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

i\in[d]
, and non-empty
\mathcal{J}\subseteq[d]
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{T\geq nc}

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\sum_{m=1}^{T}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta T)\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\big)}{\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}(n^{-1}T)^{1-\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\cdot n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)}-1\Bigg|=0,

(D.35)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{T\geq nc}\ \sup_{nx_{j}/T\in[c,C]\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}}

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\sum_{m=1}^{T}W^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta T)>nx_{j}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}\ \big|\ \sum_{m=1}^{T}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta T)\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\big)}{\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\big(\frac{\delta T}{nx_{j}}\big)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-1\Bigg|=0.

(D.36)

To see how these claims
lead to
(
D.31
)–(
D.33
),
recall that we use
\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}

to denote the set containing all
\{\mathcal{J}(i)\subseteq\mathcal{J}:\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}

satisfying
\bigcup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathcal{J}(i)=\mathcal{J}
.
Also,
recall that we use
{\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}
to denote the set of all assignments of
\mathcal{J}
to
\mathcal{I}
.
By definitions in (
3.8
),
we have

\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\subset\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}

and
|\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}|<\infty
given
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J}\subseteq[d]
.
Next, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big({N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\Big)=\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\Bigg)\quad\text{by \eqref{def, proof cluster size, N W mathcal I mathcal J bcdot j}}

(D.37)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\Bigg)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\text{(I)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\setminus\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\Bigg)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\text{(II)}}.

Given
\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}
,
by the definition of partitions (i.e., the
\mathcal{J}(i)
’s are mutually disjoint, and
\bigcup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathcal{J}(i)=\mathcal{J}
), we have

\displaystyle\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}t_{i}^{1-\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\cdot n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)

(D.38)

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}t_{i}^{1-\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\Bigg)\cdot\Bigg(\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\Bigg).

By the definition in (
D.30
),

\displaystyle\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}t_{i}^{1-\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\cdot n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big((t_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\big)\cdot\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta).

Then, applying the uniform convergence (
D.35
)
for each
\mathbf{P}\big(\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\big)

in term (I) of the display (
D.37
),
we get

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I}):\ t_{i}\geq nc\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I}}\Bigg|\frac{\text{(I)}}{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)\cdot\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)}-1\Bigg|=0.

(D.39)

Next, to bound the term (II), we note that for each
\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\setminus\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}
,
we must have
\mathcal{J}(i)\cap\mathcal{J}(i^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
for some
i,i^{\prime}\in[d]
with
i\neq i^{\prime}
:
this is because
\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}
is
not
a partition of
\mathcal{J}

but still satisfies
\bigcup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathcal{J}(i)=\mathcal{J}
and
\mathcal{J}(i)\subseteq\mathcal{J}\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I}
.
This has two useful implications.
First, due to
\mathcal{J}(i)\cap\mathcal{J}(i^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
for some
i\neq i^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)=\mathit{o}\Bigg(\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\Bigg),\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(D.40)

Second,
for each
\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\setminus\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}
,
we can find some

\{\hat{\mathcal{J}}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}

such that
\hat{\mathcal{J}}(i)\subseteq\mathcal{J}(i)\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I}
.
In particular, there exists some
\hat{i}\in\mathcal{I}
and
\hat{j}\in\mathcal{J}(\hat{i})
such that
\hat{j}\notin\hat{\mathcal{J}}(\hat{i})
.
As a result,
for each
n
and each
\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})=(t_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}
with
t_{i}\geq nc\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}(n^{-1}t_{i})^{1-\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}}{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)}

(D.41)
