\displaystyle\leq\frac{\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}(n^{-1}t_{i})^{1-\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}}{\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\hat{\mathcal{J}}(i)}(n^{-1}t_{i})^{1-\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}}\quad\text{by definitions in \eqref{def: function C J assigned to I, cluster size}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\setminus\hat{\mathcal{J}}(i)}(n^{-1}t_{i})^{1-\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\leq\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\setminus\hat{\mathcal{J}}(i)}c^{1-\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}<\infty,

where in the last line we applied
\alpha^{*}(j)>1\ \forall j\in[d]
.
Applying the uniform convergence (
D.35
)
for each
\mathbf{P}\big(\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\big)

in term (II) of the display (
D.37
),
it follows from (
D.40
) and (
D.41
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I}):\ t_{i}\geq nc\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{\text{(II)}}{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)\cdot\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)}=0.

(D.42)

Combining (
D.39
) and (
D.42
),
we establish (
D.31
).

To proceed, we
define the event

\displaystyle E_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n,\delta,\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\bigcup_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\Bigg\{\text{for each $i\in\mathcal{I}$},\ \sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\Bigg\},

and
note that our analysis above for terms (I) and (II) in display (
D.37
)
implies

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\inf_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I}):\ t_{i}\geq nc\ \forall i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbf{P}\Big(E_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n,\delta,\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)\ \Big|\ {N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\Big)=1.

Therefore, it is equivalent to prove a modified version of Claims (
D.32
) and (
D.33
),
where we condition on the event
E_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n,\delta,\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)

instead of
\{{N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\}
.
For Claim (
D.32
),
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}>nx_{j}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}\ \bigg|\ E_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n,\delta,\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)\bigg)

(D.43)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}>nx_{j}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}\ \Bigg|\ \text{for each $i\in\mathcal{I}$},\ \sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\text{for each $i\in\mathcal{I}$},\ \sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\ \Bigg|\ E_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n,\delta,\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{=}}\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{25.6073pt}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}W^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})>nx_{j}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\ \Bigg|\ \sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\Bigg)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\text{for each $i\in\mathcal{I}$},\ \sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\ \Bigg|\ E_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n,\delta,\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{25.6073pt}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}W^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})>nx_{j}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\ \Bigg|\ \sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\Bigg)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\frac{\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbf{P}\big(\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\big)}{\mathbf{P}\big(E_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n,\delta,\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)\big)}.

Here, the step
(*)
follows from the independence of

\big\{\big(N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(M),W^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(M)\big)_{j\in[d]}:\ m\geq 1\big\}

across
i\in[d]
; see (
4.36
).
Then by applying (
D.31
), (
D.35
),
and
(
D.36
),
under any
\delta>0
small enough,
it holds
uniformly over
t_{i}\in[nc,nC]

and
\frac{nx_{j}}{t_{i}}\in[c,C]
—in the sense of (
D.32
)—that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}>nx_{j}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}\ \bigg|\ E_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n,\delta,\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\sim\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\Bigg[\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}\bigg(\frac{\delta t_{i}}{nx_{j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\Bigg]\cdot\frac{\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}(n^{-1}t_{i})^{1-\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\cdot n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)}{C_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)\cdot\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\bigg(\frac{\delta}{x_{j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\Bigg]\cdot\frac{\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}(n^{-1}t_{i})\cdot\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}}{C_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)}\qquad\text{by \eqref{proof, section D, property of partition}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\bigg(\frac{\delta}{x_{j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\Bigg]\cdot\frac{g_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)}{C_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)}\qquad\text{by the definition in \eqref{def: function g mathcal I mathcal J, for measure C i bm I, cluster size}}

as
n\to\infty
.
This verifies Claim (
D.32
).
Furthermore, from the last line of display (
D.43
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}>nx_{j}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}\ \bigg|\ E_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big(n,\delta,\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\mathcal{I}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{25.6073pt}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}W^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})>nx_{j}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\ \Bigg|\ \sum_{m=1}^{t_{i}}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta t_{i})\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}(i)\Bigg)\right].

Applying (
D.36
), we verify Claim (
D.33
) for any
\delta>0
small enough.
In summary, we have shown that
it suffices to prove Claims (
D.35
)
and
(
D.36
).
In the remainder of this proof, we establish the Claims (
D.35
)
and
(
D.36
),
i.e., addressing the case where
|\mathcal{I}|=1
.

Proof of Claim (
D.35
)
.
Let
{\mathbb{J}}
be the set of all partitions of the non-empty
\mathcal{J}\subseteq[d]
.
Given any partition
\mathscr{J}=\{\mathcal{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{J}_{k}\}\in\mathbb{J}
,
let the event
A^{\mathscr{J}}_{n}(T,\delta)
be defined as in (
4.112
).
Our proof is based on the decomposition
of events in (
4.113
).
We first prove an upper bound.
Let

p(i,M,\mathcal{T})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j}(M)\geq 1\ \forall j\in\mathcal{T}\big).

Given any
T\in\mathbb{N}
and partition
\mathscr{J}=\{\mathcal{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{J}_{k}\}\in\mathbb{J}
,
it has been shown in (
4.114
) that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(A^{\mathscr{J}}_{n}(T,\delta)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{l\in[k]}T\cdot p(i,\delta T,\mathcal{J}_{l}).

(D.44)

Specifically, consider
the singleton-partition

\mathscr{J}_{*}=\{\{j\}:\ j\in\mathcal{J}\}.

By Lemma
4.7
(i),
there exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle p(i,n\delta,\{j\})\sim\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\text{ as }n\to\infty,\qquad\forall j\in[d],\ \delta\in(0,\delta_{0}).

It then follows from (
D.44
) that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{T\geq nc}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(A^{\mathscr{J}_{*}}_{n}(T,\delta)\big)}{(n^{-1}T)^{|\mathcal{J}|}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\cdot n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>T\delta)}\leq 1,\qquad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}).

Next,
we consider some partition
\mathscr{J}=\{\mathcal{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{J}_{k}\}\in\mathbb{J}\setminus\{\mathscr{J}_{*}\}
.
Due to
\mathscr{J}\neq\mathscr{J}_{*}
,
there must be some
l\in[k]
such that
\mathcal{J}_{l}
contains at least two elements.
By part (ii) of Lemma
4.7
,
(and picking a smaller
\delta_{0}>0
if needed)

\displaystyle p(i,n\delta,\widetilde{\mathcal{J}})=\mathit{o}\Bigg(n^{|\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}|-1}\prod_{j\in\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}}\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta\big)\Bigg),\quad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}),\ \widetilde{\mathcal{J}}\subseteq[d]\text{ with }|\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}|\geq 2.

Therefore, for any partition
\mathscr{J}=\{\mathcal{J}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{J}_{k}\}\in\mathbb{J}\setminus\{\mathscr{J}_{*}\}
,
we have

\displaystyle\prod_{l\in[k]}np(i,n\delta,\mathcal{J}_{l})=\mathit{o}\Bigg(n^{|\mathcal{J}|}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta\big)\Bigg),\quad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}).

Then, by (
D.44
),

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{T\geq nc}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(A_{n}^{\mathscr{J}}(T,\delta)\big)}{\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>T\delta)}=0,\quad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}),\ \mathscr{J}\in\mathbb{J}\setminus\{\mathscr{J}_{*}\}.

Using the decomposition of events in (
4.113
),
we arrive at the upper bound

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{T\geq nc}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\sum_{m=1}^{T}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta T)\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\big)}{(n^{-1}T)^{|\mathcal{J}|}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\cdot n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>T\delta)}\leq 1,\quad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}).

(D.45)

We proceed similarly for the derivation of the lower bound.
In particular, note that

\big\{\sum_{m=1}^{T}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta T)\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\big\}\supseteq\hat{A}(T,\delta),

where

\displaystyle\hat{A}(T,\delta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\bigg\{\exists\{m_{1},m_{2},\ldots,m_{|\mathcal{J}|}\}\subseteq[T]\text{ such that }

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad N^{>,(m_{j})}_{i;j}(\delta T)=1,\ \sum_{l\in[d]:\ l\neq j}N^{>,(m_{j})}_{i;l}(\delta T)=0\ \forall j=1,\ldots,|\mathcal{J}|;

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\sum_{l\in[d]}N^{>,(m)}_{i;l}(\delta T)=0\ \forall m\in[T]\setminus\{m_{j}:\ j\in\mathcal{J}\}\bigg\}.

For clarity of the notations in the display below, we write

k=|\mathcal{J}|
,
\mathcal{J}=\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}\}
,
and

\displaystyle\hat{p}(i,M,j)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i:j}(M)=1,\ N^{>}_{i:j^{\prime}}(M)=0\ \forall j^{\prime}\neq j\big),

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{*}(i,M)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i:j}(M)\geq 1\text{ for some }j\in[d]\big).

Since the sequence
\big(N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta T)\big)_{m\geq 1}
are i.i.d. copies,
by the law of multinomial distributions,
it holds
for any
T\geq\lfloor nc\rfloor
that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(\sum_{m=1}^{T}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta T)\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\big)\geq

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{P}\big(\hat{A}(T,\delta)\big)=\frac{T!}{(T-k)!}\cdot\Bigg[\prod_{l\in[k]}\hat{p}(i,\delta T,j_{l})\cdot\Bigg]\cdot\Big(1-\hat{p}_{*}(i,\delta T)\Big)^{T-k}

\displaystyle\geq\Bigg(\frac{\lfloor nc\rfloor-k}{\lfloor nc\rfloor}\Bigg)^{k}\cdot T^{k}\cdot\Bigg[\prod_{l\in[k]}\hat{p}(i,\delta T,j_{l})\cdot\Bigg]\cdot\Big(1-\hat{p}_{*}(i,\delta T)\Big)^{T}\qquad\text{due to }T\geq\lfloor nc\rfloor

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\frac{\lfloor nc\rfloor-k}{\lfloor nc\rfloor}\Bigg)^{k}\cdot\Big(1-\hat{p}_{*}(i,\delta T)\Big)^{T}\cdot(n^{-1}T)^{k}\prod_{l\in[k]}n\hat{p}(i,\delta T,j_{l}).

By part (i) and part (ii) of Lemma
4.7
,
there exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\hat{p}(i,T\delta,j)\sim\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>T\delta)\quad\text{as }T\to\infty,\qquad\forall j\in[d],\ \delta\in(0,\delta_{0}).

Analogously, using part (i) of Lemma
4.7

(and by picking a smaller
\delta_{0}>0
if needed),
it holds for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
that (as
T\to\infty
)

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{*}(i,\delta T)\leq\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>}_{i;j}(T\delta)\geq 1\big)=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>T\delta)\Bigg)=\mathit{o}\big(T^{-\tilde{\alpha}}\big),\quad\forall\tilde{\alpha}\in\big(1,\min_{i\in[d],j\in[d]}\alpha_{i\leftarrow j}\big).

As a result, we get

\lim_{n\to\infty}\inf_{T\geq\lfloor nc\rfloor}\big(1-\hat{p}_{*}(i,\delta T)\big)^{T}=1.

We then arrive at the lower bound (under any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
)

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\inf_{T\geq\lfloor nc\rfloor}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\sum_{m=1}^{T}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta T)\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\big)}{(n^{-1}T)^{|\mathcal{J}|}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}\cdot n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>T\delta)}\geq 1.

(D.46)

By the uniform convergence theorem (e.g., Proposition 2.4 of
[
71
]
),

\frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>t\cdot n\delta)}{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)}\to t^{-\alpha^{*}(j)}\ \text{ as }n\to\infty,\quad\text{uniformly over }t\in[c,\infty).

Plugging such uniform convergence into the bounds
(
D.45
) and (
D.46
), we conclude the proof for Claim (
D.35
).

Proof of Claim (
D.36
)
.
In essence, the proof above for Claim (
D.35
) regarding the event
\hat{A}(T,\delta)
has verified that

\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\big(\hat{A}(T,\delta)\ \big|\ \sum_{m=1}^{T}N^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta T)\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\mathcal{J}\big)=1

for any
\delta>0
small enough.
Therefore, to prove Claim (
D.36
),
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{T\geq nc}\ \sup_{\frac{nx_{j}}{T}\in[c,C]\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}}

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\sum_{m=1}^{T}W^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta T)>nx_{j}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}\ \big|\ \hat{A}(T,\delta)\big)}{\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\big(\frac{\delta T}{nx_{j}}\big)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}}-1\Bigg|=0,\quad\forall\delta>0.

By definitions in (
3.21
)–(
3.24
)
and the independence of
W^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(T\delta)
across
m\geq 1
,
when conditioned on the event

\hat{A}(T,\delta)
,
the conditional law of
\big(\sum_{m=1}^{T}W^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(T\delta)\big)_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
are independent across
j
,
and the conditional law of each
\sum_{m=1}^{T}W^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(T\delta)

is the same as

\mathbf{P}\big(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}\in\ \cdot\ \big|B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>T\delta\big).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sum_{m=1}^{T}W^{>,(m)}_{i;j}(\delta T)\geq nx_{j}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J}\ \Bigg|\ \hat{A}(T,\delta)\Bigg)=\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\frac{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>\frac{nx_{j}}{T\delta}\cdot T\delta)}{\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>T\delta)}.

By the uniform convergence theorem (with
\frac{nx_{j}}{T\delta}\in[cT/\delta,CT/\delta]
),
we conclude the proof of
Claim (
D.36
).
∎

Now, we are ready to provide the proofs of Lemmas
4.12
and
4.13
.

Proof of Lemma
4.12
.

Fix
M>0
and some
\bm{I}=(I_{k,j})_{k\geq 1,j\in[d]}\in\mathscr{I}
.
By Remark
2
,
there uniquely exists
j_{1}\in[d]
such that
I_{1,j_{1}}=1
.
Besides, by (
4.66
), it holds on the event
\{n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}(M,c)\}
that

n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)>M

for any
k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]
,
j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}
,
and

\displaystyle\frac{n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)}{n^{-1}\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j^{\prime}}(k^{\prime})}\in[c,1/c],\qquad\forall k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k},\ k^{\prime}\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],\ j^{\prime}\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k^{\prime}}.

On the other hand,
by the definition of

{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}}=\big(I^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)\big)_{k\geq 1,\ j\in[d]}
in (
4.22
),

\displaystyle\big\{n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}(M,c)\big\}=\big\{\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I},\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}(M,c)\big\}.

Therefore, analogous to the derivation of (
4.47
), we get
(henceforth in this proof, we write

\bm{t}(k-1)=(t_{j})_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}

and

\bm{x}(k)=(x_{j})_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I},\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in E^{\bm{I}}(M,c)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\mathbf{P}\Big(W^{>}_{i;j_{1}}(n\delta)>nM\Big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}p_{1}(n,M,\delta)}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{l}\geq nM\ \forall l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\\
t_{l}/t_{l^{\prime}}\in[c,1/c]\ \forall l,l^{\prime}\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(k-1);j}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k};\ W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(k-1);j}\geq c\cdot\max_{l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}t_{l}\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\Big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}p_{k}(n,M,\delta,\bm{t}(k-1))}.

First, due to (
4.1
),

\displaystyle p_{1}(n,M,\delta)=\mathbf{P}\Big(W^{>}_{i;j_{1}}(n\delta)>nM\ \Big|\ N^{>}_{i;j_{1}}(n\delta)\geq 1\Big)\cdot\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>}_{i;j_{1}}(n\delta)\geq 1\Big).

By part (i) of Lemma
4.7
,
there is some
\delta_{0}>0
such that for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{p_{1}(n,M,\delta)}{\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j_{1})}\mathbf{P}(B_{j_{1}\leftarrow l^{*}(j_{1})}>n\delta)\cdot(\delta/M)^{\alpha^{*}(j_{1})}}\leq 1.

Furthermore, due to
\mathbf{P}(B_{j_{1}\leftarrow l^{*}(j_{1})}>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha^{*}(j_{1})}(x)
(see Assumption
2
),
we have

\mathbf{P}(B_{j_{1}\leftarrow l^{*}(j_{1})}>n\delta)\cdot(\delta/M)^{\alpha^{*}(j_{1})}\sim\mathbf{P}(B_{j_{1}\leftarrow l^{*}(j_{1})}>n)\cdot(1/M)^{\alpha^{*}(j_{1})},

and hence

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{p_{1}(n,M,\delta)}{\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j_{1})}\mathbf{P}(B_{j_{1}\leftarrow l^{*}(j_{1})}>n)\cdot(1/M)^{\alpha^{*}(j_{1})}}\leq 1.

(D.47)

Next, for each
k=2,3,\ldots,\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}
,
Claim
(
D.31
) in Lemma
D.4
gives an upper bound for

\mathbf{P}\big(N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(k);j}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\big),

whereas Claim (
D.33
) in
Lemma
D.4
provides an upper bound for

\mathbf{P}\big(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(k);j}\geq nx\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\ \big|\ N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(k);j}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\big),

with
x=n^{-1}c\cdot\max_{l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}t_{l}
.
In particular, under the condition that

t_{l}/t_{l^{\prime}}\in[c,1/c]\ \forall l,l^{\prime}\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1},

we have

nx/t_{l}\in[1,1/c]

for each
l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}
,
and
(
D.33
)
provides a bound
that holds uniformly over
nx/t_{l}\in[1,1/c]
for each
l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}
.
Therefore, by picking a smaller
\delta_{0}>0
if necessary, it holds for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
that
(henceforth in this proof, we use
a_{n}\lessapprox b_{n}
to denote
\limsup_{n\to\infty}a_{n}/b_{n}\leq 1
)

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{l}\geq nM\ \forall l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\\
t_{l}/t_{l^{\prime}}\in[c,1/c]\ \forall l,l^{\prime}\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\end{subarray}}p_{k}\big(n,M,\delta,\bm{t}(k-1)\big)

\displaystyle\lessapprox\Bigg(\sup_{\bm{t}(k-1):\ t_{l}\geq nM\ \forall l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}C_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{t}(k-1)\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{l}\geq nM\ \forall l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\\
t_{l}/t_{l^{\prime}}\in[c,1/c]\ \forall l,l^{\prime}\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{\bm{}}j^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}\bigg(\frac{\delta t_{i}}{c\cdot\max_{l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}t_{l}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)

\displaystyle\lessapprox\Bigg(\sup_{\bm{t}(k-1):\ t_{l}\geq nM\ \forall l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}C_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{t}(k-1)\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\cdot\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{\bm{}}j^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}\bigg(\frac{\delta}{c}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta).

By
\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha^{*}(j)}(x)
,
we have

\delta^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\sim\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n).

Next,
since
C_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}\big((t_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\big)
defined in (
D.30
)
is monotone decreasing w.r.t. each
t_{i}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t_{l}\geq nM\ \forall l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}C_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\big(n^{-1}\bm{t}(k-1)\big)=\underbrace{\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{\bm{}}j^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}\frac{\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j)}}{M^{\alpha^{*}(j)-1}}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}C^{(M)}_{k}},

where
C^{(M)}_{k}
monotonically tends to
0
as
M\to\infty
.
In summary,
by setting the constant

\tilde{c}_{k}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{\{\mathcal{J}(i):\ i\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\}\in\mathbb{T}_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}}\prod_{i\in\mathcal{\bm{}}j^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}}\prod_{j\in\mathcal{J}(i)}c^{-\alpha^{*}(j)}\in(0,\infty),

it holds for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
that

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{l}\geq nM\ \forall l\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\\
t_{l}/t_{l^{\prime}}\in[c,1/c]\ \forall l,l^{\prime}\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k-1}\end{subarray}}p_{k}\big(n,M,\delta,\bm{t}(k-1),\bm{x}(k)\big)\lessapprox C^{(M)}_{k}\tilde{c}_{k}\cdot\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n),\ \ \forall k=2,3,\ldots,\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}.

(D.48)

Combining (
D.47
) and (
D.48
),
we obtain (for each
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{I}^{n|\delta}_{i}=\bm{I},\ n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i}\in B^{\bm{I}}(M,c)\big)}{\big(\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j_{1})}\cdot\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}C^{(M)}_{k}\tilde{c}_{k}\big)\cdot n^{-1}\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n)}\leq 1.

In particular, recall that
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}=\{j_{1}\}
,
so we have

n^{-1}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n)=\mathbf{P}(B_{j_{1}\leftarrow l^{*}(j_{1})}>n)

in the denominator of the display above.
By (
3.6
) and that

\lim_{M\to\infty}C^{(M)}_{k}=0,

we conclude the proof by setting

C^{\bm{I}}_{i}(M,c)=\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j_{1})}\cdot\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}C^{(M)}_{k}\tilde{c}_{k}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
4.13
.

It suffices to find
\delta_{0}>0
such that the following holds for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
:
given
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists
\rho=\rho(\epsilon)\in(1,\infty)
such that the inequalities

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\bigg(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i;j}\in A^{\bm{I}}(\bm{x},\bm{y})\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq(1+\epsilon)\cdot\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j^{\bm{I}}_{1})}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}\Bigg(\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}(x_{k,j},y_{k,j}]\Bigg),

(D.49)

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\bigg(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i;j}\in A^{\bm{I}}(\bm{x},\bm{y})\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\epsilon)\cdot\bar{s}_{i,l^{*}(j^{\bm{I}}_{1})}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}\Bigg(\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}(x_{k,j},y_{k,j}]\Bigg)

hold
under the condition that
c\leq x_{k,j}<y_{k,j}\leq C
and
y_{k,j}/x_{k,j}<\rho
for any
k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}
.
To see why, note that we can always partition
the set
\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}(x_{k,j},y_{k,j}]

in
(
4.71
) into a union of finitely many disjoint sets of the form

\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}(x^{\prime}_{k,j},y^{\prime}_{k,j}]
,
where we have

c\leq x^{\prime}_{k,j}<y^{\prime}_{k,j}\leq C
and
y^{\prime}_{k,j}/x^{\prime}_{k,j}<\rho
for each
k,j
.
Then, we obtain (
4.71
) by applying (
D.49
) onto each of the disjoint subset and sending
\epsilon
to
0
in the limit.

To prove (
D.49
), we make some observations regarding
\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}
.
Consider
\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}(x_{k,j},y_{k,j}]

with
0<x_{k,j}<y_{k,j}
for each
k,j
.
For clarity of the displays below, we write
\bm{w}_{k}=(w_{k,j})_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}
.
By the definition of
\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}
in (
4.68
) and the definition of
\nu^{\bm{I}}
in (
3.10
),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}\Bigg(\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}_{k}}(x_{k,j},y_{k,j}]\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\left[\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1}\underbrace{\int_{w_{k,j}\in(x_{k,j},y_{k,j}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}_{k}^{\bm{I}}}g_{\bm{j}_{k}^{\bm{I}}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{w}_{k})\Bigg(\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\nu_{\alpha^{*}(j)}(dw_{k,j})\Bigg)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\hat{c}_{k}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\underbrace{\prod_{j\in\bm{j}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}^{\bm{I}}}\int_{w_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j}\in(x_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j},y_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j}]}\nu_{\alpha^{*}(j)}(dw_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j})}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\hat{c}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}}.

By the definitions in (
3.7
) we get

\hat{c}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}=\prod_{j\in\bm{j}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}^{\bm{I}}}\big(\frac{1}{x_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j}}\big)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}-\big(\frac{1}{y_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j}}\big)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}.

Next, for each term
\hat{c}_{k}
with
k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1]
,
by the intermediate value theorem (in particular, due to the continuity of the mapping
g_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}
defined in (
3.9
)),
there exists some
\bm{z}_{k}=(z_{k,j})_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}

with

z_{k,j}\in[x_{k,j},y_{k,j}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}

such that

\displaystyle\hat{c}_{k}

\displaystyle=g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{z}_{k})\cdot\int_{w_{k,j}\in(x_{k,j},y_{k,j}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\Bigg(\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\nu_{\alpha^{*}(j)}(dw_{k,j})\Bigg)

\displaystyle=g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{z}_{k})\cdot\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\bigg(\frac{1}{x_{k,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}-\bigg(\frac{1}{y_{k,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}.

On the other hand,
due to
0<x_{k,j}\leq z_{k,j}\leq y_{k,j}
and the monotonicity of
g_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{x}_{k})}{g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{y}_{k})}\cdot\hat{c}_{k}\leq\frac{g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{x}_{k})}{g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{z}_{k})}\cdot\hat{c}_{k}=g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{x}_{k})\cdot\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\bigg(\frac{1}{x_{k,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}-\bigg(\frac{1}{y_{k,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}.

In addition, by the definitions in (
3.9
),

\displaystyle\frac{g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{x}_{k})}{g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{y}_{k})}

\displaystyle\geq\min_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\bigg(\frac{x_{k,j}}{y_{k,j}}\bigg)^{|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}|}.

In summary,

\displaystyle\Bigg(\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1]}g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{x}_{k})\Bigg)\cdot\Bigg[\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\bigg(\frac{1}{x_{k,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}-\bigg(\frac{1}{y_{k,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\Bigg]

(D.50)

\displaystyle\geq\Bigg[\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1]}\min_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\bigg(\frac{x_{k,j}}{y_{k,j}}\bigg)^{|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}|}\Bigg]\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\hat{c}_{k}=\Bigg[\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1]}\min_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\bigg(\frac{x_{k,j}}{y_{k,j}}\bigg)^{|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}|}\Bigg]\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}\Bigg(\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}(x_{k,j},y_{k,j}]\Bigg).

Similarly, one can obtain the upper bound

\displaystyle\Bigg(\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1]}g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{y}_{k})\Bigg)\cdot\Bigg[\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\bigg(\frac{1}{x_{k,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}-\bigg(\frac{1}{y_{k,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\Bigg]

(D.51)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg[\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1]}\max_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\bigg(\frac{y_{k,j}}{x_{k,j}}\bigg)^{|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}|}\Bigg]\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\bm{I}}\Bigg(\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\bigtimes_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}(x_{k,j},y_{k,j}]\Bigg).

To proceed,
for any non-empty
\mathcal{I}\subseteq[d]
and any
\mathcal{J}\subseteq[d]
, we define

\displaystyle p_{\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J}}\big(\delta,\bm{t}(\mathcal{I}),\bm{w}(\mathcal{J})\big)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\mathbf{P}\bigg(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}=w_{j}\ \forall j\in\mathcal{J},\ W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}=0\ \forall j\in[d]\setminus\mathcal{J}\bigg),

where we write

\bm{w}(\mathcal{J})=(w_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
,
and the

W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}
’s are defined in (
4.37
).
For
\mathcal{J}=\emptyset
, we set

p_{\mathcal{I},\emptyset}\big(\delta,\bm{t}(\mathcal{I})\big)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\mathbf{P}\big(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{t}(\mathcal{I});j}=0\ \forall j\in[d]\big).

By the Markov property in (
4.34
),
we get (recall that
j^{\bm{I}}_{1}
is the unique index
j\in[d]
such that
I_{j,1}=1
,
and that we write
\bm{w}_{k}=(w_{k,j})_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)=w_{k,j}\ \forall k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k};\ \tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)=0\ \forall k\geq 1,\ j\notin\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big(\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}(1)=w_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}};\ \tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(1)=0\ \forall j\neq j^{\bm{I}}_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k+1)=w_{k+1,j}\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1};\ \tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k+1)=0\ \forall j\notin\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \bigg|\ \tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)=w_{k,j}\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k};\ \tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)=0\ \forall j\notin\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\bigg)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{=}}\mathbf{P}\Big(W^{>}_{i;j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}(n\delta)=w_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}};\ W^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)=0\ \forall j\neq j^{\bm{I}}_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\mathbf{P}\bigg(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{w}_{k};j}=w_{k+1,j}\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1};\ W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{w}_{k};j}=0\ \forall j\notin\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big(W^{>}_{i;j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}(n\delta)=w_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}};\ W^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)=0\ \forall j\neq j^{\bm{I}}_{1}\Big)\cdot\Bigg[\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1}p_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k},\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}\big(\delta,\bm{w}_{k},\bm{w}_{k+1}\big)\Bigg]\cdot p_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}},\emptyset}\big(\delta,\bm{w}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\big).

Here, the step
(*)
in the display above follows from the definition of the
\tau^{n|\delta}_{i;j}(k)
’s in (
4.9
) and (
4.11
).
Then by (
4.72
),
we have
(in the displays below, we interpret
\sum_{w_{k,j}\in(a,b]}
as the summation over all the integers in
(a,b]
because
W^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)
will only take integer values by definition)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i;j}\in A^{\bm{I}}(\bm{x},\bm{y})\bigg)

(D.52)

\displaystyle=\sum_{w_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}\in(nx_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}},\ ny_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}]}\mathbf{P}\Big(W^{>}_{i;j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}(n\delta)=w_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}};\ W^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)=0\ \forall j\neq j^{\bm{I}}_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sum_{w_{2,j}\in(nx_{2,j},ny_{2,j}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{2}}p_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{1},\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{2}}\big(\delta,\bm{w}_{1},\bm{w}_{2}\big)\cdot\ldots\cdot\sum_{w_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j}\in(nx_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j},\ ny_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}}p_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1},\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}}\big(\delta,\bm{w}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1},\bm{w}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot p_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}},\emptyset}\big(\delta,\bm{w}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\big).

To characterize the asymptotics of (
D.52
),
we first note that

\displaystyle p_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}},\emptyset}\big(\delta,\bm{w}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{w}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}};j}=0\ \forall j\in[d]\Big)=\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{w}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}};j}=0\ \forall j\in[d]\Big)\qquad\text{due to \eqref{property, sum of W and N i M l j when positive, cluster size}}.

Recall that we have

c\leq x_{k,j}<y_{k,j}\leq C
for each
k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}],j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}
.
By Claim (
D.31
) of Lemma
D.4

under the choice of
\mathcal{J}=\emptyset

(in which case we have
C_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\emptyset}\big((t_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\big)\equiv 1
in (
D.30
)),
we can identify some
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(c)>0
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\min_{w_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j}\in(nx_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j},\ ny_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}},j}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}}\mathbf{P}\Big(N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{w}_{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}};j}=0\ \forall j\in[d]\Big)=1,\qquad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}).

(D.53)

Similarly, for each
k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1]
,

\displaystyle p_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k},\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}\big(\delta,\bm{w}_{k},\bm{w}_{k+1}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\bigg(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{w}_{k};j}=w_{k+1,j}\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}\ \bigg|\ N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{w}_{k};j}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{P}\bigg(N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{w}_{k};j}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}\bigg)\quad\text{by \eqref{property, sum of W and N i M l j when positive, cluster size}},

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow

\displaystyle\sum_{w_{k+1,j}\in(nx_{k+1,j},ny_{k+1,j}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}p_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k},\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}\big(\delta,\bm{w}_{k},\bm{w}_{k+1}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\bigg(W^{>|\delta}_{\bm{w}_{k};j}\in(n{x_{k+1,j}},n{y_{k+1,j}}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}\ \bigg|\ N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{w}_{k};j}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{P}\bigg(N^{>|\delta}_{\bm{w}_{k};j}\geq 1\text{ iff }j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}\bigg).

Besides,
under the condition that
w_{l,j}\in(nx_{l,j},ny_{l,j}]
and
c\leq x_{l,j}<y_{l,j}\leq C

for each
l,j
, we have

\frac{nx_{k+1,j}}{w_{k,j^{\prime}}}\in\bigg[\frac{c}{C},\frac{C}{c}\bigg],\ \ \frac{ny_{k+1,j}}{w_{k,j^{\prime}}}\in\bigg[\frac{c}{C},\frac{C}{c}\bigg],\qquad\forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1},\ j^{\prime}\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}.

This allows us to apply Claim (
D.31
) and (
D.32
) in Lemma
D.4
and obtain that (by picking a smaller
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(c,C)>0
if needed)
for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{w}_{k}:\ w_{k,j}\in(nx_{k,j},ny_{k,j}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\quad\Bigg|\frac{\sum_{w_{k+1,j}\in(nx_{k+1,j},ny_{k+1,j}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}p_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k},\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}\big(\delta,\bm{w}_{k},\bm{w}_{k+1}\big)}{g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(n^{-1}\bm{w}_{k})\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\cdot\big[\big(\frac{\delta}{x_{k+1,j}}\big)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}-\big(\frac{\delta}{y_{k+1,j}}\big)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\big]}-1\Bigg|=0.

We stress that the choice of
\delta_{0}
only depends on
c
and
C
,
due to
c\leq x_{l,j}<y_{l,j}\leq C
for each
l
and
j
.
Furthermore, due to

\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha^{*}(j)}(x)
(see Assumption
2
),
for each
\delta>0
we have (as
n\to\infty
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n\delta)\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{\delta}{x_{k+1,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}-\bigg(\frac{\delta}{y_{k+1,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\sim\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n)\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{1}{x_{k+1,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}-\bigg(\frac{1}{y_{k+1,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\Bigg].

Also, the monotonicity of
g_{\mathcal{I}\leftarrow\mathcal{J}}
implies

g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{x}_{k})\leq g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(n^{-1}\bm{w}_{k})\leq g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{y}_{k}),

provided that
w_{k,j}\in(nx_{k,j},ny_{k,j}]
for each
j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}
.
In summary, for each
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{k,j}\in(nx_{k,j},ny_{k,j}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\sum_{w_{k+1,j}\in(nx_{k+1,j},ny_{k+1,j}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}\frac{p_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k},\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}\big(\delta,\bm{w}_{k},\bm{w}_{k+1}\big)}{\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n)}

(D.54)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{y}_{k})\cdot\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{1}{x_{k+1,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}-\bigg(\frac{1}{y_{k+1,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\Bigg],

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{k,j}\in(nx_{k,j},ny_{k,j}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\sum_{w_{k+1,j}\in(nx_{k+1,j},ny_{k+1,j}]\ \forall j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}\frac{p_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k},\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}\big(\delta,\bm{w}_{k},\bm{w}_{k+1}\big)}{\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\geq g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{x}_{k})\cdot\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{1}{x_{k+1,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}-\bigg(\frac{1}{y_{k+1,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\Bigg].

Lastly, for the term

\displaystyle\sum_{w_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}\in(nx_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}},\ ny_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}]}\mathbf{P}\Big(W^{>}_{i;j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}(n\delta)=w_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}};\ W^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)=0\ \forall j\neq j^{\bm{I}}_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\bigg(W^{>}_{i;j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}(n\delta)\in\big(nx_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}},\ ny_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}\big];\ W^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)=0\ \forall j\neq j^{\bm{I}}_{1}\bigg)

in the display (
D.52
),
by part (i) of Lemma
4.7

(pick a smaller
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(c,C)>0
if needed),
it holds for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left|\rule{0.0pt}{25.6073pt}\frac{\sum_{w_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}\in(nx_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}},\ ny_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}]}\mathbf{P}\big(W^{>}_{i;j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}(n\delta)=w_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}};\ W^{>}_{i;j}(n\delta)=0\ \forall j\neq j^{\bm{I}}_{1}\big)}{\bar{s}_{1,l^{*}(j^{\bm{I}}_{1})}\cdot\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\bm{I}}_{1}\leftarrow l^{*}(j^{\bm{I}}_{1})}>n)\cdot\big[\big(\frac{1}{x_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}}\big)^{\alpha^{*}(j^{\bm{I}}_{1})}-\big(\frac{1}{y_{1,j^{\bm{I}}_{1}}}\big)^{\alpha^{*}(j^{\bm{I}}_{1})}\big]}-1\right|=0.

(D.55)

By (
3.6
) and our assumption of
\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}=\bm{j}
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\prod_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n)=\mathbf{P}(B_{j^{\bm{I}}_{1}\leftarrow l^{*}(j^{\bm{I}}_{1})}>n)\cdot\prod_{k=2}^{\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}}n\mathbf{P}(B_{j\leftarrow l^{*}(j)}>n).

Plugging (
D.53
), (
D.54
), (
D.55
) into (
D.52
), we obtain (for any
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\bigg(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i;j}\in A^{\bm{I}}(\bm{x},\bm{y})\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\bar{s}_{1,l^{*}(j^{\bm{I}}_{1})}\cdot\Bigg(\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1]}g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{y}_{k})\Bigg)\cdot\Bigg[\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\bigg(\frac{1}{x_{k,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}-\bigg(\frac{1}{y_{k,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\Bigg],

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{-1}\mathbf{P}\bigg(n^{-1}\bm{\tau}^{n|\delta}_{i;j}\in A^{\bm{I}}(\bm{x},\bm{y})\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\bar{s}_{1,l^{*}(j^{\bm{I}}_{1})}\cdot\Bigg(\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1]}g_{\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}\leftarrow\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}}(\bm{x}_{k})\Bigg)\cdot\Bigg[\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}]}\prod_{j\in\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k}}\bigg(\frac{1}{x_{k,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}-\bigg(\frac{1}{y_{k,j}}\bigg)^{\alpha^{*}(j)}\Bigg].

Lastly, to verify Claim (
D.49
) given
\epsilon>0
, we observe the following.
By the bounds in (
D.50
) and (
D.51
),
it suffices to pick
\rho>1
such that

\displaystyle\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1]}1/\rho^{|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}|}>1-\epsilon,\qquad\prod_{k\in[\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}-1]}\rho^{|\bm{j}^{\bm{I}}_{k+1}|}<1+\epsilon,

In case that
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}=1
, the display above holds trivially as the product degenerates to
1
.
In case that
\mathcal{K}^{\bm{I}}\geq 2
, the display above holds for any
\rho>1
close enough to 1.
∎
